# cua's own macOS native engine (#117; child 4 of #114)

## Purpose

On macOS, cua's computer surface (`cua.getApp(...)`, `getAXState`, `click`, `typeText` and the rest of the window API the model learns from `docs/cua-api/core.md`) is answered by OpenAI's native helper, `Codex Computer Use.app` from the pinned `@oai/sky` package: it reads the accessibility tree, numbers the elements, delivers input, captures the window, and holds the Accessibility and Screen Recording permissions the user granted. Because that helper accepts only the vendor's `node_repl` as a socket peer, macOS computer sessions are also the last sessions still served by the vendor's REPL host instead of cua's own (#116). After this work, cua's own engine, `cua Engine.app`, answers the same API behind the same contract, cua's own host serves every session on every platform, and `cua install` on macOS stops downloading the vendor runtime archive altogether.

Three things become observable that are not today:

- **Nothing of the vendor runs.** `ps` during a session shows `cua-engine`, cua's Node and no `SkyComputerUseService`, `node_repl` or vendor Node started by cua. `cua doctor` shows cua's engine, its build and signature, and the permissions granted to it.
- **A driven app stays where it is.** Clicking a button in a background app does not bring that app's window to the front or take the keyboard focus from the window the user is working in, and the user typing in their own window does not stop the agent. Today the vendor's click path activates the app, which the owner experiences as windows jumping forward (#162's investigation).
- **The same speed or better, measured.** Every operation of the native baseline (`docs/evidence/2026-10-14-native-baseline/`) is answered within the no-regression rule, and the three points the owner named (speed, finding elements, not interfering) are recorded against the vendor's engine on the same machine before the default changes.

How to see it: on the mini, with the plugin at the release this work ships, run a session that binds TextEdit, keep a Finder window in front, and ask for a click in the TextEdit document. The Finder window stays in front and keeps the keyboard, the click lands, and `cua doctor` names `cua Engine.app` as the engine. The vendor engine stays selectable with `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` until M6 retires it.

## Scope, inheritance and evidence

Child 4, board #117, of the independence composite #114 (recorded on the board; no checked-in parent spec). The parent contract is full independence from OpenAI with no capability or performance regression and an unchanged model-facing API. Siblings: #113 own browser service (landed), #116 own REPL host (landed; `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-14-own-repl-host-design.md`), #115 own sandbox (landed; `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-17-own-sandbox-design.md`), #120 clean-room rewrite (deferred by the owner, 2026-10-17), #171 Linux engine (deferred, opens after this).

What is in scope: the macOS engine, its JS service, the switch of macOS computer sessions to cua's own host, the engine selection and attestation, the verification system, install and signing, the retirement of the vendor runtime on macOS including cua's own module tree for cells, and approvals on both platforms through one policy module. What is not: the Linux engine (`sky_linux` stays pinned and served by cua's own host until #171), the Windows target (cua drops it), audio recording (dormant in cua), the vendor's Skysight, Messages, Calendar, picture-in-picture, lock-screen auto-unlock, analytics and app-server features, a cursor overlay, and per-app instruction texts (the vendor ships its own; cua writes none in this work).

Terms used below:

- **Engine**: the native process that touches the Accessibility API, input and capture. The vendor's is `SkyComputerUseService` inside `Codex Computer Use.app`; cua's is `cua-engine` inside `cua Engine.app`.
- **Service**: the JavaScript that runs in the trusted worker of the REPL host and talks to the engine. The vendor's is `@oai/sky`'s `service.js` and `targets/mac/*`; cua's is `src/services/engine/`. cua's existing trusted wrapper `src/services/sky.mjs` (secret substitution) sits in front of either.
- **Facade**: the model-facing API in `src/cua-api/` (a port of `@oai/cua`'s `tinysky_alt`), which calls `nodeRepl.rpc("sky", {type: "setup" | "execute", ...})`. Unchanged by this work.
- **Skyshot**: the vendor's name for one observation of an app: its key window's accessibility text, optionally a screenshot. Kept as the name of the engine's observation result.
- **Scene**: one harness-owned window in one app family, used by the verification system (defined under "Verification system").
- **Lineage**: the engine's per-connection, per-app numbering of elements, in which an index keeps meaning across observations until the lineage ends.
- **Approved engine**: the one on-disk copy of `cua Engine.app` per Mac whose grants the owner confirmed, which every session and harness on that Mac serves in place (defined under "Install, signing and permissions").

Research inputs, cited as `R/<file>` in `docs/doperpowers/research/2026-10-18-own-native-engine/`:

| File | What it settles |
|---|---|
| `A-contract.md` | The full JS-side contract: the `setup`/`execute` RPC, the 11 macOS methods and their shapes, error codes and sentences, the socket framing and version, per-app approval flow and where its memory lives, the accessibility text grammar as reconstructed, diffing, the harnesses' dependencies, what a drop-in must reproduce |
| `B-helper.md` | The helper bundle: executables, plists, signatures, entitlements (partial) |
| `E-helper-catalogue.md` | Tier-1 static reading of the pinned helper: the wire schema from Swift reflection, the AX attribute and action surface, input and capture vocabulary, grammar literals (including the short string immediates `strings` misses), policy lists, the intervention state machine, limits, the functions that need disassembly (§9) and what statics cannot give (§10) |
| `F-helper-logic.md` | Tier-2 findings from targeted disassembly, including the recovered names of the soft-linked private functions: tree walk and batching, naming and pruning, index assignment and re-resolution, diffing, the click path and its activation, typeText, paste, settle, intervention, timing values (M0) |
| `sky-behaviour.md` | The consolidated behaviour specification the engine and renderer are built against, every rule tagged with its source (M0) |
| `docs/evidence/2026-10-14-native-baseline/` | The vendor engine's measured cost on the mini, TextEdit, three attested runs (PR #133) |

`C-macos-prior-art.md`, `C-linux.md` and `D-cua-driver.md` stay local: by the owner's ruling the engine takes no code from the projects they survey, and their platform facts are folded into the design below where used. Decompiled vendor code (M0 tier 2) stays local too; `F` cites functions by name and address and quotes at most short fragments.

The pinned helper to read is the one cua installs, `~/Library/Application Support/cua/runtimes/26.928.40906-darwin-arm64/cua_node/lib/node_modules/@oai/sky/Codex Computer Use.app` (bundle 26.924.1001281), whose main executable keeps its exported, type and reflection symbols (161,617 symbols; 3,653 Swift types decode with their field names) while 72% of its function symbols are `<redacted function N>` placeholders (93,856 of 130,919, `R/E-helper-catalogue.md`). The helper inside the ChatGPT app installed under `/Applications` (26.1013) is stripped further and is not read. Three facts about the pinned binary shape M0: it imports no `CGEventPost*`, `CGEventCreateKeyboardEvent` or `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` and carries no SkyLight names, because even its public CGEvent calls go through a `SoftLink` module that resolves symbols by salted SipHash (28 salts of 125–128 hex characters sit in the binary), so the functions it calls are recoverable only by hashing candidate names against its table; Swift string literals of 15 bytes or fewer live as instruction immediates that `strings` never sees, which is where most grammar words, short AX names and short bundle ids were found; and the symbol names that survive show the mechanisms, not the code (Surprises & Discoveries).

## Design

### The contract kept, and what changes behind it

The facade's contract with the service does not change: `setup` returns `{target: "mac", methods: [the 11 names]}` and `execute {method, args}` returns what each method returns today (`R/A-contract.md` §1.2–1.4). The trusted wrapper `src/services/sky.mjs` keeps its pinned eligible shapes for secret substitution and its value-free error classification; the vendor module it delegates to is replaced by cua's service, selected by the launcher through the one slot it already reads (`CUA_SKY_SERVICE`, M1a's rename of `CUA_SKY_VENDOR_SERVICE`; `skyServiceFromEnv` and the `sky_not_configured` sentence name it), and cua's service raises errors of the same class names and `errorName` values (`SkyComputerUseError`, `SkyComputerUseTransportError`) so the wrapper's classification needs no change. Error sentences the model sees are kept where cua's harnesses, acceptance scripts or the first-call document depend on them (`noWindowsAvailable`, `timeoutReached`, the `userIntervened` and `screenLocked` sentences, `The element ID is no longer valid…`, `<n> is an invalid element ID`, the select and set-value refusals, `Computer Use was not approved to use <app>`, the forbidden-app sentence); elsewhere they are cua's own and bounded.

What changes behind it: the engine process, the socket it listens on, who may connect, where approval memory lives, and which REPL host runs the session.

### The engine: `cua Engine.app`

**One Swift program, Apple frameworks only.** `native/engine/` is a Swift 6 package (`swift build -c release --scratch-path <outside the package>`) producing the executable `cua-engine`, assembled into `cua Engine.app` (`LSUIElement`, hardened runtime, bundle id `io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine`, minimum macOS 14). It links ApplicationServices (the Accessibility API), CoreGraphics (events, window lists), ScreenCaptureKit (capture), AppKit (`NSWorkspace`, `NSRunningApplication`, `NSPasteboard`), Carbon's Text Input Sources (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardInputSource`) and Foundation. No package dependencies: the owner's ruling bars unvetted third-party code, and Swift against Apple's frameworks needs none, where Rust would need binding crates. Apple SPI (private functions of SkyLight or CoreGraphics), where "Input" below needs it, is resolved with `dlsym` at run time and feature-detected, never a dependency. `engineVersion` is the package's version and `build` is the hash of the `native/engine/` source tree plus the assembling script, so two bundles with the same `build` are the same program. The executable also has a `--probe` mode for the harnesses (Interfaces, `UserProbe`).

**Process model: one engine per Mac, launched on demand, resident.** The service launches the approved engine through the own host's existing `nodeRepl.launchServices.openApplication({applicationPath})` relay (`src/repl-host/launch-services.mjs`), which M1a changes to run `/usr/bin/open -n -g -a <path>` (`-g`: launch in the background, so the engine's own launch never takes the user's focus; `-n`: a new instance even when another copy of the same bundle id is running, because LaunchServices otherwise reuses the running instance and launches nothing; M0 probes both). Before it unlinks or binds the socket, an engine takes `flock` on `engine.lock` beside it; the loser of that lock exits at once, so two services launching in the same instant (two checkouts whose connects failed together) never leave an engine listening on a replaced socket inode, and `-n` never produces a duplicate. On the vendor host (comparison A's lane) the vendor relay runs plain `open -a`, which does not activate the engine (an `LSUIElement` app: M0 probe (c) on the mini, three of three, no activation and the frontmost application unchanged), so that lane needs no pre-launch; probe (c) also showed two copies of the bundle at two paths each coming up under `open -n -g -a` and binding their own socket (three of three). LaunchServices is then the engine's parent and TCC attributes the Accessibility and Screen Recording grants to the bundle, not to Node (the vendor's arrangement; Apple's guidance on responsible processes in `C-macos-prior-art.md` §6). The engine stays resident across sessions, as the vendor's does, and exits on a `shutdown` request or when the login session ends. `shutdown` is sent by `buildEngine` before it moves a new build into the approved location and by `cua engine stop`; the engine refuses it while another connection is open (`{error: "busy", connections: n}`), and `buildEngine` then fails visibly with the approved bundle and the running engine untouched, rather than leaving a stale process serving a new bundle's socket. **A service never shuts down an engine it did not launch**: on a version or build mismatch between the `ping` answer and the bundle it was given, the service fails the connection with a diagnostic naming `cua install --engine`. The reason is the mini, where several checkouts and harness homes meet one Mac: a service that "fixed" a mismatch by shutting another's engine down would restart that session's lineages mid-task. The alternative process model, one engine per connection as a child of the trusted worker, would give peer authentication for free but makes TCC attribution depend on the process tree and re-prompts after rebuilds and relaunch heuristics; rejected.

**Socket and peer check.** The engine listens on `engine.sock` beside its own bundle: the engine derives the path from `Bundle.main.bundlePath`, the service from `CUA_ENGINE_APP`, so no argument crosses the LaunchServices launch (the own host's relay runs `open` with `PATH` only, the vendor relay passes `node_repl`'s environment, and the design relies on neither; an already-running instance gets no new arguments). The directory is created with mode 0700. `CUA_ENGINE_SOCKET` overrides the path only when a harness runs the engine executable directly rather than through the relay. On accept the engine reads the peer's uid with `getpeereid(2)` and closes any connection from another uid; the kernel's seatbelt profile denies the engine directory's sockets to cells (the unix-socket deny pattern that already exists in `src/sandbox/seatbelt.mjs`), so under the scoped sandbox only the trusted worker reaches the engine; and the engine itself refuses the forbidden list (system security processes and password managers, "The service" below) on `resolve_app` and every app-scoped request, whoever the client is. That is the whole peer policy. The threat model is the owner's own account, where an unsigned Node cannot carry a code-signing requirement anyway (`C-macos-prior-art.md` §6, "Authenticating a local Node client"); the vendor's ancestry check is what forced the vendor host on cua and is not reproduced. What remains exposed: any process of the owner's uid outside the sandbox, including a cell under `CUA_SHIM_SANDBOX=disabled` or the agent's own shell, can connect and observe or drive any non-forbidden app without an approval. That exposure is recorded in the Decision Log and was accepted by the owner on 2026-10-18.

**Protocol: the facade's vocabulary, one layer down.** JSON-RPC 2.0, each frame a 4-byte little-endian length then UTF-8 JSON, 8 MiB maximum each way, one request in flight per connection, as the vendor's transport (`R/A-contract.md` §2.2). Methods: `ping {clientApiVersion}` → `{serverApiVersion, engineVersion, build}` with `serverApiVersion = "cua-engine-1"`; `execute {method, args, deadlineUnixMilliseconds, lineage?}` → the method's result or a JSON-RPC error `{code, message}` using the vendor's code numbers for the conditions cua maps (`R/A-contract.md` §1.5) and `-10005` with a bounded sentence otherwise (the engine checks `deadlineUnixMilliseconds` on receipt, −32001 when already past, as the vendor does, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §8.9, and, cua's addition, cancels a settle or a launch wait still running when the deadline passes, failing `timeoutReached`); `status` → the engine's permissions and identity; `approve` → the grant prompts and the one discarded capture of the approve step; `shutdown`. `execute.method` is one of the facade's 11 snake_case methods with the facade's argument shapes (`R/A-contract.md` §1.4, input column) plus two engine-only methods, `resolve_app {app | pid}` and `get_app_state` returning a `Skyshot` tree instead of text. So the service translates one thing, the facade's `element_index` into the lineage's element token ("Indexes, tokens and handles"), and otherwise adds policy, approval, rendering and diffing around a request it forwards. The vendor's own IPC vocabulary (`ComputerUseIPC*Request`, the `{_0: …}` enum encodings) is not adopted: the earlier reason for it, one JS client for both engines, was false, since cua's client can never reach the vendor helper (its peer check), and the vendor client would have to be impersonated to reach cua's (Decision Log entry 2).

**Observation (skyshot).** `get_app_state {app, disableDiff?, includeScreenshot?, outputDirectory}` observes the app's primary window (`AXFocusedWindow`, else `AXMainWindow`, else the last of `AXWindows`, the vendor's three-step fallback, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §1.7; none → error `noWindowsAvailable`). Before reading, the engine opens the app as the vendor's preparation does (`0x10014c494`, its open helper `0x1001648c4`): a launch when it is not running, and a **non-activating open (a reopen) when it runs with no primary window**, since some apps (TextEdit, Finder) answer a reopen by opening a window; either is followed by a wait for the primary window, 5 s polled every 50 ms (`0x1006fbdf0`), `timeoutReached` when it runs out. The reopen is visible on the owner's screen (TextEdit shows its Open panel), so it is named here; it never activates the app. *(Added 2026-10-21 by M1b.5's comparison A, Decision Log "M1b.5 sitting".)* The engine walks that window's accessibility tree and emits it depth-first in preorder (the order the text prints; which elements a walk the budgets cut keeps, and in what order it reads them, is #301's, below and in the Decision Log), plus the menu bar's top-level items as the vendor adds them, fetching the vendor's 20-attribute set (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §1.2: role, subrole, role description, title, description, value, value description, placeholder, number of characters, help, identifier, selected, focused, enabled, disclosing, URL, traits, serves-as-title, row and column index ranges) plus position and size, through the public `AXUIElementCopyMultipleAttributeValues` per element, then `AXUIElementCopyActionNames` per element. The private batched `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` the vendor prefetches with (recovered by name with its four option keys in `R/F-helper-logic.md` §11 and §1.1) was priced against the public route in M0 and lost on every scene, so the engine does not resolve it: in-cell medians, public against hierarchy, TextEdit 119.7 / 135.1 ms, Finder-200 267.7 / 340.7, Chrome 114.9 / 157.7, Electron 75.3 / 81.4 (the mini's first series, 90 samples each, alternated per sample; `spike/summary.md`), and the same direction on the MacBook. The engine reads the full attribute set for every element: M0's per-role reduction rendered the same text as the full set on TextEdit, Finder and Electron and one line differently on Chrome, and saved nothing (−5 to −10 ms in-cell, 15 samples per scene), so it is withdrawn and the full set is the rule. The owning process (`AXUIElementGetPid`, the vendor's `_AXUIElementGetActualPid` at input time, §5.7) is read for the elements an action targets, because sheets, open and save panels and some menus are served by another process (TextEdit's Open panel is; the baseline's `setup.newDocument` clicks its button) and input must be posted to the element's owner, not the app. The messaging timeout is set to 1 s on every element it touches (timeouts set on the application element do not reach its descendants), and a whole observation has its own 10 s traversal deadline apart from the request's 120 s: a hung app yields a truncated tree with the marker, not a stall (both cua's additions; the vendor has neither, §1.6). Enablement follows the vendor (§1.9), confined to web-shaped apps (the Settle paragraph's detection) and the bundles the vendor names (`com.apple.iWork*`, `com.spotify.client`), so an AppKit app such as Finder is never left with `AXEnhancedUserInterface` set, which changes its window behaviour for as long as it stays set (cua's departure; the matrix carries one iWork app and Spotify as the parity check): an application element that lists `AXManualAccessibility` gets it set (Electron, Chromium), else one that lists `AXEnhancedUserInterface` gets that, never both. Before the enablement, every observation of an eligible app reads the application element's role (AXRole, AXSubrole and AXRoleDescription in one request, never cached, timed as `appRoleRead`), which gives Chromium and Electron their Basic accessibility mode, and the page's tree, at once, as the vendor's eager construction of the application element does (§1.3); Basic mode lasts the app's lifetime, a declared difference nothing can release (Decision Log, #244). The attribute is read and set when it does not read true (the vendor's rule, §1.9); once the engine has set and owns it, later observations of that process neither read nor write it until the release writes it back, as the vendor's per-(app, kind) assertion writes once, since Electron's getter keeps reading false and every write restarts its 2 s countdown to complete mode (Decision Log, #244, 2026-11-01, reversing the critique's withdrawal of the once-per-process memory; a process that resets the attribute itself keeps a missing tree until the next release); a value that read true or could not be read is not owned and is read again at every observation. The engine remembers that it set the attribute, which it does only when it read false, and **releases it**: when the engine's last connection that observed that pid closes, it sets the attribute back to false; an attribute that read true before cua touched it (VoiceOver or another assistive client) is never written. After a write-back, the next observation of that process sets the attribute again, owned, whatever it reads, since Chromium's reads true after the write-back while its accessibility mode is gone (Decision Log 2026-10-22). The release is cua's declared difference, since the vendor never unsets (§1.9), and it exists because a driven web app left in full-accessibility mode after cua is done is interference the purpose section forbids. The first observation after enabling, or of any Chromium window, waits 100 ms and then up to 1 s for an `AXWebArea` to appear (without it a browser page is a single group); once a web area has been seen in a process, later observations of that process skip the wait (cua's departure: the vendor waits on every Chromium observation, which the MacBook shakedown priced at about 145 ms per observation, and a process whose web content is already exposed gains nothing from it; a window whose web area has gone, a new tab or a navigation, is caught by the settle's busy veto on an unloaded `AXWebArea`). The vendor's global VoiceOver preference is flipped only for Excel, Music, Atlas, Arc and Dia; cua never touches it, since it is visible to every app on the machine, so those five may expose less under cua until the owner decides otherwise at M5's sitting. Caps: the vendor's width rule is kept (100 children per element, then the visible subset, children omitted above 499, rendered as `(showing … of N items)`, §1.5), depth is the vendor's 200 levels (it drops the subtree silently; cua marks it), and cua adds what the vendor lacks, each with a marker in the text: a node budget of 4,000, spent on what the window shows first (#301, the design session's rulings aed3f041 and 66f90453: frames order the walk on every app, Chromium included), after which the remaining subtrees are omitted and counted in `truncated` with which way they lie from the visible area, 10,000 characters per value, cut with a `[truncated]` suffix, and a total budget of 6 MiB for the serialized `Skyshot`, enforced during the walk so that a text-heavy window (a long article, a log view) yields a truncated tree rather than the relay's frame-too-large error (the vendor's hierarchy call truncates strings at a length the SPI chooses; its 4,000 is the URL-character budget per tree, which the renderer keeps, §2.5). The method matrix includes one scene state large enough to hit the budget. `valueMarkdown`, the Markdown rendering of an attributed value (headings, emphasis; the vendor renders them so `selectText` can quote them), is fetched only for text elements whose plain value is at most 2,000 characters; M0 priced the fetch at 0.4–3.1 ms of engine time per observation on the four scenes (the in-cell difference with it off was within noise), so the cap is a bound, not a saving. The result is a JSON tree (`Skyshot` in Interfaces), not text: **rendering to the model's text happens in the service.** The split is the point of the design: element handles (`AXUIElementRef`) cannot leave the process, so handle identity and re-resolution must live in the engine, while numbering, the grammar, pruning and diffing are pure functions that the service can run against recorded trees and the vendor's recorded text, which is how the grammar is pinned (goldens, under "Verification system"). Linux already renders client-side (`src/cua-api/linux-accessibility-tree.mjs`), so both platforms end up with the renderer where it can be tested. **The split, as the critique settled it (2026-10-19, Decision Log):** the engine emits a stable per-lineage handle token for every node (equal exactly when two observations' handles are `CFEqual` at a matched position), and the service does the transformations, the numbering, the grammar and the diff as pure, golden-tested code, so the model sees the vendor's contiguous post-transform numbering (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §23.1) over nodes the engine numbered only by token; the handles never leave the engine, whose token-to-handle table is the lineage ("Indexes, tokens and handles").

**Indexes, tokens and handles.** Per (connection, app) the engine keeps a lineage: an id, the previous revision's tree with its handles, and a table from token to element handle plus the eleven attributes the vendor matches on (role, subrole, role description, title, value, value description, description, placeholder, help, identifier, URL; `R/F-helper-logic.md` §3.6) and the owning pid. A token is stable across observations exactly when the element's handle is `CFEqual` to the previous revision's at a matched position (sibling list against sibling list under matched parents, the vendor's rule, §3.2); a new element gets a new token; a walk a budget cut retires nothing it left unread (each unread child of a read element has its previous entry carried over with its subtree, in its place among its siblings, #301); a token absent from one revision is retired at that revision and never reissued in the lineage (N = 1: under the vendor's rule an element absent for one observation gets a new identity when it returns, so a longer grace would only keep handles nothing can name). The service numbers the tokens that survive its transformations, 0-based in preorder as the vendor numbers its rendered nodes (§3.1), keeps the index-to-token map per lineage, gives a new element the next unused index, and retires a vanished element's index **never to reuse it in that lineage**, where the vendor reuses indexes above the surviving maximum (§3.3), a defect cua does not copy because a stale index would then resolve silently to another element. An action names its lineage and a token: the engine refuses a token from another lineage, or a retired one, with `The element ID is no longer valid. Try to get the on-screen content again…` (the scoping by the `lineage` every `execute` carries is the engine's guard, chosen over a service-only check because the protocol already carries the field and a stale token across a reconnect is the engine's to refuse; the service's own check that the index belongs to the app's current lineage runs first and answers the same sentence; so a reconnect or a relaunched engine can never resolve a stale index to a different element, cua's addition, the vendor having no lineage check on actions). Resolving a token, the engine re-fetches only after a `layoutChanged` or `uiElementDestroyed` notification since the observation (the vendor's invalidation monitor, §3.5), else validates the handle with one `AXRole` read; a re-fetch walks the current tree for the one element matching the stored attributes, answering the "no longer valid" sentence when none matches and the vendor's before/after ambiguity sentences when several do. Every `Skyshot` carries its `lineage` id and its nodes' tokens; `disableDiff` starts a new lineage, which the service renumbers from 0 with the full tree, as the vendor does (§3.4). The sentences and their trigger order are the vendor's (`R/A-contract.md` §3.3), which the first-call document and the model's habits rely on.

**Screenshot.** When asked, a JPEG of the key window through ScreenCaptureKit (`SCContentFilter(desktopIndependentWindow:)`, `SCScreenshotManager.captureImage`; the shareable content cached per engine after a first call the MacBook priced at 156 ms cold), captured on its own thread as soon as the window's frame is known and joined after the walk, so an observation with a screenshot costs the longer of the walk and the capture rather than their sum (iteration 1 of the mini sitting: the engine's TextEdit total fell from 113.3 ms, walk 55.9 then capture 49.7 in sequence, to 48.7, walk 43.0 beside capture 41.0 with a 2.6 ms join; Decision Log). The image is at the display's pixel size: the window's frame times the pixels per point of the display under the frame's centre (`CGGetDisplaysWithPoint`, then the display mode's `pixelWidth` over its `width`), which on the mini's 2× display gave 1220 × 1720 for the TextEdit scene window, the vendor's size for the same window (at quality 0.8 the median file was 142,085 bytes against the vendor's 141,865; the vendor's Finder and Electron windows came out 1840 × 872 and 1440 × 1120 in the first series, twice the point-size images cua returned then, and the equal-size comparison was re-measured on TextEdit only), written with mode 0600 into `outputDirectory` (the session's run directory, which the kernel's sandbox lets the cell read) and returned as a `file:` URL, as the vendor returns it (`R/A-contract.md` §3.7); the `Skyshot` carries the image's width, height and scale. The pixel-size capture costs the engine 42.7 ms of capture and 7.2 of encode on the mini (41.0 and 2.1 at points), and iteration 2's in-cell `getScreenshot` median was 45.8 ms against the vendor's 53.4 with equal images. The vendor does not capture through ScreenCaptureKit: its `SkyshotOperation.capture` calls a private window-server function, `WindowServerSPI.captureWindowImagesInRect`, with an `imageSize` parameter (read from the decompiled capture path; Decision Log, iteration 2). cua keeps the public API, which met the vendor's size and beat its time; the private call is a later speed lever, not taken. The vendor's sizing rule, read from its capture path (U1, resolved 2026-10-19; Decision Log): the window's WindowServer bounds in points times the backing scale factor of the screen the window sits on (the best-matching `NSScreen`, 2.0 when none matches), each side rounded up, with no cap on size or pixel count. A downsizing fit (long side at most 2048, then short side at most 768) exists only behind `should_use_legacy_screenshot_sizing` in the server-side Statsig config `406601636`, which both Macs' cached copies leave unset (false) on 2026-10-19. The image covers the window's bounds, which the vendor also stores as the observation's `visibleRect`, not a clipped visible part. All four measured vendor sizes fit this rule, including the 2026-10-14 baseline's 1172 × 976, which was taken on the mini (a 586 × 488-point window), not on the MacBook. cua's capture differs in two ways that never show at 2× with integral frames: it takes the display mode's pixels per point rather than the screen's backing scale factor, and it rounds each side to nearest rather than up. M1a aligns both before its renderer goldens are recorded and re-reads the config then, since a golden recorded at the wrong size would be regenerated in another owner sitting. Coordinates the model sends (`click {x, y}`, a drag's points, a scroll at a point) are **in the pixel space of the screenshot that the connection's last screenshot-bearing observation of that app returned**: the engine keeps that observation's per-axis scale and the window it captured on the connection's lineage state (an observation without a screenshot keeps the stored one; `disableDiff` clears it), and at the one place a coordinate becomes a window point divides it by that scale and offsets it by the window's **current** origin. It refuses before anything is posted, as `noScreenshotForCoordinates` (Interfaces), when the connection holds no such observation, when the request names a lineage that is not current, or when the app's primary window is no longer the one in the screenshot (`CFEqual`), since the coordinates would then be placed in an image the model did not see; the refusal's sentence names `getScreenshot` as the way to get an image to click in (c13759a4's sentence names the tree and is revised in M1a), the wrapper's value-free classification admits the name, and the first-call document (M4) tells the model to observe after an action before clicking by coordinates, since a sheet or a new window replaces the primary window and the click is refused. This is a declared difference from the vendor, which applies the observation's scaling factor and the *observed* `visibleRect` origin (`screen = p × scalingFactor + visibleRect.origin`, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.2): a window that moved since the observation receives the vendor's click relative to a stale rect, where cua follows the moved window, and a replaced window receives it too, where cua refuses (the dispatching session's ruling, 2026-10-19, after the sitting review; Decision Log). The sitting's build divided by the display's scale at click time, which the code review found and the fix wave corrected (c13759a4, merged in 1be26dd1). Measured live in the second sitting (U2, `spike/sit2/u2`, the mini's 2× display, scale 2 on both axes): two coordinate clicks aimed at lines 0 and 8 of the TextEdit scene's document landed on exactly those lines (line error 0, the caret read back), a click after the window had been moved by (60, 40) points landed in the moved window on the aimed line, a click with no screenshot on the connection was refused as `noScreenshotForCoordinates` with nothing posted, and a click after the document window had been replaced by a new one was refused the same way. The second sitting's four-scene speed triple (U9, build b99ea967 against a same-sitting vendor triple, 90 samples per row; `spike/sit2/u9/summary.md`) gives the per-scene starting point for M2b's speed cells: an observation with a screenshot, vendor against cua, TextEdit 53.2 / 64.4 ms, Finder-200 441.7 / 162.3, Chrome 103.2 / 67.5, Electron 54.8 / 69.5; `getScreenshot` 54.7 / 44.2, 446.8 / 141.7, 104.3 / 46.0 and 55.2 / 56.9; the promote rows on TextEdit 52.4 / 49.9 (`getAXState`) and 54.7 / 44.2. The engine's capture, 55–58 ms on every scene, is the floor of an observation with a screenshot, so the two small trees (TextEdit's walk 48.6 ms, Electron's 22.3) finish behind the vendor's whole observation while Finder and Chrome finish far ahead; the private window-server capture is the later lever for those two scenes, not taken in this work unless M2b's beat requires it. `CGWindowListCreateImage` is deprecated since macOS 15 and is not a fallback. Screen Recording's periodic re-confirmation on macOS 15 and later applies to any app without Apple's capture entitlement; the vendor's helper has none either, so this is parity, not a regression. What is not parity-neutral is the first capture of each new on-disk copy, which raises a confirmation only the owner can answer (the sibling spec's evidence; "Install, signing and permissions" below handles it with the approved engine).

**App resolution and launch.** `resolve_app {app}` and every app-scoped method accept, in the one `app` string the facade binds and sends on every later call, a bundle id, a display name or a path. A bundle id, name or path that matches more than one running instance is `ambiguousApp` with the vendor's sentence, never a silent pick; that is why the harnesses' scene processes carry bundle ids and paths no owner process uses (part 2 of the verification system), so a path names exactly one process on either engine. Running regular apps match first; otherwise LaunchServices (`NSWorkspace.urlForApplication(withBundleIdentifier:)`, and for a name the `.app` of that name or localized name in `/Applications`, `/System/Applications`, `~/Applications` and `/System/Library/CoreServices`), with `ambiguousApp` when more than one matches and `invalidApp` when none. An app that is not running is launched without activation (`NSWorkspace.openApplication(at:configuration:)` with `activates = false`) and the observation waits up to 5 s for a window, then fails `timeoutReached` as the vendor does; the baseline's `bind.getApp.retry` row exists for exactly that path. `list_apps` returns every running regular app plus the apps this engine has resolved before, from its own usage store (`<approved engine directory>/usage.json`: bundle id, last used, use count), so the vendor's `lastUsedDate` and `useCount` fields have cua's meaning; the bound identity returned to the facade is the app's path, as the vendor binds (`R/A-contract.md` §7).

### Input

Every input request names the app and either an element index or window coordinates (coordinates in the pixel space of the lineage's last screenshot, "Screenshot" above). The rule that produces "not interfering": **the engine never activates an app on its own** (an observation's non-activating reopen of a windowless app, "Observation (skyshot)", is not an activation), and input is posted to the process that owns the target element (`AXUIElementGetPid`), which for a sheet or an open panel is not the app; the engine checks that owning pid against the forbidden list before posting anything, so a panel served by a forbidden process (a password manager's autofill) is refused with the forbidden-app sentence even when the bound app is allowed. **M2a's route table, measured in the second sitting (2026-10-19, 19:17–20:42Z; `spike/sit2/u12u13`, three samples per cell with an outcome read-back and the activation watcher, all four scenes, each driven with another scene's window in front; Decision Log, "second input sitting"):** typed text and plain key presses (`type_text`, `press_key` without ⌘) go by **route 2′**, the plain post with no fake activation, which delivered on all four scenes; **a ⌘ chord in `press_key` (a menu key equivalent) and an element's own action or attribute write (a click carried by `AXPress` or `AXPick`, `set_value`, `select_text`, `perform_secondary_action`) go by route 3 when the app does not believe it has focus**, the activation held across the interaction (amended 2026-10-22 from M2b's matrix, Decision Log "route 3 for key equivalents and element actions"; U12 measured typed text, never these); a synthesized click (an element without an advertised action, or a coordinate click) goes by **route 3**, the fake activation, the only click route that delivered on all four; the ladder for clicks is **1 → 3 → 4**, route 4 only through the per-app override; **route 2, the ⌘ post, leaves the defaults**: when route 3's guard fails the click is refused as `activationFailed` (Interfaces), never retried with ⌘, because ⌘ changes what a click means. The routes, with what the two sittings measured for each on the mini (the first: `spike/iter1-input`, `spike/iter2-input`, TextEdit and Finder; the second: `spike/sit2/u3u4`, `spike/sit2/u12u13`, the four scenes; in both the device-side front-window probe read the frontmost application and the top window after every action and the activation watcher ran through every cell, and no action in either sitting changed the frontmost application or the top window or was seen as an activation):

1. **Accessibility actions** for element-targeted requests when the element advertises the action: `AXPick` when advertised, else `AXPress`, for a plain single left click (the vendor's choice, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.1; `AXShowMenu` is never used and right clicks are synthesized), `AXIncrement`/`AXDecrement`, the element's own secondary actions for `performSecondaryAction`, `AXValue` for `setValue` when settable, `AXSelectedTextRange` for `selectText`. These reach the element without events; into an app that does not believe it has focus they take route 3's held activation first and report route 3 (amended 2026-10-22: an Electron button's `AXPress` and its field's value and selection writes were ineffective in a background app by the action alone), and inside a recorded menu's span they stay route 1. The route applies only where the element advertises the action: the TextEdit document's text area and a Finder row's cell advertise neither `AXPick` nor `AXPress`, so the forced route-1 cells refused on both (the route's precondition, not a delivery failure), while the Electron fixture's button and the Chrome loopback page's checkbox do, and the background click on each went by this route in the second sitting (the natural route for both; `treeChanged: true`). The action is taken only when a hit-test at the element's clickable point (the midpoint of its visible frame, scrolled into view) does not land on a strict descendant of the element; when it does, the vendor's nested-content gate (§5.1, its "web view" case), the click is synthesized instead, since the action would reach the container and not the control the point names.
2. **Synthesized events posted to the owning process with the vendor's recipe** (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.3–5.4, §6.2) for everything else: coordinate clicks, multi-clicks, right clicks, drags, scroll wheel, key presses and typed text, in two forms. **Route 2′** is the plain post: the same events with no ⌘ flag and no fake activation, which the vendor never takes into a background app. Measured in the second sitting (U12): **keys by route 2′ delivered on all four scenes**, three of three on each by outcome, with no activation seen and, on the Electron fixture, zero became-active, resigned-active, window, page or text focus and blur events per key; **clicks by route 2′ delivered on Finder and Electron only** (TextEdit's caret did not move, three of three for the element click and the coordinate click alike; Chrome's checkbox stayed unchecked and its focus unchanged). So it is the route for typed text and plain keys and for no click; a ⌘ chord, a menu key equivalent, takes route 3 into an app that does not believe it has focus (amended 2026-10-22: TextEdit's ⌘A into a background window left the selection unchanged by 2′, with ⌘ on every event). The vendor's fake activation before every keyboard action (§6.1) is a declared difference: it fires the app's activation hooks on every key for no delivery gain. **Route 2** is the ⌘ post, the vendor's ordinary background click. Mouse events are made through AppKit (`NSEvent.mouseEvent(...)` taken as a `CGEvent`, no event source), carry the global point as their location and the window-local point through the private `CGEventSetWindowLocation` (recovered by name; `dlsym`), the button in field 3, mouse subtype 3 in field 7 and the window id in fields 91 and 92, and are posted back-to-back with `CGEventPostToPid`, down and up per click; **when the target app is not active the ⌘ flag (0x100000) is added**, which AppKit treats as a click to deliver to an inactive window without activating it; that, not SPI, is the vendor's ordinary background click (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.4). Measured by outcome in the second sitting (U10): a ⌘-click delivered on Chrome and Electron, **did not move TextEdit's caret** (three of three), and **changed Finder's outcome**, adding the clicked row to the selection (`["003"]` → `["003", "005"]`) where a plain click selects it alone; ⌘-flagged keys delivered everywhere. So route 2 is no default and no fallback: a click reaches the app with the flag, and the flag changes its meaning. It stays in the engine as a forced spike route only. Keyboard, scroll and modifier events come from an HID-system event source (`CGEventSourceCreate(.hidSystemState)`), as the vendor's do, because the Text Services Manager and some apps ignore other source states; what that costs the intervention rule is now known ("User intervention": the engine's own posts pass the session tap, where its source pid excludes them, and reset the HID idle counter). The real pointer is never moved.
3. **Fake activation**: post to the pid an AppKit-defined event of subtype 1 (application activated; modifier flags 0xC0000, the window number set) plus a left mouse down and up at the window's `AXActivationPoint`, so the app believes it is active while the user's app stays frontmost; the vendor wraps it in `SLSDisableUpdate`/`SLSReenableUpdate`, polls every 10 ms up to 1 s that the user's app is still frontmost (answering a window that ordered above the user's with `AXRaise` on the user's window), re-activates the user's original app with `activateWithOptions: 0` if it lost frontmost, and posts the matching deactivation subtype when done (`SyntheticAppFocusEnforcer`, §5.5); its `SystemFocusStealPreventer` (§5.6) watches process-level focus notifications and hands stolen key focus back through `CPSReleaseKeyFocusWithID`. The event posting is public API; the SLS and CPS calls are private, resolved by `dlsym` with feature detection and skipped when absent. The believed-focus state the route turns on is read through the private `CPSGetKeyFocusProcess`, which takes two out-arguments, the process serial number and a Boolean: the sitting's first series declared one and crashed in SkyLight on every input that reached the check (27 crash reports; Surprises), so every private declaration the engine resolves is pinned by arity in `R/F-helper-logic.md` §11 and exercised by a call in the engine's test target, not only resolved by `dlsym`. **This is the engine's route for every synthesized click into an app that does not believe it has focus**, an element click where no action is advertised and every coordinate click, **and, since 2026-10-22, for a ⌘ chord and an element's own action or attribute write into such an app** (route 1 above, route 2′'s keys). For clicks the vendor reserves the fake activation for held keys or non-zero flags, elements inside a web view (really nested content, §5.1), `clickingMayCauseSelection`, Catalyst apps and menu-bar items, and ⌘-clicks every other background click; cua's departure is to take route 3 for every synthesized background click, because the ⌘ flag is visible to the app and changes what a click means (route 2 above: Finder's selection), and because it is the only click route that delivered on all four scenes (U12, three of three on each, element and coordinate clicks alike, where route 2′ failed on TextEdit and Chrome and route 2 on TextEdit and Finder). Measured in both sittings (but see the Decision Log, #200, 2026-11-01: on the MacBook a route-3 click raised the target's window at its own mouse down, which the delivery guard now undoes, and the paste route's events go out through the same guard and watch, the Decision Log's #200 paste entry of the same day): every route-3 action delivered with the frontmost application and the top window unchanged and no activation seen by the watcher, with the activation guard (the 10 ms poll) at 37–58 ms (a coordinate click into TextEdit 82), so a route-3 click costs about 40 ms more than a plain post, a tenth of the settle that follows an action. When the guard fails (`timeoutReached` from the poll, or the user's app lost frontmost and was re-activated) the click is **refused as `activationFailed`** with nothing posted (Interfaces), not retried with ⌘ (the vendor rethrows too: `R/F-helper-logic.md` §17 corrects §5.4's retry), because the retry would change the click's meaning; the result reports the route that carried the action. The earlier inference that this needed a SkyLight posting function with a window-local authentication field was wrong: no such function is in the binary (§11). **Its effect on the driven app, measured (U13, `spike/sit2/u12u13`, `effects.*`):** the two mechanisms are the per-action activate/deactivate pair, which runs the app's became-active and resigned-active hooks, and the activation pair's left mouse down and up at the window's `AXActivationPoint`, a real click into the app. On the mini all four scene windows list the attribute; TextEdit's, Finder's and Electron's activation points lie inside the window, 16 points below its top edge (TextEdit (30, 16), Finder (10, 16), Electron (30, 16) relative to the frame), and a hit-test there answers the window itself, no control; **Chrome's is (−1, 1441), outside its frame** (80, 60, 1000 × 760), where the hit-test answers Chrome's menu bar (the MacBook read the same corner, (0, 982), on a Finder desktop window, and attribute-unsupported on Safari's and Chrome's windows, 2026-10-19). **So the activation click is never posted at a window's `AXActivationPoint` that lies outside the window's frame**; this is a declared difference from the vendor, which posts the pair wherever the point is. **Inside the frame it is posted at the point only when a hit-test there answers the window itself or window chrome that is no control** (the case U13 read on TextEdit, Finder and Electron); on a control, an element advertising a click-invoked action, an element whose actions could not be read, or no answer, nothing is clicked at the point, with no fallback to route 1 (maws-3f's ruling, 2026-10-22, on the focus hold's review: a click on a control would invoke it before the action the model asked for). **A hold never ends with its arm set** (amended 2026-11-01, Decision Log, #187's inert click; it replaced the skip-only rule, under which Chrome was left armed with no key window): wherever the click at the point is skipped, an inert click is the fallback, posted right after subtype 1 for a holder that posts no left mouse down of its own (keys, element actions and writes, the menu record, paste, a non-left click, and such a holder joining a hold whose arm is unconsumed), while a holder that does (a left click, a drag) lets its own mouse down consume the arm as before, with no ordering, and the inert click goes before subtype 2 only when none went (a refusal or an error before the click). The click is a left down and up stamped with the window's number at a point outside the window's frame, the `AXActivationPoint` itself when it lies at least 8 pt beyond the frame, else 24 pt left of the frame at its mid-height (AppKit's resize band reaches 4 pt outside a titled resizable frame); AppKit routes it by the window number, so it consumes the activation's arm (the window made key and main, nothing ordered) and reaches no view. Only a spared activation point (a non-left click's target), a window with no number and a window of unknown frame take no click. The result's meta `activationClick` says what was decided at the point, and `activationClickKind` which click consumed the arm and when. A selection set through `selectText` (location 4, length 9) was preserved through a route-3 activation and through a following key on TextEdit, Chrome and Electron (`effects.selection`, `preservedByActivate` and `preservedByKey` true on all three). The Electron fixture counted, per route-3 action, exactly one became-active, one resigned-active, one window focus and blur, one page focus and blur and one text focus and blur, and none per route-2′ or route-2 action (`effects.counters`): the hooks fire once per action, so with keys on route 2′ only synthesized clicks pay for them. The `SLSDisableUpdate` hold is released as soon as the activation pair is posted, or capped well under the guard's 1 s poll, so a slow guard never freezes the display for a second. **The activation stays per action for synthesized clicks, drags and paste**, posted and withdrawn around each (the hooks fire once per action and the selection survives). **For a ⌘ chord and an element's own action it is held, the vendor's enforcer lifecycle** (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.5, §16.9 item 6): the first such action of a connection on an app activates it with the guard, the actions after it run inside it, and it is withdrawn when the connection closes, before a request aimed at another app, or after 120 s with no request observing or acting on the app; the activation is the process's, and every holder takes it through one registry, the holds, a recorded menu's span and each synthesized click, drag or paste for its action's duration, so only the last holder's release withdraws it; a real activation or deactivation of the held app makes the hold stale, and the next action re-establishes it (Decision Log, 2026-10-22, both entries). Whether synthesized clicks should join the hold, as the vendor's do (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §23.18), stays open (the critique's A1(iii)), to be decided from M2b's matrix rather than guessed.
4. **Foreground assist**, bounded and reported: activate the app, post, then restore the previously frontmost app and its key window. The result's meta (`cua/inputRoute: 4`) lets the harness count it, and the non-interference metric counts it as a failure for that action, except for a family whose route 4 the owner accepted (Decision Log). It exists so a canvas app still works; it is not a route the engine chooses silently, and no scene needed it in M0. It is reachable only through a per-app override the owner writes, `routes: {<bundle id>: 4}` in `~/.config/cua/engine.json` (default none), so a family the owner accepted gets it by name and no other app ever does; when route 3's guard fails for an app on three actions in a row, the result's meta carries `cua/routeAdvice` naming the override and `doctor` lists the app, so the owner learns of the case rather than the engine deciding it.

Which route each family lands on was decided by measurement, not assumed. The second sitting's matrix (U12, U10; three samples per cell, judged by outcome): **keys** by 2′, 2 and 3 delivered on every scene; **element and coordinate clicks** delivered on TextEdit by 3 only, on Finder by 2′ and 3 (2 added to the selection), on Chrome by 3 and 2 (2′ left the checkbox and the focus unchanged), on Electron by all three. The natural routes (`spike/sit2/u3u4`): TextEdit and Finder, click and key by route 3 before the table, route 1 not advertised by their targets; **Chrome**, the element click by route 1 (the loopback page's checkbox advertises `AXPress`), the key by route 3, and probe (e)'s Latin `type_text` by route 3 landing 7 of 8 exactly; **Electron**, the click by route 1 (the fixture's button), the key by route 3, both delivered. The first sitting's Chrome refusals and its 0-of-8 typing were the harness's: the first sitting's `-AppleLanguages (en-US)` pair left a stray `(en-US)` argument that Chrome opened as a second tab beside the scene, so the scene's window was never the front one (fixed in e0751a31); the owner's route-4 fork for Chrome is therefore resolved as not needed. The first sitting's build had sent coordinate clicks by route 2 with ⌘ when the app is inactive, which no live cell exercised then; the table above replaces it. The baseline's `super+w` and `super+n` prove nothing about background delivery: the harness sent them while TextEdit was frontmost (the #162 key guard). Every result carries `cua/inputRoute` (1, `"2'"`, 3, 4, or `"paste"` for the text fallback below; 2 only when forced by a spike cell) so the matrices can see what carried each action.

**Keys** (`pressKey`): the vendor's xdotool-style names, exact and case-sensitive, from its fixed ANSI key table (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §6.4: `super`/`cmd`/`command`, `ctrl`, `alt`, `shift`, `Return`, `KP_0`, …; no `Control`, `Ctrl`, `Alt`, `Shift`, `Enter` or `Esc` exists, whatever the vendor's documentation says), sequences split on space and combos on `+`, each combo one key press with the vendor's four events (a flags-changed with the mask, key down, key up, a flags-changed restoring the session's real modifier state), no gap between events; cua accepts exactly the vendor's names in this work (aliases would be an extension recorded in the first-call document) and corrects the vendor's `equal` defect, which types `+`.

**Text** (`typeText`): the engine reads the active input source (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardInputSource`) and routes by it. With a Latin source active, every character the layout can produce is keyed as the vendor keys it (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §6.2): one key press per UTF-16 unit, four events each (flags, key down, key up, flags restored), 250 µs apart, whose virtual key code and modifier flags come from a reverse map of the layout (`UCKeyTranslate` with `kUCKeyActionDisplay` over key codes 0–127 under the vendor's eight shift/option/control masks, never ⌘, built once per layout and rebuilt on `kTISNotifySelectedKeyboardInputSourceChanged`; the engine also compares the current source's id (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardInputSource`, read directly rather than through a notification flag, since the notification would wait for the run loop the request occupies) before every event and after the last, so a source switch during a keyed request, the user switching to Hangul for their own window mid-action, stops the request with the error name `inputSourceChanged` and a bounded sentence naming how many characters (UTF-16 units) were entered, rather than letting the Hangul input method compose the remaining Latin keys; the wrapper's value-free classification admits that name, and for a concealed request renders its "may have been partly entered" sentence) and whose Unicode string (`CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`) is the character, so an app that reads virtual key codes and an app that reads the event's characters both receive it; a character the layout cannot key (an emoji, CJK, a combining sequence) goes as a Unicode-string event with key code 0, in chunks of at most 20 UTF-16 units that never split a surrogate pair or a combining sequence (cua's departure: the vendor logs and drops such characters, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §6.2, which is what the Korean and Unicode matrix must beat). Events are posted to the owning process by route 2′, the plain post, with no fake activation first (the vendor's fake activation before every keyboard action, §6.1, is a declared difference: route 2′ delivered keyed text and key presses on all four scenes in the second sitting without firing the app's activation hooks, "Input"); a newline is a real Return key event. With the Korean source active, the Text Services Manager interprets key events by key code and composition state, so neither form is trusted: the engine takes the paste route for the text and sends Return as key events, each Return between two lines settled before the next line's baseline so its late effect cannot confirm that line (Decision Log, #187's Return settle, 2026-11-01) (the paste route is M2a's; the spike refused such a request with a sentence naming it, so probe (e)'s Korean-source rows are unmeasured until then). One more case makes the session-wide reading unreliable: macOS can hold an input source per app (Keyboard → Input Sources → "Automatically switch to a document's input source", the per-context input preference in `com.apple.HIToolbox`), and when it is on, a background driven app may sit in its own Hangul context while the session's source reads Latin; the engine reads that preference and, when it is on and the driven app is not frontmost, treats the source as unknown and takes the paste route. M0 probe (e) checks whether keyed Latin events are transliterated to jamo by the Hangul input method in that state. Every paste fallback is reported (`cua/inputRoute: "paste"`). There is no per-app fallback list: the matrix proves the keyed form on its scenes, and the ruling is any ordinary app. **Secrets never take the paste route.** The vendor's typeText keyed through the layout and never touched the pasteboard, which clipboard managers poll and keep; so `src/services/sky.mjs` marks the request copy it substitutes into with `concealed: true`, and for a concealed `type_text` the engine keys the text under a Latin source whose reading is reliable (the per-context preference off, or the driven app frontmost) and otherwise fails closed before anything is entered with the error name `concealedInputUnavailable`; a concealed `paste` is refused with the same name. The wrapper's value-free classification (`src/services/sky.mjs`, `secret-input.mjs`) admits that name and renders it as its own fixed sentence, which says that nothing was entered, names the active input source, and names `setValue` as the alternative (a settable field takes the value through `AXValue`, no keystrokes and no pasteboard); the wrapper's generic "may have been partly entered" sentence is reserved for every other failure after substitution. Never the value. The vendor keys through the layout and drops what it cannot key; `src/mcp/surface.mjs`'s "paste emoji" rule and its secrets rule are revised in M4 once the matrix shows what cua carries.

**Paste** (`paste {text, format: text|md|html}`): declare the pasteboard contents through a lazy data provider (an `NSPasteboardItem` whose data is supplied when an app asks for it; the vendor's representations, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §7.2: `text` → plain only; `html` and `md` (Markdown converted to HTML first) → `public.html` as the source, `public.rtf`, and plain as the rendered text), with `NSPasteboardContentsCurrentHostOnly` so the paste stays off Universal Clipboard, and in addition, cua's own, the `org.nspasteboard.ConcealedType` and `org.nspasteboard.TransientType` marker types that clipboard managers honour by not recording the item (the vendor writes no markers); snapshot the previous contents; give the app route 3's fake activation when it does not believe it has focus, as the vendor does before ⌘V (§7.1, 7.3); post ⌘V to the owning process, the paste's first event (its first ⌘V, or a Return opening the text) through route 3's delivery guard inside the activation's hold and every event under the after-action watch until a second after the last, as a pointer action's (Decision Log, #200 on the paste route, 2026-11-01); treat the provider's callback as the evidence that the app read the pasteboard (bounded at 2 s), then wait up to 2 s, polling every 25 ms, for an effect (a `valueChanged` or `selectedTextChanged` notification, or a change of the focused element's selected range or character count against the snapshot taken before the paste), the read wait failing with `clipboardReadTimedOut` (the vendor's sentence, extended) and the effect wait with `pasteUnconfirmed`, both bounded by the request's deadline too, with no effect wait at all when nothing could show an effect (then a read after ⌘V answers success with an unconfirmed note in the result's text, a read only before ⌘V `pasteUnconfirmed`), where the vendor answers both with its one read sentence (§7.4–7.5; cua's departure, Decision Log #187, 2026-11-01); then restore the previous pasteboard contents on every path, success or error, only if the change count is still the engine's own (a human's newer clipboard is never overwritten; the vendor's "The user may have conflicted with your paste operation" sentence covers that case, §7.6). Secret substitution is unaffected: the wrapper places the value into the request before the service runs, whichever route delivers it. The vendor serializes pastes process-wide while cua's one-in-flight rule is per connection, so two connections pasting at once could clobber the pasteboard and each other's restore (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §23.5): the engine holds one engine-wide FIFO paste lock from the snapshot through the restore, across connections, and `type_text`'s paste route takes the same lock (the critique of 2026-10-19).

**Scroll**: one scroll-wheel event in pixels, `round(pages × max(the element's or window's length on that axis, 100))`, at the element's centre or the point, the element first scrolled into view (the vendor's amount, revised 2026-10-21, Decision Log "M2a vendor reading"; a horizontal scroll carries its delta on the horizontal axis); a scroll never arms the settle (the vendor's rule, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §8.1). **Drag**: down, interpolated moves, up, posted to the owning process.

**Settle.** The vendor's rule, read in full (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §8): after an action, before the next observation on that app, the engine waits for quiet. An `AXObserver` on the application, created for the wait, listens for ten notifications: `titleChanged` (ignored when the title is unchanged), `layoutChanged`, `focusedWindowChanged`, `focusedUIElementChanged`, `elementBusyChanged`, `menuOpened`, `sheetCreated`, `windowCreated`, `created` and `uiElementDestroyed` (not `valueChanged`). Sampling every 100 ms (250 ms for a web-shaped app: the bundle's URL types include `http`, or its principal class is Electron's `AtomApplication` or Chromium's `BrowserCrApplication`), the engine returns once 250 ms have passed since the last notification, or since the wait began when none arrives, so a native app with nothing posted returns at about 300 ms (there is no 1 s floor; the earlier reading of one was wrong); it keeps waiting while the primary window's tree shows a busy sign (`AXBusyIndicator`, an `AXWebArea` with `AXLoaded` false, `AXElementBusy`, or the DOM class `macos-mac-parity-spinner`; a progress indicator is not one), and gives up at 5 s whatever keeps arriving (a browser page's animations and live regions post layout changes continuously), which the vendor does silently and cua reports as `cua/settle: "capped"` so the settle cell can tell it from slowness. Because the vendor's IPC path defers the settle to the next `get_app_state` rather than running it inside the action (§8.6), cua does the same: an action returns when its events are posted, and the next observation on that app settles first, reusing the tree the busy walk fetched. An observation no action preceded settles only when its preparation had to open the app (a launch, or the reopen of a running windowless app): after the window wait it settles with a 50 ms sampling delay and the 250 ms quiet period, the vendor's `0x100164c08`, and then observes; no observation waits for web content to fill beyond the web area's presence (the vendor's rule, Decision Log "M2a vendor reading"). M0 measured the settle at rest on the four scenes (the cua lane's first series, five samples per scene per run, three runs; `spike/summary.md`, the engine's per-phase timings in `results.jsonl`): no notification arrived in any sample; the quiet point came at 307–315 ms on the native scenes (the third 100 ms tick) and 251–255 ms on the web-shaped ones, the observer cost about 1 ms, and one busy walk followed (TextEdit 43 ms, Finder-200 137, Chrome 41, Electron 10) whose tree the observation reused, for engine totals of 431, 550, 358 and 328 ms and in-cell 440, 582, 369 and 337. That is the rule's own cost, and it is the vendor's: the vendor returns at the same quiet point plus its own walk (§8.3), and on the mini the three-cell TextEdit task, whose `act` and `close` cells each settle before their observation, took cua 2,755 ms against the vendor's 4,028 like for like (iteration 2; `act` 722 against 980, `close` 1,617 against 2,603), so the settle is not where cua loses time. The design therefore keeps the vendor's order (the busy walk after the quiet point) and timing (the check on the sampling tick). The two cheaper orders, the busy walk started at the wait's start with its reads kept when nothing arrives, and the check at exactly 250 ms, remain recorded alternatives, not taken: they would save the walk's time on a quiet app, but the first can miss a value change, since `valueChanged` is not among the ten notifications, and the measured cost gives no reason to take that risk. What the reuse does not cover, the observation's own per-element actions, settable, focusable and selectable reads after the settle (Finder-200: 72 ms of walk after the 137 ms busy walk), is M2a's tuning, not a rule change. The cell's time budget is suspended around launch wait, action and settle through `nodeRepl.withSuspendedTimeout`, which the own host honours by pausing the budget on the worker's `suspend_timeout` (`src/repl-host/supervisor.mjs`).

**User intervention.** The vendor's mechanism, read in full (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §9), is a listen-only event tap at the annotated session level on five event types (left, right and other mouse down, scroll wheel, key down), excluding only events whose source pid is the helper's own, attributing each event to an app by the owner of the window under it (private field 51) or else by the event's target pid, with no frontmost test; an event attributed to a driven app renews a 5 s debounce and sets a sticky re-query flag that only a successful `get_app_state` clears. cua keeps that mechanism and attribution: a click or scroll on the driven app's window counts, a keystroke counts when the driven app is the event's target, and the user typing or clicking in their own window never counts, which is the promise of the purpose section without any frontmost heuristic. The tap exists only so the engine yields to the human: its callback reads the event type, the source pid, the target pid and the window id and nothing else, never a key code, never characters, never a location it stores, and it is torn down when the last connection closes (a unit test pins the fields the callback touches). The engine's own events are excluded by source pid, as the vendor's are, and by `CGEventPostToPid`'s delivery, which does not pass a session tap (a run-time question probe (d) was to settle). **Decided (the owner, 2026-10-20, on the second sitting's evidence): intervention detection requires Input Monitoring, through the listen-only tap.** The second sitting measured (U5, `spike/sit2/u5-ax-only` and `u5-input-monitoring`, the engine launched fresh for each pass, the owner switching the grant off and on between them): under Accessibility and Screen Recording alone both tap kinds, the listen-only tap and an active tap with the default options, are created (the listen preflight false), but neither receives an event: the engine's own key post counted 0 and the probe's three HID-tap keystrokes 0 of 3 on both; with Input Monitoring on, both receive them (own 1 of 1, driven 3 of 3). And the HID idle counter is reset by the engine's own posts, reading 420–783 ms right after one against 31–37 s before, so the HID-idle fallback could not see the user during an action, which is exactly the case intervention exists for; it is withdrawn. The active tap needs the same grant and sits in the event path, where a slow callback would delay the user's input, so the listen-only tap is the mechanism, as the vendor's. **Without the grant the engine works with intervention detection off**: `status.inputMonitoring` reads false, `doctor` says so, every input result's meta carries `cua/intervention: "off"`, and the in-flight stop, the pre-action refusal and the re-query lock below do not run; acceptance 1's permission keys gain `inputMonitoring`, and onboarding grants three permissions by hand (Accessibility, Screen Recording, Input Monitoring; "Install, signing and permissions"). Two of probe (d)'s questions are answered on the way: the engine's own pid-posted HID-source events **do** pass the annotated-session tap (counted as its own by source pid: 1 of 1, and 10,009 of 10,009 during a 10 s `type_text`, U6), so the exclusion by source pid is load-bearing, not the backstop F §9.2 inferred; and an HID-tap-posted keystroke is seen by the tap (3 of 3) and resets the counter (610–733 ms after the post), so the probe serves the matrix's positive cells. The operator steps ran (U6, `spike/sit2/u6`): during a 10 s `type_text` into the frontmost TextEdit scene the owner's one keystroke over Screen Sharing was attributed to the driven app by the tap, six key events, all inside the action's interval, and one click into the scene's background window likewise, one of one inside; the rule that acts on them is M2a's (the rows report `intervened: false` because the spike engine has no intervention path, its tap being a counting probe), so the promise that the engine's own `super+n`, sheet clicks and window changes never fire it is measured by M2b's cells, not yet. What cua does with the evidence differs from the vendor in two bounded ways, both recorded as expected differences: an in-flight sequence **stops** at the first attributed event and fails with `userIntervened` and the vendor's "The user changed '<app>'…" sentence, a `type_text` so stopped reporting in its meta how many UTF-16 units were entered (`entered`, as the `inputSourceChanged` sentence counts them) (the vendor never stops an action in flight and reports the intervention only at the next request, §9.5); and the pre-action guard refuses at once, for every input kind, with the vendor's "The user is still interacting with '<app>'. Wait 5 seconds and retry." sentence while the debounce is live (the vendor waits out the debounce for up to 100 s before proceeding or refusing, which stalls the model for the whole time the user keeps working in the app). After an intervention the engine refuses further actions on that app until a fresh `get_app_state` has been taken, as the vendor does and as its sentence tells the model. A `get_app_state` during the live debounce observes at once and clears the re-query flag only if the debounce has expired by the time the observation completes (the critique of 2026-10-19; `R/sky-behaviour.md` §23.4): the model's own re-query after a refusal is never itself refused, and a re-query taken while the user is still working leaves the flag set, so the next action is refused rather than posted into the user's work. The engine's own `super+n`, its click on a sheet button, the window it opens or closes, and a route-4 activation it performs itself are never interventions, since none of them is a tapped user event attributed to the app. `cua/hidIdleMs` on results and refusals reports the milliseconds since the last user event the engine attributed to the driven app. Input through Screen Sharing counts as the user's (it enters at the HID level like a local user's): the second sitting's operator steps above were sent over Screen Sharing.

**Lock screen.** `CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary`'s `CGSSessionScreenIsLocked` true → every request fails `screenLocked` (−10020) with the vendor's first sentence. No auto-unlock, no guardian.

### The service: `src/services/engine/`

The service replaces `@oai/sky`'s macOS target behind `src/services/sky.mjs`. On the vendor host (comparison A) it loads in the vendor trusted worker through the same slot, so `src/services/engine/` joins `NODE_REPL_TRUSTED_CODE_PATHS` in the launcher. Its parts:

- **Client** (`client.mjs`): connects through `nodeRepl.nativePipe.createConnection(path)` (the own host's relay, `src/repl-host/native-pipe.mjs`, opens the socket outside the sandbox; the vendor host's relay does the same), pings with `cua-engine-1` and checks both `engineVersion` and `build` against the bundle it was given, launches the bundle through `launchServices.openApplication` when the connect fails, reconnects for up to 5 s, fails with the `cua install --engine` diagnostic on a mismatch, chains one request at a time with the 120 s default deadline, wraps launch wait, action and settle in `nodeRepl.withSuspendedTimeout`, and raises `SkyComputerUseError` / `SkyComputerUseTransportError` with the vendor's `errorName` values so `src/services/sky.mjs` classifies them unchanged.
- **Renderer** (`render.mjs`): `Skyshot` JSON → the vendor's text grammar (`R/A-contract.md` §3.2, the literal set in `R/E-helper-catalogue.md` §5, the composition rules `R/sky-behaviour.md` establishes): the `Window: "<title>", App: <name>.` header (no `App=` line: `R/F-helper-logic.md` §2.6 shows neither producer of the IPC text emits one; the TextEdit test double's first line is corrected in M1a), one line per element, `<id> <roleWord> (<flags>) <parts>` with tabs per depth: the role word is the localized role description with the vendor's overrides (`row`, `element`, `container`, nothing for menu-bar items, menu items and radio groups), the flags in the vendor's order (`selectable`, `selected`, `disabled`, `expanded`/`collapsed`, `settable` or `settable, <type>`), then the parts in the vendor's order, title bare, `Description`, `Rows`, `Columns`, `URL` (shortened under the 4,000-character budget), `Help`, `Value`, `Details`, `Placeholder`, `ID` (dropped entirely when it starts with `_` or `NS`) and `Secondary Actions`, a part whose value repeats an earlier part skipped, long parts moved last, a lone part printed without its label (§2.4); partial arrays rendered as `(showing … of N items)`; links rendered as Markdown; the trailer with the vendor's precedence, selected text, else selected elements, else the focused element (§2.6); the truncation markers. The nine tree transformations and four Mac-only ones the vendor applies before rendering are named in E, their predicates recovered in F §2.1–2.3, and decided in `sky-behaviour.md` from F and the captures.
- **Diff** (`diff.mjs`): per (connection, app), the default mode diffs against the previous rendered tree, keyed on element id, with the vendor's one live header (`The following is a diff from the previous accessibility tree for Window: "<title>" …` with the removed-ID-ranges legend, the flag that chooses the legend defaulting to true; the cumulative header is dead code, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §4.1–4.2), `~`/`+`/`-` marks before the tabs, inserted and removed subtrees in full, no context lines, changes in depth-first order, the `Removed element IDs:` ranges line first, the no-change sentence when nothing changed, and the full tree instead of the diff when the diff's lines would exceed the full tree's node count (the vendor's budget, ratio 1.0, §4.3); `disableDiff` returns the full text on a fresh lineage; every observation advances the baseline, including one whose text the facade discards for a screenshot (the first-call document says so; the vendor has no screenshot-only mode, §4.8).
