```
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs run --device mini --experiment <sitting> --approved-runtime <home> --approved-engine <bundle> --host vendor --engine vendor --out <dir>/vv/run-<n>
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs run --device mini --experiment <sitting> --approved-runtime <home> --approved-engine <bundle> --host vendor --engine cua    --out <dir>/vc/run-<n>
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs run --device mini --experiment <sitting> --approved-runtime <home> --approved-engine <bundle> --host cua    --engine cua    --out <dir>/cc/run-<n>
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs compare --engines    <dir>/vv <dir>/vc --out <dir>/A.md
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs compare --hosts      <dir>/vc <dir>/cc --out <dir>/B.md      # M3
node scripts/native-baseline.mjs compare --end-to-end <dir>/vv <dir>/cc --out <dir>/C.md      # M3
# expected: each prints VERDICT: PASS (or NO VERDICT with the missing rows named) and the per-operation table

# M2b onward: the differential suite and the matrix (A = --approved-runtime <home> --approved-engine <bundle>)
node scripts/repl-parity.mjs prepare --device mini --commit <sha> $A
node scripts/repl-parity.mjs run --device mini $A --suite native-mac --host vendor --engine vendor --out <dir>/vendor
node scripts/repl-parity.mjs run --device mini $A --suite native-mac --host cua    --engine cua    --out <dir>/cua
node scripts/repl-parity.mjs diff <dir>/vendor <dir>/cua --expected scripts/repl-parity/expected/native-mac.json
# expected: the JSON report ending with `summary: 0 unexpected differences` and the declared list
node scripts/native-matrix.mjs run --device mini $A --host vendor --engine vendor --runs 3 --out <dir>/matrix/vendor
node scripts/native-matrix.mjs run --device mini $A --host cua    --engine cua    --runs 3 --out <dir>/matrix/cua
node scripts/native-matrix.mjs summary <dir>/matrix --out docs/evidence/<sitting date>-own-native-engine/summary.md
# expected: the method table, the per-scene speed table, the three-point section, the Korean matrix, the concurrent-use row

# M5: acceptance, one line per item (owner-run steps marked); items 1, 2, 8 and 9 run on the mini in the sitting's prepared checkout, the rest from the MacBook with --device mini
ssh mini 'cd <prepared checkout> && CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=cua node bin/cua.mjs doctor --json | jq .engine' # item 1 (the setting is explicit because the sitting runs before the default flips; harmless after)
ssh mini 'cd <prepared checkout> && node verify.mjs --engine cua --bind com.apple.TextEdit'            # item 2 (socket holders, one approval, the second bind silent)
node scripts/native-matrix.mjs cell non-interference --device mini $A --scene textedit --user finder   # item 3 (frontmost, top window, focus, watcher)
node scripts/native-matrix.mjs cell korean --device mini $A --scene textedit    # item 4
(the compare commands above)                                                    # item 5
(the diff command above)                                                        # item 6
(the summary command above)                                                     # item 7
ssh mini 'cd <prepared checkout> && export CUA_HOME=$(mktemp -d /tmp/cua-accept.XXXXXX) && node bin/cua.mjs install --archive <pinned runtime zip on the mini> && node scripts/accept-native.mjs --engine cua --live-textedit --report "$CUA_HOME/acceptance.json"'   # item 8 on a scratch home installed from the archive and serving the approved engine; OWNER present: the fresh install copies the vendor helper again, so the vendor-pinned items 12 and 13 may raise the capture confirmation; the onboarding item after the OWNER revokes and regrants
ssh mini 'cd <prepared checkout> && CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor node verify.mjs'                          # item 9

# Unit tests, every milestone
npm test
```

## Interfaces and Dependencies

**EngineProtocol** (`native/engine/Sources/Engine/Protocol.swift`, `src/services/engine/protocol.mjs`): JSON-RPC 2.0 over the 4-byte little-endian length-prefixed framing, 8 MiB per frame; methods `ping`, `execute`, `status`, `approve`, `shutdown`; `serverApiVersion = "cua-engine-1"`.

```
ping     {clientApiVersion: string} → {serverApiVersion: string, engineVersion: string, build: string}
execute  {method: string, args: object, deadlineUnixMilliseconds: number, lineage?: string} → result | error
         (lineage: the lineage an element token in args belongs to; a token from another lineage or a retired one answers the
          "no longer valid" sentence; the deadline is checked on receipt and cancels a settle or launch wait still running when it
          passes. An element-targeted method carries args.element: token, the service's translation of the facade's element_index)
status   {} → {engineVersion, build, accessibility: boolean, screenRecording: boolean, inputMonitoring: boolean, canPostHid: boolean, socket: string, pid: number, bundlePath: string, connections: number,
          holders: [{pid: number | null, label: string | null}]}
         (connections = open connections other than the requesting one, so `connections > 0` means "someone else"; shutdown's "busy" uses the same count;
          holders (#237) names them in accept order: the peer's pid read at accept (LOCAL_PEERPID) and the session's label from its cursor
          requests (at most 64 characters, client-asserted), each null when unknown; an engine before #237 sends no holders;
          inputMonitoring is the non-prompting listen preflight, CGPreflightListenEventAccess, the intervention tap's grant, read for doctor and
          the approve step and never requested by the engine; false means the engine runs with intervention detection off)
approve  {} → {accessibility: boolean, screenRecording: boolean, captured: boolean}   (calls the two prompting requests, whose prompts may not appear, and reads the grants;
          captures one display image and discards it once both read true; the by-hand path is the command's, "Install, signing and permissions")
shutdown {} → {} then exit, or error "busy" {connections: n, holders} while another connection is open

methods: list_apps, get_app_state, click, drag, paste, perform_secondary_action, press_key, scroll, select_text,
         set_value, type_text (the facade's shapes, R/A-contract.md §1.4), resolve_app {app} | {pid}
         (a coordinate in click, drag or scroll is in the pixel space of the screenshot the connection's last screenshot-bearing
          observation of the app returned, divided by that observation's per-axis scale and offset by the current window origin;
          refused as noScreenshotForCoordinates, nothing posted, when the connection holds no such observation, the named lineage
          is not current, or the app's primary window is no longer the one captured; "Screenshot")

resolve_app → {appPath: string, bundleIdentifier: string, displayName: string, isRunning: boolean, pid: number | null}
get_app_state {app, disableDiff?: boolean, includeScreenshot?: boolean, outputDirectory: string} → Skyshot
Skyshot  {app: {bundleIdentifier, displayName, appPath, pid, catalyst: boolean}, window: {title: string | null, frame: Rect, pid: number},
          lineage: string, revision: number, nodes: Node[], truncated: {depth?: number, count?: number, above?: number, below?: number} | null,
          screenshot: {url: string, width: number, height: number, scale: number} | null, focused: string | null,
          selectedText: string | null, selectedElements: string[], menuBar: Node[], settle?: "capped"}
          (app.catalyst: the vendor's ApplicationUIElement.isCatalystApp 0x100704c88, the application's
          `_AXExhibitsMacCatalystBehavior`, false when absent; filterActions drops AXCancel in such an app (M1a.2b).
          window.title: the vendor's `ApplicationWindow.title` 0x10076e174, the window server's name for the window, else its
          AXTitle, "Desktop" for an untitled Finder window at the desktop-icon level, null when there is none (the renderer
          prints `Unknown`); without Screen Recording the window server gives no name and the AX title stands (M1a.2b).
          menuBar: the AXMenuBar element at depth 0 (roleDescription null when it lists none, printed `menu bar`), then its
          top-level items at depth 1 but the first (the Apple menu) and any titled `Debug`, each read through the attributes
          it lists, tokens in the same lineage after the window's nodes (`addMenuBarWithImmediateItems` 0x1007569b8); empty
          when the bar itself cannot be read. The renderer prints the items through the ordinary line with their actions
          cleared, as the vendor does, and synthesizes no bar of its own (M1a.2b); their order in
          the text is the renderer's, from sky-behaviour.md. focused and selectedElements: the tokens the focused/selected-text
          trailer names. settle: "capped" when the settle that preceded this observation hit the 5 s cap — on the
          observation result, since the settle runs at the next get_app_state, not inside the action, R/sky-behaviour.md §23.
          timings, the engine's diagnostics, carries focusRead when the focus read named no node: the AXError, "notAnElement"
          or "outsideTheWalk"; M1a.2b), which the service passes on in `cua/observation` beside the focused element's
          index and role (#188), and #301's recordOnlyNodes. truncated.count: the omitted parts (each unread child of a
          read element, once, whatever it holds); above and below: how many of them lie past the visible area's top and
          bottom, present on a walk of the window, which the renderer turns into a direction (#301, ruling 66f90453)
Node     {token: string, depth: number, pid: number, role: string, subrole: string | null, roleDescription: string | null,
          title: string | null, description: string | null, value: string | number | boolean | null,
          valueType: "float" | "integer" | "boolean" | "string" | "date" | "unknown" | null, valueMarkdown: string | null,
          identifier: string | null, actionDescriptions: (string | null)[],
          valueDescription?: string, placeholder?: string, numberOfCharacters?: number, help?: string, url?: string,
          traits?: number, servesAsTitleFor?: string[], rowIndexRange?: [number, number], columnIndexRange?: [number, number],
          domClassList?: string[], scrollBars?: {horizontal: boolean, vertical: boolean},
          flags: {enabled: boolean, focused: boolean, focusable: boolean, selected: boolean, selectable: boolean,
                  settable: boolean, expanded?: boolean, disclosable?: boolean},
          actions: string[], frame: Rect | null, children: number,
          shown?: {count: number, of: number, subset: "visible" | "omitted", ranges?: [number, number][]}}   (the width rule: present when an
          element's children were cut to the visible subset or omitted above 499; the renderer's grammar needs every
          named field, R/sky-behaviour.md §2.4 and §23 — token is the engine's per-lineage handle token, stable while the handle is
          CFEqual at a matched position and retired after one absent revision; the service numbers the tokens that survive its
          transformations, so the model's indexes are the service's ("Indexes, tokens and handles"; M0's raw Skyshot goldens carry
          the spike engine's indexes and are re-keyed when M1a records the renderer goldens); servesAsTitleFor names the tokens a
          title element serves (the title-association rule), shown.ranges the index ranges of the children kept (the vendor's
          `(showing lo-hi, … of N)`), valueType backs the `settable, <type>` flag, domClassList
          backs the DOM-origin markers, disclosable/disclosing pairs with expanded for outline rows; the optional
          attributes are present only when the element carries them, so a tree from either fetch route has the same
          shape. The grammar never prints `checked`, so the flag is dropped. The derivations are the vendor's (FUN_10074cb90 in
          get_app_state's fullUIContext; `Derivations.swift`, M1a.2b): valueType is `valueTypeName` FUN_10074a794 (the CFNumber's
          own float or integer type; boolean for a switch or toggle checkbox), null without a value; actionDescriptions runs
          parallel to actions, the vendor's description past its fixed table and a custom action's `Name:` (FUN_1007816a0):
          null for an action either describes (the renderer prints the table's English word or the name, in every language;
          the system is not asked), else the system's description (AXUIElementCopyActionDescription), else the action's
          name; url is
          AXURL, or for a window without one its AXDocument parsed as a URL; flags.disclosable is AXDisclosing settable;
          flags.selected is AXSelected, else the selected trait of AXTraits; flags.selectable is AXSelected settable, never on a menu
          or menu-bar item (FUN_10077d1e4; AXSelectable is not read); flags.settable is read only for a node with a value and, in a Chromium window, only for AXComboBox,
          AXIncrementor (not a date or time field's), AXTextArea, AXTextField and a tab, FUN_100751614; roleDescription is null
          when the element lists none; scrollBars, present on a node with a page-scroll action, says whether it answers
          AXHorizontalScrollBar and AXVerticalScrollBar. `actions` are the element's own; the renderer applies the vendor's
          filterActions, FUN_100782c24, to what it prints)
input results → {meta: {inputRoute: 1 | "2'" | 2 | 3 | 4 | "paste", startedAt: number, endedAt: number,
                        receivedAt: number, hidIdleMs: number | null, entered?: number, routeAdvice?: string, intervention?: "off",
                        activationGuardMs?: number, pointUnconfirmed?: true}}
                (every input method; the four times are Unix ms from the engine's clock; "2'" is route 2′, the plain post, the route of
                 every key press and typed text; 3 is every synthesized click; 2 appears only when a spike cell forces it; entered
                 is a type_text's UTF-16 units entered, on its result and on an error that stopped it; routeAdvice names the per-app
                 route-4 override after route 3's guard has failed three actions in a row for the app; intervention: "off" when the
                 Input Monitoring grant is missing and the tap is not running, so the stop, the refusal and the lock are not in force)
input errors  → the error below plus the same meta (startedAt/endedAt null when nothing was posted), for userIntervened in both its
                sentences (the pre-action "still interacting" refusal and the in-sequence "user changed"), concealedInputUnavailable,
                inputSourceChanged, noScreenshotForCoordinates and activationFailed (route 3's guard failed: the user's app lost
                frontmost or the 1 s poll timed out; the click is refused, nothing posted, never retried with ⌘); every error of an
                input method carries the meta, startedAt/endedAt
                null when nothing was posted (an unresolvable or forbidden app, or a forbidden owning process of the target element,
                refused at dispatch included)
type_text/paste/set_value args may carry concealed: true (set by src/services/sky.mjs on a substituted request);
  a concealed request that would need the paste route fails with errorName "concealedInputUnavailable" before input
error    {code: number (the vendor's codes where mapped; -10005 otherwise), message: string, errorName: string}
  engine-only errorNames the wrapper admits: concealedInputUnavailable, inputSourceChanged, noScreenshotForCoordinates, activationFailed (all value-free)
  engine-only errorNames the wrapper does not admit (no secret is substituted into a click): menuBarItemUnsupported (a click on a menu-bar
    item or on the menu bar's own node, refused before anything is posted until M2a's activate-and-record path; M1a.2; its sentence names the app's keyboard
    shortcut through pressKey as the alternative, M1a.2b); screenChangedSinceScreenshot (#296: a coordinate click or a drag's
    start whose point no longer holds what the screenshot showed there, refused with nothing posted in either device mode;
    its sentence names the element the screenshot showed and what is there now, and asks for a new screenshot)
  activationGuardMs: route 3's guard time, present only when a guard ran; activationClick: what the inert-point rule
    decided at the window's AXActivationPoint when an activation ran, "posted" there or the reason it was not ("noPoint",
    "outsideFrame", "spared", "control", "actionsUnknown", "hitTestFailed"); activationClickKind: the click that
    consumed that activation's arm and when (#187, 2026-11-01), {kind: "activationPoint", when: "early"} | {kind:
    "inertOutside", point: "activationPoint" | "offset", when: "early" | "release"} | {kind: "action", when:
    "consumedByAction"} | {kind: "skipped", skippedReason: "spared" | "noWindowNumber" | "noFrame"}, also on a holder
    that joined a hold and posted its kept inert click; pointUnconfirmed: true when the hit-test at a synthesized element
    point answered nothing or only a container (M1a.2); delivery: route 3's delivery guard after a pointer action's events
    or a paste's first event (#200; the paste route's since 2026-11-01), present when one ran: {restores, frontmostRestored, otherAppFront, restoreLatencyMs?, guardMs, screenHoldMs,
    capped, raiseLeft, holdStartedAt, releasedAt, postedAt, repairedAfterRelease, repairAfterReleaseLatencyMs?,
    personInput}; frontmostRestored and raiseLeft are what the guard's samples saw, not what its requests answered;
    past the hold's cap the guard repairs on until 1 s after the events unless the person gave input since them
    (repairedAfterRelease, personInput); holdStartedAt, releasedAt and postedAt are Unix ms on one clock, the hold's start
    (the activation's when the action ran it, else the guard's own) read as releasedAt less the hold's monotonic span, so
    a sitting can tell a raise before the hold (on the display) from one inside it, and postedAt the events' post;
    afterActionRepairs: on every input result and every observation of a connection once its after-action watch has
    repaired (#200: after each guard returns, a watch repairs until 1 s after the events under the guard's rules, a
    paste's while it runs and until 1 s after its last event, ended by the connection's next execute): {repairs, actions: [{postedAt, repairs, latencyMs}]}, the connection's total and
    the last 8 repaired actions, each keyed by its delivery's postedAt, latencyMs its first repair's from the events;
    each repair is also a `route3.afterAction.repair` signpost event
  paste: the paste route's value-free diagnostics of its last line (#187), on its result and every error after its first
    line began: {line, lines, notificationSource ("trusted" | "untrusted" | "none"), baselineEmpty, keyFocusAtPaste?
    {holds, frontmost, keyFocusProcess, focusedInWindow (null: unreadable)}, readWait? ("handedOver" | "released" |
    "timeout" | "deadline"), readWaitMs?, readAfterPostMs? (the first hand-over, signed, negative for a read before ⌘V),
    firstReadAfterPostMs? (the first hand-over at or after ⌘V, which the no-channel answer keys on), effectWait?
    ("notification" | "reading" | "timeout" | "deadline" | "noChannel"), effectWaitMs?, mainLagMs?, mainLagOpen?,
    stage? ("returnEffect" | "read" | "effect"), confirmed?, unconfirmedLines? (the operation's, when any), restore ("restored" |
    "skippedChanged" | "notTaken"), focusEnforcement {activated, point? ("inside" |
    "outside" | "unknownFrame" | "none"), click? (activationClickKind's shape), activationToCommandVMs?,
    clickToCommandVMs?} (what the paste's own activation did, Decision Log #187's inert click, 2026-11-01), perLine [{line, stage?, confirmed?, keyFocusAtPaste?,
    readWait?, readWaitMs?, readAfterPostMs?, firstReadAfterPostMs?, effectWait?, effectWaitMs?, effectAfterPostMs? (the
    effect wait's end from the line's ⌘V), baseline? and confirming? {range? [location, length], characters?},
    openingReturn? {settle? ("reading" | "notification" | "noChannel" | "timeout" | "deadline"), settleMs?, toCommandVMs?}}]
    (one per line reached, oldest first; the top-level fields are the last one's; Decision Log, #187's Return settle,
    2026-11-01)}; never the clipboard's contents or the field's text, the readings as numbers only
  elementClick: an element click's plan (#188), on its result and its errors: {plan ("action" | "synthesized"), action?
    (AXPick | AXPress), reason ("advertised" | "descendant" | "noAction" | "notPlainLeft" | "forced"), hit? ("element" |
    "descendant" | "ancestor" | "other" | "noAnswer"), hitTest? ("application")}, hit present when a hit-test was taken
  coordinateClick: a coordinate click's placement (#259), on its result and its errors once its target is made:
    {screenshotPoint {x, y} (the model's, in the screenshot's pixels), windowPoint {x, y} (the window-local point the
    conversion computed, in points), postedLocation {x, y} (the events' global location), postedWindowLocation {x, y} |
    null (the window location `stampWindow` stamps, the point less the frame's origin; null with no window number),
    windowFrame {x, y, width, height} (the AX frame the conversion used), frameBeforePost {x, y, width, height} | null
    (the window's frame read again just before the events; null when the click stopped before them or it was unread)}
  stalePoint: #296's stale-point guard at a coordinate click's point or a drag's start, on its result and its errors once the
    point is placed: {verdict ("kept" | "refused"), reason ("unrecorded" | "sameElement" | "noHit" | "nonInteractiveHit" |
    "hitAboveNode" | "unrecordedDescendant" | "recordedHit" | "nodeGone" | "frameMoved" | "interactiveHit" | "pressedOther"), identity? ("handle" | "recreated"), recordedRole?, hitRole?, recordedHitBy? ("hit" | "ancestor", #305: the frame that carried a
    `recordedHit`), ms, rechecked? (true: #282's or #283's
    route waited and the guard ran again before the press; the verdict is that check's, ms both checks'), afterActivation?
    (true: that check ran after the route's focus hold had activated the app)}; roles only, never a title
  note: on a paste or type_text result that pasted a line with no channel to see it, read after ⌘V (#187): the sentence
    the service adds to the tool result's content
  pasteUnconfirmed (engine-only, not admitted by the wrapper: a concealed request never pastes): ⌘V was sent and the text
    read from the clipboard, but no effect was seen, or none could be (Decision Log, #187); or, at stage "returnEffect", a
    Return between two of type_text's lines showed no effect, and the next line was not pasted (its own sentence)
  noScreenshotForCoordinates: a coordinate click, drag or scroll with no usable screenshot on the connection ("Screenshot"); nothing
    posted, the input meta carried with startedAt/endedAt null; the sentence names getScreenshot as the way to get an image to click
    in (c13759a4's "There is no screenshot of the app's window to place the coordinates in. Try to get the on-screen content again
    and see if that resolves the issue." names the tree instead and is revised in M1a)
```

**EngineClient** (`src/services/engine/client.mjs`, its framing and error classes in `src/services/engine/protocol.mjs`): `createEngineClient({nodeRepl, appPath})` → `connect() → {serverApiVersion, engineVersion, build}`, `execute(method, args, {deadlineMs = 120_000, lineage, suspend = true}) → {result, engineMs}`, `status()`; owns the one-in-flight chain, the version-and-build check, the launch-on-failure path through `nodeRepl.launchServices.openApplication` and `nodeRepl.nativePipe`, the `withSuspendedTimeout` wrapping (the connect with any launch wait, and every request but those passed `suspend: false`, the policy's `resolve_app` and `list_apps`), and the error classes.

**SkyshotRenderer** (`src/services/engine/render.mjs`): `render(skyshot) → string` in the vendor grammar; **TreeDiff** (`src/services/engine/diff.mjs`): `diff(previous, current) → string` with the vendor's headers; both pure, golden-tested under part 3's modulo-renumbering rule.

**Policy** (`src/services/engine/policy.mjs`): `createPolicy({nodeRepl, resolve})` → `withPolicy(toolName, input, fn)` as the vendor's `withComputerUsePolicy` contract (`R/A-contract.md` §2.6), asking on every call, `resolve(app)` the service's kept `resolve_app` and `fn(input with app = the resolved path, target)`; the forbidden list and risk tables as data at the top of the module, shared with the engine's copy by a generated Swift file committed under `native/engine/Sources/Engine/Policy.generated.swift` (so an edit to the list changes `build`) with a unit test that regenerates it and fails when the committed file differs from `policy.mjs`. **ApprovalMemory** (`cua serve`'s MCP connection, `src/mcp/`, from M3; `src/repl-host/supervisor.mjs` before): per MCP connection, surviving the runtime's idle stop, the set of `(connector_id, tool_params.app)` the client accepted; a repeat elicitation with the same key is answered `accept` from memory with `content.source: "computer-use-persisted-state"`, as the vendor host answered.

**EngineSelection** (`src/runtime/launch.mjs`, mirrored in `scripts/repl-parity/selection.mjs`): `nativeEngineFor({env, config, installed}) → {engine: 'vendor' | 'cua', reason, status}` and the engine × host table above, with the refusal codes `repl_host_refused`, `repl_host_node`, `engine_not_installed`, `invalid_setting`. **EngineAttestation**: `_meta['cua/nativeEngine'] = {engine, version, build}` on every `js` result. **InputRoute**: `_meta['cua/inputRoute']` on every input result, 1, `"2'"`, 2, 3, 4 or `"paste"` as the design defines, with `cua/serviceMs` beside it and `cua/routeAdvice` when route 3's guard has failed three actions in a row for the app; the per-app route-4 override is `routes: {<bundle id>: 4}` in `~/.config/cua/engine.json`, read by the engine at launch and reported by `doctor`; `cua/settle` rides on the `get_app_state` result instead, since the settle precedes the next observation.

**EngineInstall** (`src/runtime/engine.mjs`): `buildEngine({identity | 'adhoc', scratchPath}) → {bundlePath, engineVersion, build, signature}`, staged beside the approved location and moved into it after a successful `shutdown` of the engine it replaces, writing `pending.json {build, identity, builtAt}` (M1b, the `cua engine build [--sign adhoc]` command); `approveEngine()` → refuses while `status.connections > 0`, sends `approve` for the prompts, prints the by-hand steps (the pane, the "+" button, the bundle's path) for each grant that reads false and polls `status` until it reads true or the operator confirms it at the console, sends `shutdown`, relaunches through `open -n -g -a`, polls `status`, sends `approve` again for the capture (the owner answers the confirmation), then writes `approved.json {build, identity, approvedAt}` (M1b, the `cua engine approve` command); `engineStatus()` → the engine's `status` answer plus `approved.json`'s state (M1b, `cua engine status`); `stopEngine()`; `installedEngine()` → the approved bundle or null; and install's selection rule (M4).

**NativeMacScenes** (`scripts/spike/engine/scenes.mjs` and `scripts/spike/engine/fixtures/` in M0, `scripts/repl-parity/scenes-mac.mjs` and `scripts/native-matrix/fixtures/` from M1b): the four scenes with their fixed states, pairings, target lists and refusal preconditions, including how Chrome for Testing and the Electron fixture app are placed, started and stopped. **FrontWindowProbe** (`scripts/spike/engine/front-window.swift` in M0, `scripts/accept/front-window.swift` from M1b, used by `scripts/accept/key-guard.mjs`): `frontmost() → {bundleId, pid}` (`lsappinfo front` then `lsappinfo info -only pid,bundleid`) and `topWindow() → {ownerPid, bounds}` from `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`, a Swift script run with `swift`, reading no window names. **UserProbe** (M2a): `cua-engine --probe <command>`, run as a separate instance of the approved engine bundle through `open -n -g -W --stdout <file> --stderr <file> --args …` (`-g` so the probe never takes focus, which would fail the checks it serves; `-W` so the harness reads the output file only after the probe exits; the resident `watch-activations` runs without `-W` and prints its own pid first, so the harness can stop it and the oracles can exclude the probe instances), exempt from the live-socket exit rule since it binds nothing; commands: `keystroke --user-pid <n> --user-bounds <x,y,w,h> [--at <unix ms>] <text>` (posts from an HID-state event source at the HID event tap only while the front window is the given one, at the scheduled time when `--at` is given, and prints the post time), `input-source <id|current>`, `focus [--window <pid,x,y,w,h>]` (the system-wide focused application and focused window, by pid and bounds, through the Accessibility API, plus `valueHash`, the SHA-256 of the focused element's value, reported only when the focused window is the one named; it sets `AXManualAccessibility` on the named pid before reading and waits up to 1 s for the focused element to carry a value, as the engine waits for the `AXWebArea`, since a Chromium or Electron window exposes no value until an assistive client asks, and the fixture must not enable it itself, which would bypass the engine path the scene proves), `watch-activations` (resident until killed, printing every `NSWorkspace.didActivateApplicationNotification` with its time); `status.canPostHid` reports its ability. Input results carry `meta.startedAt` and `meta.endedAt`, surfaced as `cua/actionInterval`, so the matrix can place a post inside an action. **NativeMatrix** (`scripts/native-matrix.mjs`): `run`, `compare`, `summary`, `cell`.

**ModuleTree** (M6, `runtime/modules/package.json` and lockfile; `src/runtime/install.mjs`): the packages cells import and `classic-level`, installed under `$CUA_HOME/modules/<lock hash>/`, named to the own host as `CUA_REPL_NODE_MODULE_DIRS`.

Dependencies: Xcode 26's Swift 6 toolchain on each Mac; Node 24+ for the own host; Ghidra 12.1.4 with its natives built (a tool under `~/tools/ghidra/`, M0 only); the pinned `electron` dev dependency for the fixture app (M0, dev only) and the pinned Chrome for Testing download (a harness cache, never installed); the pinned vendor runtime until M6, for the vendor lanes.

## Surprises & Discoveries

- Observation: the pinned helper (bundle 26.924) keeps its exported, type and reflection symbols, but most of its function symbols are redacted placeholders; the helper inside the currently installed ChatGPT app (26.1013) is stripped further. B-helper's "not stripped" and the coordinating session's first reading of the `nm` counts overstated what is named.
  Evidence: `nm | wc -l` 161,617 on the pinned executable; `R/E-helper-catalogue.md`: 93,856 of 130,919 function symbols are `<redacted function N>`; the owner's measurement of the installed build, about 1.5k symbols (2026-10-18).
- Observation: Swift string literals of 15 bytes or fewer are instruction immediates, invisible to `strings`; a dedicated decoder recovered 13,171 of them, including most of the grammar words, short AX names and short bundle ids.
  Evidence: `~/.claude/jobs/1ea561bc/tmp/117/work/swift_smallstrings.py` and `S.small` (tier 1, 2026-10-18).
- Observation: the pinned helper imports no `CGEventPost*`, `CGEventCreateKeyboardEvent`, `CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString` or `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType`, carries no SkyLight names in its strings, and resolves symbols through a `SoftLink` module with `SipHashState` and `SymbolRequest{index, priority}` types; it does import `activateWithOptions:` and `activateIgnoringOtherApps:`.
  Evidence: `~/.claude/jobs/1ea561bc/tmp/117/work/S.undef` and `S.strings` (spec review round 1, 2026-10-18). Consequence: the vendor's input functions are named only by recovering the hash table (M0 tier 2), and the vendor's click path does activate.
- Observation: the Ghidra 12.1.4 release zip ships decompiler natives for `linux_x86_64` and `win_x86_64` only; `support/buildNatives` no longer exists, the documented route is `cd support/gradle && gradle buildNatives`, and the natives land under `build/os/mac_arm_64/`.
  Evidence: `~/tools/ghidra-projects/analyze-import.log`: `ERROR os/mac_arm_64/decompile does not exist`; `build-natives.log`: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 6s` with Gradle 9.8.1.
- Observation: each new on-disk copy of a capturing helper raises a one-time screen-capture confirmation only the owner can answer, which stops the mini's CI runner, even with an identical designated requirement.
  Evidence: `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-17-own-sandbox-design.md`, Surprises (the vendor helper copy, 2026-10-17); `scripts/native-baseline/prepare.mjs`'s header. Consequence: one approved engine per Mac.
- Observation: the spike engine's first approval on the MacBook (2026-10-18, 21:49–21:55) ran as the design expects: the Accessibility grant was visible to the running process at once, the Screen Recording grant never read true in that process and read true only after the relaunch (System Settings' own "Quit & Reopen", taken by the owner), and the relaunched engine's one display capture raised the per-bundle capture confirmation, which the owner answered; `captured: true` followed.
  Evidence: the spike executor's status log (pid 72077 → 28197 at 21:54:30) and the owner's confirmation that the dialog appeared ("떴음"). Consequence: probe (f)'s first data point; both relaunch flows stay independent of a running process observing its grant. Both grants then survived a later relaunch through `open -n -g -a` (the engine had exited between calls, cause not yet known; relaunched as pid 82960).
- Observation (M0 spike, MacBook development run, not the mini's measurement): the public per-element fetch is slower than the vendor's whole in-cell figure. On a harness-launched TextEdit document (46–47 nodes, launched with `open -g`, which left the frontmost application unchanged) the engine-side tree read took 136–138 ms (walk 72–96 ms, of which attribute fetches 45–68 ms and action names 13–20 ms), against the vendor's 51 ms in-cell `getAXState` on the mini; a screenshot added 134 ms of capture (60 ms of it `SCShareableContent`). Release build, MacBook, one connection per call.
  Evidence: the controller's `get_app_state` exchanges on the run directory's scene document (spike report). Consequence: the private hierarchy call priced in probe (c′) and caching the shareable content are the first tuning iterations, before the mini's sitting.

- Observation (M0 spike, MacBook, 2026-10-18, probe (f) and the first tuning iteration): a staged rebuild into the approved location (stop, stage, move aside, move into place; new `build` b5b659bf…, same identity and path) kept both grants as the relaunched engine's `status` reads them, and its first window capture succeeded (a 610×860 JPEG of the run's document written by pid 98369) with no capture confirmation raised (the owner watched the console and saw none): on this Mac a staged rebuild at the same path and identity re-raises neither grant nor the confirmation. Whether a copy at a new path re-raises the confirmation stays open for the mini sitting. On the rebuilt engine, a harness-launched TextEdit document (47 nodes plus 8 menu-bar items) observed with a screenshot took 109–218 ms engine-side on the public per-element route and 149–231 ms on the private `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` route (its prefetch 42–89 ms, attribute reads after it 3–6 ms), with the window capture at 22–51 ms once `SCShareableContent` is cached (156 ms on the cold first call), and app resolution alone 30–39 ms per call. The MacBook was loaded by concurrent builds, so the spread is wide; the direction is that the hierarchy call does not pay on a small AppKit tree, and resolution is the next cost to cut.
  Evidence: the controller's `get_app_state` exchanges with `prefetch: public|hierarchy`, five each, against build b5b659bf… (spike report). Consequence: the mini sitting prices both routes on Finder-200 and the Chrome page, where the hierarchy call's batching should matter, and resolution is cached before the sitting.

- Observation (M0 spike, 2026-10-18): the input half could not be written by agents. A model-side safety classifier withheld the output of every agent asked to write the engine's input posting, its fake activation and its listen-only tap (three workers and the dispatching session), and once withheld a worker whose context held that discussion while it was writing the observation fixes; the observation path, written by a fresh worker with no input content in its context, went through.
  Evidence: the spike executors' BLOCKED returns (spike report); the dispatching session's own attempt. Consequence: the owner writes the input half by hand (Decision Log, scope without input).

- Observation (M0 spike, MacBook shakedown 2026-10-19, cua lane only, one run, five samples, no input; direction, not the mini's verdict): the whole harness ran end to end on the four scenes (bind, goldens, the per-scene tree and screenshot series, both fetch routes, the switches, settle at rest, probe (c)) with every input cell reported not run. In-cell medians: TextEdit `getAXState` 156 ms, `getScreenshot` 126 ms (the vendor's mini figures are 51 and 53 ms); per-scene tree reads TextEdit 118, Finder-200 474, Chrome page 264, Electron 205 ms. The public and hierarchy routes priced alike on TextEdit (101 / 105), Chrome (274 / 291) and Electron (210 / 210), and the hierarchy call was slower on Finder-200 (613 against 523). The per-observation web-area wait costs about 145 ms on each Chromium-shaped scene (Chrome 245 against 100, Electron 207 against 59 with it off). Settle at rest took 460–800 ms. Probe (c): plain `open -a` and two copies at two paths behaved as the rows record (both ok). Finder's list view held; Finder keeps an off-screen document-sized window of its own, which the refusal now ignores, and `up finder` first recorded that window as the run's (fixed, 7b5c0a50).
  Evidence: `/tmp/cua-spike-ctl/macbook-run3/run-1/results.jsonl` on the MacBook (spike report). Consequence: the speed half is the fork the promote rule names; the first tuning moves before the mini are the per-observation web-area wait, app resolution, and the settle's cost at rest; the hierarchy call does not pay on these trees.

- Observation (M0 spike tuning, 2026-10-19, from the MacBook shakedown's rows): app resolution is not the cost it looked like. The engine's own `resolveMs` was 0.2–0.6 ms in all 150 shakedown rows (the 30–39 ms of the earlier entry came from an older engine on a loaded machine), and `resolve_app` over the socket 1.1–1.7 ms; the in-cell time above the engine's `totalMs` is 17–53 ms per call, which is the service's two relay round trips through the own host (the pre-flight's per-call `resolve_app`, then `get_app_state`) plus the stand-in render. Settle at rest reaches the vendor's quiet point (300 ms native, 250 ms web) with no notification at rest and 3–12 ms of observer cost; its excess over the vendor's ~300 ms is tree-read cost, the full busy walk plus the per-element actions, settable, focusable and selectable reads (Finder-200: 77 + 17 + 49 + 17 ms), which the reuse does not cover.
  Evidence: `task-1-fix-report.md` "Tuning round" over `/tmp/cua-spike-ctl/macbook-run3/run-1/results.jsonl`; engine a97beb4f (1ca14d9c) adds `settleQuietAtMs` and `settleObserverMs` to show the split live. Consequence: the levers are the per-element read set and the service's extra round trip, both design questions sent to the dispatching session.

- Observation (M0 spike, MacBook window 2026-10-19, local vendor lane on a scratch home, one run; direction, the mini's sitting is the measurement): the same-machine vendor baseline. In-cell medians: TextEdit `getAXState` 78 ms, `getScreenshot` 84 ms, `task.total` 4,360 ms (10 of 10 sent); per-scene trees TextEdit 94, Chrome 84, Electron 75, Finder-200 669 ms (14 valid samples); screenshots Chrome 81, Electron 77 ms; the four fixed-state goldens recorded. Against the cua lane's shakedown on the same Mac before tuning (TextEdit 156 / 126, trees 118 / 264 / 205, Finder 474 ms), cua was faster on Finder-200 and slower everywhere else. The scratch home's new vendor-helper copy raised the capture confirmation once (owner allowed), as probe (f) predicts for a copy at a new path. Taints: the owner quit Finder at 00:26:46 local mid-run, so Finder's tree samples 15–30, its screenshot and its action golden are void (the front-window guard had marked the tree rows invalid), and the Chrome and Electron action goldens failed because their targets were not in the vendor's tree at all: the vendor exposed no web content on either Chromium scene (every one of 30 samples identical, 45 elements on Chrome and 12 on Electron, the page containers present but empty, over more than 70 s after binding, while the page had loaded: its title on the tab, Chrome's offer to translate it), where cua's engine on the same Mac and the same launchers exposed both pages' controls (its shakedown goldens). Under part 5 that is a finding-elements result for these two scenes; its cause (a bundle-id gate in the vendor's enablement, or an attribute this Chromium 155 / Electron 44 build does not honour) is unknown, so it says nothing yet about the vendor on the owner's Chrome, and the mini sitting confirms it with the three-read wait. The cua lane and the input probes did not run on the MacBook: the owner's terminal took the front repeatedly, and the window was ended (the owner's choice).
  Evidence: `/tmp/cua-spike-ctl/window3/vendor/run-1/` (results.jsonl, meta.json, goldens, taint.json) and `window4` on the MacBook (spike report). Consequence, generalized: the Mac running a sitting must have no app at its console that activates on terminal activity, including whatever hosts the coordinating session, since the harness's guarded key cells and front-window checks refuse whenever another app takes the front; the mini, driven over SSH with Terminal.app at its console and no such app, meets it.

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19, first series: build 2f0263b6, vendor and cua triples on the four scenes, 90 samples per row): the cua lane was slower than the vendor on the promote rule's speed rows. In-cell medians, vendor against cua: TextEdit `getAXState` 56.0 / 98.2 ms, `getScreenshot` 55.3 / 99.8, `listApps` 34.9 / 2.7; per-scene tree reads TextEdit 62.9 / 120.5, Finder-200 458.8 / 293.1 (cua faster), Electron 57.4 / 86.7. The engine's TextEdit time was the walk (55.9 ms: attributes 36.2, actions 6.6) followed by the window capture (49.7: a new `SCContentFilter` and `SCScreenshotManager.captureImage` per call, the shareable content already cached), in sequence; 7 ms sat above the engine. The hierarchy route was slower than the public one on every scene (Finder 340.7 / 267.7). Settle at rest took 267–456 ms with no notification. The reduced read set rendered text equal to the full set on TextEdit, Finder and Electron and saved nothing; Chrome differed by one line. Probe (c): a plain `open -a` did not activate the engine, and two copies at two paths each bound their own socket. Probe (f): a copy at a new path (same build, same identity) came up with both grants and captured with no confirmation; a running engine reads its own grants. Approve: the engine's requests raised no Accessibility or Screen Recording prompt on the mini (the owner added the bundle under Screen & System Audio Recording by hand; Accessibility already read true), then the capture confirmation appeared once and the owner allowed it.
  Evidence: `docs/doperpowers/research/2026-10-18-own-native-engine/spike/{approve,vendor,cua,probe-f,summary.md}`. Consequence: the in-sitting tuning iterations the promote rule allows (Decision Log).

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19): every input action that reached the engine's key-focus check crashed the engine (27 crash reports, all SIGSEGV in SkyLight's `SLPSGetKeyFocusProcess`, called from `ActivationSystem.keyFocus` ← `appBelievesItHasFocus` ← `click`, `pressKey` or `typeText`). The cause was the declaration, not the thread: `CPSGetKeyFocusProcess` takes two arguments (the process serial number out, and a Boolean out), and the engine declared one; with one it returns 1003 in a process with no window-server connection and faults in one that has it, from the main thread too. Input that did not reach the check ran: the TextEdit task cells 30/30, the action goldens on TextEdit and Electron, probe (a) and (b) on Electron, probe (d)'s no-fire case, Latin typing. The listen-only tap could not be created without Input Monitoring, and the engine raised no prompt for it. *(The tap rows were later found uninformative either way; the records-reading entry, (4).)*
  Evidence: `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/cua-engine-*.ips` on the mini (top frames read for all 27); the dispatching session's fix f62f3cc7 with an XCTest reading the real key focus from the main and a background thread (crashes before, passes after). Consequence: a private function's arity is part of what F must pin; the engine's SPI declarations are tested by a call, not only by `dlsym`.

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19, tuning iteration 1, build 503399e2: the window capture on its own thread beside the tree walk, joined after it, with the key-focus fix; TextEdit only, a fresh vendor triple and a cua triple of the same sitting, 90 samples per row): TextEdit in-cell medians, vendor against cua, `getAXState` 51.3 / 42.2 ms and `getScreenshot` 49.3 / 40.6, `listApps` 26.2 / 2.2. The engine's total fell from 113.3 to 48.7 ms: walk 43.0, capture 41.0, join wait 2.6, so the total is the longer of the two plus the join. The walk's own phases read lower than in the first series (attributes 28.7 against 36.2, actions 5.1 against 6.6) although the change does not touch them, and the vendor read lower too (51.3 against 56.0), so the session-to-session drift is the machine's; the comparison that counts is within the sitting. The render was unchanged (5088 characters, 186 lines, 61 elements in every sample), and three captures viewed after the series were real, fresh images of the scene's document; the first capture of a fresh engine waited 21.7 ms in the join (the shareable content cold), later ones none. Input with the fix, on TextEdit and Finder with the other as the user window: probe (a) click and key left the frontmost application and the top window unchanged on both; probe (b): both actions took route 3 with a 38–52 ms activation guard and the device-side watcher recorded no activation; forced key routes 2 and 3 succeeded; forced click routes 1–3 returned an error result on both apps (the harness's forced-click cell was a syntax error, fixed in 7e3e2a1e; the forced click rows await a live run); the TextEdit task cells 10/10, the action goldens on TextEdit and Finder, probe (d)'s no-fire case; no crash. Under the Korean source `type_text` still refuses (the paste route is not built), and the tap still needs Input Monitoring.
  Evidence: `docs/doperpowers/research/2026-10-18-own-native-engine/spike/iter1/` (approve, vendor, cua, summary.md) and `spike/iter1-input/`. Consequence: the speed half passes on TextEdit in the sitting; the input half's background rows pass on TextEdit and Finder.

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19): the vendor's screenshots are at the display's pixel size and cua's at points. On the mini's 2× display the vendor returned 1220 × 1720 for the TextEdit scene window (1840 × 872 Finder, 1440 × 1120 Electron) and cua 610 × 860 (920 × 436, 720 × 560), so cua encodes a quarter of the pixels and the model sees a smaller image. The Screenshot paragraph's "scaled to points (the baseline's 1172 × 976 for a window on a 2× display)" presents points as the vendor's size, which this sitting does not show.
  Evidence: the `warm.getScreenshot` rows' width and height in `spike/vendor` and `spike/cua` (and `spike/iter1`). Consequence: the `getScreenshot` speed rows of the first series and iteration 1 do not compare like for like; ruled the same day: match the vendor (Decision Log).

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19, tuning iteration 2, build e3547c25: the screenshot at the display's pixel size; TextEdit only, a fresh triple per lane, 90 samples per row): with the images now equal (1220 × 1720 on both lanes, median 141,865 bytes vendor against 142,085 cua), the TextEdit in-cell medians, vendor against cua, are `getAXState` 50.7 / 43.1 ms (15% below), `getScreenshot` 53.4 / 45.8 (14% below), `listApps` 24.7 / 2.3, and the three-cell task 4,028 / 2,755 ms (30/30 each). The pixel-size capture costs the engine little: capture 42.7 ms (41.0 at points), encode 7.2 (2.1), join wait 8.1, total 54.6. The render was unchanged. The coordinate-click mapping is unit-tested only; the harness's clicks are element clicks. Engine exits over the whole sitting, read from the unified log (`log show --predicate 'subsystem == "io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine"'` works unprivileged): 85 starts, 28 logged exits (27 shutdown requests, one quit request at approve's relaunch), 56 unlogged inside the two input crash windows (27 crash reports; the rest consistent with ReportCrash's throttling), and one start (pid 45110, 14:49:47Z, the cua triple's start) with neither an exit line nor a crash report *(later found: a SIGSEGV at 14:56:25Z with a crash report whose file mtime postdates its capture, one of the 27; U11, resolved)*.
  Evidence: `docs/doperpowers/research/2026-10-18-own-native-engine/spike/iter2/` (approve, vendor, cua, summary.md); the mini's unified log. Consequence: on TextEdit the speed half meets the promote rule like for like and the part-5 beat of 10%; Finder, Chrome and Electron were not re-measured after the iterations, and the input half's promote rows (click and pressKey to TextEdit and Finder, frontmost unchanged) pass on iteration 1's build.

- Observation (M0 spike, mini sitting 2026-10-19, after iteration 2: the forced routes live, probe (d)(i), probe (f)'s f2): with the harness's forced-click cell fixed (7e3e2a1e), a cua-lane pass on TextEdit and Finder (build e3547c25) carried a background click by route 2 and by route 3 on both apps and a background key by routes 2 and 3 on both, every one with the frontmost application and the top window unchanged and no activation seen by the watcher; route 1 refused on both apps' probe targets ("the element advertises neither AXPick nor AXPress"), which is the route's precondition, not a failure of delivery. The natural route was 3 for every action. With Input Monitoring granted by the owner and the engine launched fresh, the listen-only tap still could not be created (`probe.d.tap` and `probe.d.hidReset`: "the tap could not be created"); not troubleshot in the hold. *(Those rows were later found uninformative either way; the records-reading entry, (4).)* Probe (f), f2: each of the two staged rebuilds at the approved path (503399e2, e3547c25, same identity) raised the capture confirmation at the console once, and the owner allowed both; `approve` read `captured: true` because the answer came in time. This differs from the MacBook, where staged rebuilds re-raised nothing, and from f3 on the mini, where a copy at a new path raised nothing; the asymmetry (a new path silent, the same path prompting) is unexplained and worth one controlled re-check in a later sitting.
  Evidence: `docs/doperpowers/research/2026-10-18-own-native-engine/spike/iter2-input/` and `spike/iter2-d1/`; the owner's report through the dispatching session ("두 번, 둘 다 허용"). Consequence: every engine update on a Mac needs the owner present to answer the capture confirmation, as the approve step already assumes; install (M4) and `buildEngine` (M1b) plan for it rather than for a silent rebuild. The intervention mechanism's choice between the tap and the HID-idle fallback stays open for the next input sitting.

- Observation (2026-10-19, reading the sitting's records for the design revision): six places where the running records say more, or other, than the outputs.
  (1) The Chrome refusals were the harness's, on both lanes: in the first series `tree.getAXState` on the Chrome scene was invalid, "the scene window was not the app's front window", for 90 of 90 samples on the vendor lane as on cua's, and both goldens and `warm.getScreenshot` refused on both; the check compares the rendered header's window title with the scene's page title (`cells.mjs`, `__front`), so what differs on the mini is the title the scene window carries, not either engine; and since no Chrome tree was accepted on either lane, the MacBook finding that the vendor exposes no web content on the Chromium scenes is unconfirmed on the mini, not confirmed as the MacBook entry expected.
  (2) Electron's background key was never delivered: in all three first-series runs `probe.a.key` on Electron failed with "cua engine connection closed before response" (the key-focus crash), and the background click went by route 1 (the fixture's button advertises `AXPress`), so the PROMOTE entry's "Electron took background click and key in the first series" overstates it.
  (3) `probe.d.noFire` is vacuous: the spike engine builds no intervention path (its tap is the counting probe `probe_tap`, which feeds no rule), so nothing could have fired.
  (4) `probe.d.tap`'s and `probe.d.hidReset`'s rows are uninformative either way: the harness's `ops.probeTap('start')` returned the rpc envelope whole, a refusal as `{ok: false, error}` and a success as `{ok: true, result}`, so `tap.created` was undefined whichever the engine answered and the `else if (tap)` branch wrote "the tap could not be created" with `listenAccess: null` in both cases; a tap the engine created would have produced exactly the recorded row and been left running, the second start then answering `tapBusy`. The engine's sentence, which names the listen preflight's state, is in no record, and whether the tap was ever created is unknown (first read as a discarded refusal; corrected 2026-10-19 from the sitting review's fix wave, b3e3e9ee).
  (5) Probe (d)'s operator steps never ran: every pass's `meta.json` records `operator: false`, and the rows say "operator steps not requested", although the command line the spike report prepared carries `--operator`.
  (6) The first series' `approve.json` reads both grants false at the engine's first request, while the report says Accessibility "already read true" when the owner looked; the record does not say whether the owner toggled it, so the Accessibility prompt's fate on the mini is as unknown as Screen Recording's.
  Evidence: `spike/vendor/run-*/results.jsonl` and `spike/cua/run-*/results.jsonl` (the Chrome rows, the Electron probe rows), `scripts/spike/engine/measure/cells.mjs` and `measure/lane.mjs` (the title check, probe (d)), `native/engine/Sources/Engine/Tap.swift`, `spike/*/meta.json`, `spike/approve/approve.json`. Consequence: the revised design cites what the outputs show, and the delegated unknowns of the revision entry carry the gaps.

- Observation (the second mini input sitting, 2026-10-19 19:17–20:42Z, build b99ea967, 27 engine starts and 27 shutdown exits, no crash): five things the first sitting could not show. (1) A plain pid-post with no ⌘ and no fake activation (route 2′) delivers keys to every scene but clicks only to Finder and Electron; a ⌘-click delivers to Chrome and Electron, not to TextEdit, and adds the clicked row to Finder's selection; the fake activation delivers clicks everywhere, at 37–58 ms of guard. (2) The fake activation's click lands at the window's `AXActivationPoint`, which is 16 points below the top edge for TextEdit, Finder and Electron and the screen's bottom-left corner, (−1, 1441), for Chrome, where it hits the menu bar; the Electron fixture counts one became-active, one resigned-active and one focus and blur per window, page and text field for each route-3 action and none for a plain post; a `selectText` selection survives the activation. (3) Both tap kinds are created under Accessibility alone and receive nothing; with Input Monitoring both receive the engine's own posts (its source pid, 10,009 of 10,009 during a 10 s `type_text`) and the user's (6 of 6 keystroke events, 1 of 1 click, inside the action's interval); the engine's own posts reset the machine-wide HID idle counter. (4) The engine's Accessibility request is denied by `universalAccessAuthWarn` within about 40 ms with no window; a fresh bundle id's first capture raised replayd's `SCAlert` user-acknowledgement alert, where the owner reports Allow and replayd "refused"; three engine moves (a same-path replacement, a staged same-path rebuild, a new-path copy) raised no confirmation and kept the grants. (5) The first sitting's Chrome refusals were a stray `(en-US)` argument Chrome opened as a tab; with it gone, two coordinate clicks on the 2× display landed exactly on their lines, and the four-scene triple put cua ahead on Finder and Chrome and behind on TextEdit and Electron by the 55–58 ms capture floor.
  Evidence: `spike/sit2/` (`sitting-notes.md`, `u12u13`, `u3u4`, `u2`, `u5-ax-only`, `u5-input-monitoring`, `u6`, `u7`, `u8`, `u9/summary.md`, `u78-tcc.jsonl`, `engine-exits.txt`). Consequence: M2a's route table, the owner's Input Monitoring decision, the install rule without an attended update, and M1a next (Decision Log, "second input sitting").

- Observation (M1a.1, 2026-10-19): the M0 engine's re-fetch never updated an element's owning pid, so after a panel service restarted, anything reading the stored pid would have addressed the dead process (M0's clicks read the live owner and were not hurt). Fixed with the re-fetch. The spike's service and `measure` still send `element_index`, `prefetch` and `readSet`, which this engine refuses or ignores (pre-flight item 2); their tests use fake engines.
  Evidence: `native/engine/Tests/EngineTests/LineageTests.swift` (the pid update), the M1a.1 report.

- Observation (M1a.3, 2026-10-19): rendered by cua's renderer, the second sitting's cua-lane trees add **0 lines** against the vendor's text on every scene (TextEdit, Finder-200, Chrome, Electron), where the spec expected Chrome and Electron above part 3's 10% cap: on the mini the vendor exposed both Chromium pages fully. The same goldens refute four of the engine's derivations (`disclosable`, `settable`, the value type, the window's document URL) and show attributes it does not report (Chrome's header title, Electron's focus, Finder's name field's selection, the system's action descriptions, the menu-bar node); each is declared as a correction to the recorded tree until M1b re-records the goldens, and M1a.2b fixes the engine from the vendor's code.
  Evidence: `test/native-goldens/` (`expected.json`, `PROVENANCE.md`), the M1a.3 report and its review.

- Observation (M1a.4, 2026-10-19): the own host never sent `mode: "form"` on its elicitations while the vendor host does, an undeclared #116 parity gap the parity harness could not see (it does not compare request params); fixed in M1a.4. And the approved engine's directory, `~/Library/Application Support/cua/engine/`, lies inside the default `CUA_HOME` (`~/Library/Application Support/cua`, `src/runtime/layout.mjs`), so "outside every `CUA_HOME`" ("Install, signing and permissions") holds for non-default homes only; the property that matters, one fixed location that no `CUA_HOME` moves, holds, and the default home's layout uses no `engine` entry.
  Evidence: the M1a.4 report; `docs/evidence/2026-10-13-own-browser-service-parity/m5/maws/maws-live-vendor.json`.

- Observation (M1a.2b, 2026-10-19): five things only M1b's live recording decides, each declared in `test/native-goldens/expected.json` with its cause. (1) Electron's `focused` is null in every cua capture although the recordings' AXFocused flags sit on the text area, then the clicked button: either the app-level read answered nothing (hypothesis: the lanes' activation histories differ, the vendor's clicks activating the app, #162) or it answered an element outside the walk (an engine gap: the vendor's `focusTree` 0x100755388 adds that branch); `timings.focusRead` tells which. (2) Finder's name field reads selected only in the vendor lane; the predicate is the same on both sides, hypothesis: Finder reports it selected only while its selection is emphasized with Finder active. (3) The vendor's `menu bar` word means its bar read got no role description (`FUN_1006156c8` prints the word only for a nil one); hypothesis: the listed-attributes read does not return AXRoleDescription for the bar. (4) The header title matches Chrome's only under Screen Recording. (5) The vendor's settable has a value-length rule cua lacks: behind the role gate `FUN_10077dfdc`, a string value over 100,000 characters is cut and `isValueSettable` cleared (`FUN_10074cb90`), where cua caps every value at 10,000 UTF-16 units (`AX.swift`) and keeps settable, so an over-long value differs in its text and its flag (tracked; no scene reaches it). And `filterActions`' predicate, read now, decided two Finder differences: its scroll areas print `Scroll Up, Scroll Down` because they have a vertical bar only, and its name fields drop `Confirm` as text fields.
  Evidence: the M1a.2b report and its reviews; `test/native-goldens/expected.json`.

- Observation (maws-3f, 2026-10-19T21:03Z, the mini, outside a hold): a one-shot launchd job bootstrapped from SSH into `gui/501` (`launchctl managername` answered `Aqua`) ran `codesign` with the configured identity on a throwaway file. securityd logged `displaying keychain prompt for /usr/bin/codesign(<pid>)` with the access list's `KeychainPromptAclSubject(desc:privateKey)`: the keychain was unlocked there, but the private key's access list does not trust `codesign`, so the partition list set in the second sitting's phase 0 is not enough on its own. The job was booted out after 30 s with `codesign` killed; the SecurityAgent prompt (434×182, layer 1000) stayed on the console with no requester until its process was killed with the owner's permission (it ignores SIGTERM). So a key prompt outlives its requester, and the first GUI-domain sign on a Mac belongs inside a hold with the owner at the console (Decision Log, M1b's signing route).
  Evidence: the mini's unified log (securityd `kcacl`, SecurityAgent), 2026-10-19 21:03–21:08Z.

- Observation (M1a.5, 2026-10-20): GitHub's hosted macOS runner grants the engine Accessibility: the real-engine untrusted-path test skipped there with "this runner grants the engine Accessibility" (run 39348630686), so the spec's assumption that the hosted runner has no grants was wrong. Whether it grants Screen Recording too is untested; if so, live observation of a harness-launched app could run on hosted CI instead of the mini.
  Evidence: the run's `test` log; `test/services-engine-real.test.mjs`.

- Observation (M1b.5 phase 1, 2026-10-20 22:10–22:16Z, the mini): **the GUI-domain sign job killed `codesign` at a fixed 60 s** while the owner's key prompt was up. `cua engine build --sign-wait 600000` failed `engine_sign_failed` with `spawnSync /usr/bin/codesign ETIMEDOUT`: the job ran `codesign` through `engine-sign.mjs`'s default `spawnSync` bound (60 s), and `--sign-wait`/`CUA_SIGN_WAIT_MS` lengthened only the build's outer wait. SecurityAgent's prompt outlived its killed requester (orphaned); the owner cancelled it. The owner's **Always Allow** went instead to a one-shot key-trust job in `gui/501` that ran `codesign` with no bound (bootstrapped 22:15:07Z, exited 0 at 22:15:28Z on the answer; a re-run exited 0 in about 2 s with no prompt), after which `cua engine build --sign-wait 600000` signed silently (22:15:42Z, build ef32ba38c943). A first-time user would hit the same kill. Fixed in 1e4a5425: the job bounds `codesign` by the sign wait less a margin (`jobCodesignMs`), and a `codesign` killed at that bound reads `engine_sign_prompt_waiting`.
  Evidence: the build's output and SecurityAgent's window list on the mini, 22:10–22:16Z (the ledger); `src/runtime/engine-sign.mjs` (`jobCodesignMs`), `test/runtime-engine-sign.test.mjs`.

- Observation (M1b.5 phase 1, 2026-10-20 22:15:49Z and 22:22:06Z, the mini): **the capture watch counted replayd's `SCAlertStatsManager` bookkeeping as a confirmation.** Both approves reported "replayd shows a screen capture confirmation" with nothing on the console: the owner saw no capture confirmation and no notification banner on the mini's screen during approve (asked by maws-3f, answered 2026-10-20), no SecurityAgent or alert window was on the window list, and replayd's log held no `+[SCAlert userAcknowledgementAlertWithBundleID:]` or `showAlertWithTitle:` line (a real confirmation's lines, `spike/sit2/sitting-notes.md`). What matched `eventMessage CONTAINS "SCAlert"` were four lines replayd logs at every capture stream's start, `-[SCAlertStatsManager approveOnStreamStartWithCanonicalBundleID:auditToken:streamID:methodType:]`, `requireAlertWithCanonicalBundleID:auditToken:`, `updateClientWithIdentifier:lastUsedDate:lastAlertedDate:` and `requirePrivacyHintWithCanonicalBundleID:auditToken:`, once per approve's capture (eight per approve). The second sitting's "replayd logged no `SCAlert` line" for its U8 moves probably came from a narrower search than the predicate built from it (an inference, unchecked: those moves captured, and these lines come with every capture stream). The second approve was confirmed at the FIFO-fed terminal after that check and wrote `approved.json` (22:22:24Z); the grants carried over (all three true). Fixed: the watch matches `[SCAlert ` (the SCAlert class's own lines), not the substring. An earlier `log show` over SSH found no replayd line at all because zsh's builtin `log` shadows `/usr/bin/log`; use the full path.
  Evidence: `/usr/bin/log show --predicate 'process == "replayd" AND eventMessage CONTAINS "SCAlert"'` on the mini over both approves; `src/runtime/engine.mjs` (`CAPTURE_ALERT_PREDICATE`), `test/runtime-engine.test.mjs`.

- Observation (MAWS P4-B2, 2026-10-20 12:00Z, the mini, reported to maws-3f by the MAWS architect): **the vendor's key presses bypass the input method.** Korean 2-Set was selected through TIS in the Aqua session and confirmed before and after. Through the relay, the vendor helper's `pressKey` r then k typed `rk`. The page saw plain keydowns (keyCodes 82 and 75), keypress and insertText, with no keyCode 229 and no composition. So the vendor's `CGEventPostToPid` keys from an HID-system source reach the app without Hangul composition. Whether that is because the helper sets each event's characters or because the post skips TSM routing was not read. Consequence: M2b's Korean matrix includes `press_key` under 2-Set with this as the vendor's answer, and route 2′ must match it, or declare the difference.
  Evidence: MAWS branch `worktree-agent-a0ef27fba0a0f4b08` (bf8a1466a), `spikes/p4-click-model/hold-2026-10-20/L0.result.json`; MAWS's tracker row "cua #117 (the relay's keys and the input method…)".

- Observation (M1b.5 sitting, 2026-10-21 00:02–01:34Z, the mini, macOS 27): **comparison A could not bind TextEdit on cua's engine until the engine reopened windowless apps as the vendor does.** Launched in the background, TextEdit opens no window until it gets a reopen request; the vendor's preparation sends one (Decision Log "M1b.5 sitting" item 1), cua's did not, so every cua-engine run failed its bind with `noWindowsAvailable` after a `timeoutReached` first call that the vendor's runs share. Two direct probes at 00:26Z got a window anyway, a race left unexplained by ruling. The fix's review took four rounds, the last three on deadline boundaries of the new preparation; the comparison's verdict rests on the fourth, reviewed build.
  Evidence: `m1b/comparison-a*/` (the runs' `results.jsonl` rows `bind.getApp` and `bind.getApp.retry`), the vendor's preparation read from its decompile (the addresses in the Decision Log), the ledger.

- Observation (M1b.5 sitting, the goldens): **two scene states are not fixed on the mini.** (1) Finder: macOS 27 shows the status bar while `ShowStatusBar` is unset, and the scene's table assumes macOS 26's hidden default, so no ⌘/ is sent and the fixed-state check finds the status bar's text ("200 items, … available") as unowned text. The fix is an OS-dependent unset default carried through the save and restore path, tested for 26 and 27. (2) Chrome for Testing: the scene's `up` returns before the page's accessibility tree has filled (44 nodes at once, 83 five seconds later, the page heading's level appearing as `Value: 1`), so the first-launch fixed state and a reset's differ. The harness fix is to wait for the page's load (CDP's load event) in `up`; whether `get_app_state` should settle on a filling web tree is M2a's settle question, against the vendor's UI-settle wait (`0x1006f7408`). Earlier in the sitting the Chrome restart came up with the AI-mode omnibox placeholder (fixed by a fresh profile per launch) and Finder's unset keys were not recognised in macOS 27's wording (fixed).
  Evidence: `m1b/goldens-run/meta.json`, `m1b/goldens-run-incomplete-f6d713d2/meta.json`, the ledger.

- Observation (M1b.5 sitting, the ways in): over SSH on the mini zsh's builtin `log` shadows `/usr/bin/log` (the 22:15Z "no replayd lines" was that); a background Chrome ignores a posted ⌘Q while its "Warn before quitting" is on, so the owner's Chrome was quit from its own app menu (`terminate:`); and another session's headless Playwright Chrome appeared on the mini for a minute inside the hold (00:29Z), which is why every run's start now records the on-screen windows.
  Evidence: the ledger.

- Observation (M2a.5 hold, menus): **a background app works out its menu items' enablement before an activation that comes only at the press.** In TextEdit in the background, the vendor's Select All and Bold read enabled and acted, because the vendor's fake activation is held from the menu-bar record. cua's read disabled (Bold was not exposed), and route 3's activation around the press changed nothing. Evidence: `m2a/live/menu/result.json`. Consequence: the Decision Log entry "menu activation held from the record".
- Observation (M2a.5 hold, minimized): **a minimized window takes AX writes but not posted keys.** set_value landed. type_text by route 2′ reported every unit entered, yet the value did not change, and ⌘A had no effect. The engine's own `entered` count cannot tell a delivered key from a dropped one. Consequence: keys and paste into a minimized window are refused.
- Observation (M2a.5 hold, enablement): **Chromium's enablement attribute read true on the next connection after the engine wrote it back.** The release is a declared difference beyond the vendor. Chromium 155's source (`chrome/browser/chrome_browser_application_mac.mm` L384–497) explains why that reading proves nothing about the reset. A false `AXEnhancedUserInterface` write does release the scope the attribute requested, immediately once the 2 s enable debounce has run. But Chrome implements no getter for the attribute, so the readback is AppKit's and is not tied to Chromium's accessibility mode. Other scopes, such as reading the application role or descending into web content, keep their own flags. Electron's `AXManualAccessibility` getter compares the current mode with `kAXModeComplete` exactly. So a "true" readback does not show that the release failed, and an attribute read cannot verify the release on Chrome. Residue for M2b: verify the release by Chromium's own mode (for example the web tree's size or a CDP accessibility query), not by the attribute.
- Observation (M2a.5 hold, environment): **an unrelated SecurityAgent prompt can sit on the mini's console for a whole hold.** This one came from the owner's ClaudeUsage menubar app after another Claude CLI's keychain reads over SSH. While it showed, the paste section's alert watch aborted (by design), and `codesign` over SSH hung behind it. That made `cua engine status` report a signed, approved engine as "unsigned" and "approved: no", because an unreadable signature was read as an unsigned one. Fixed: status now reports "unknown" and gating stays fail-closed.

- Observation (M2b hold 1, the HID row): **under Korean 2-Set the current keyboard layout is com.apple.keylayout.2SetHangul, and posting r and k by their characters puts Option on them.** Under that flag TextEdit commits Latin "rk", and a small TextEdit-owned layer-0 window comes to the top. Plain key codes 15 and 40 with no flags compose 가.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold1/live/paste-hid2/result.json`, `paste-hid3`.
- Observation (M2b hold 1, the guard): **a scene window's bounds recorded at up go stale.** TextEdit placed its window after up, so a guard against the recorded bounds refused every post. Compare against the front window's bounds read at the same moment.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold1/live/paste/result.json`.
- Observation (MAWS P4-B2 L1–L7, 2026-10-22 on the mini, read by plan-executor from MAWS 7c7bcd5fe's evidence): **a source switch made while a text field already has focus changes the TIS source but not the keyboard layout.** `--probe input-source com.apple.inputmethod.Korean.2SetKorean` returned selected with its read-back 2SetKorean, and every later probe's `previous` stayed 2SetKorean. But the keystroke probe's `layout` (TISCopyCurrentKeyboardLayoutInputSource) read com.apple.keylayout.ABC, and the keys arrived raw (`gksrmf`). The same switch made before the field took focus engaged the input method (layout 2SetHangul, syllables composed). Both cases are Electron fields (MAWS's composer and its notes textarea); whether a native AppKit field behaves the same is open, and hold 3's `korean:textedit pre-refocus` reading records it. Consequences:
  - The matrix's Korean read-back read the TIS source, which this state passes. Hold 2b's korean:textedit selection was made with TextEdit's field focused, so its rows may have run with the input method not engaged. The typeText rows stand, because the engine routes by the TIS source (paste). `textedit/korean/pressKey.rk` entering `rk` is not evidence of keys under an engaged input method.
  - From hold 3, Korean counts only when, after the target is refocused, the source reads 2SetKorean and the layout reads 2SetHangul (Decision Log "Korean by its layout").
  - The engine's `inputSourceChanged` check reads the TIS source. A switch in this state still stops typing, which the spec's Text rule asks for, since the source did change.
  Evidence: MAWS `docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4-b2/l1-l7-2026-10-22/` (run2, run4 and run6 L3 probe outputs); `m2b/hold2b/korean/cua/run-3/result.json` (readings and the rk rows).
- Observation (M2b hold 2b, findings to judge, both model-facing): **(1) midSwitch's `entered` count is one short at the stop in 3 of 8 recorded samples.** The field held one unit more than `cua/entered`: chrome 198/199 (hold 2a run 2), electron 188/189 (hold 2a run 1), chrome 180/181 (hold 2b run 3). The count tells the model where to resume, so a short count makes it type a character twice. **(2) Chrome's background paste times out** with "Timed out waiting for the application to read the clipboard". Hold 3 records both again with the engine's message kept.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold2a/matrix/*/result.json`, `m2b/hold2b/korean/cua/run-3/result.json`, matrix cua run 3.
- Observation (M2b hold 3, 2026-10-22, the architect's AppKit question): **a native AppKit field also keeps ABC's layout when the source is switched while it has focus.** `korean:textedit pre-refocus` read source 2SetKorean with layout com.apple.keylayout.ABC; after the refocus, 2SetHangul. The same held for selections made with Chrome's and Electron's fields focused (perContext pre-refocus readings). Focus arriving after the switch engages the input method in every scene.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold3/matrix/cua/run-1/result.json` (meta.unicode.readings).
- Observation (M2b hold 3, route 3 live): **with route 3 the background Chromium actions and TextEdit's background ⌘A take effect on cua**, in both cua runs: electron click.button (60.8 ms, the vendor 105 ms), setValue.text and selectText.text @finder, TextEdit pressKey.selectAll @finder and @chrome (`whole: true`), all route 3, interference clean, Electron's accessibility mode enabled throughout. Electron's background typeText, pressKey.tab and paste @finder still fail their prepare on cua ("the field could not be given the focus") where the vendor's pass.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold3/matrix/cua/run-1`, `run-2`; the vendor's `matrix/vendor/run-1`.
- Observation (M2b hold 3, the Finder scene): **the run's Finder window grows on each open**: 660 (hold 2b), 716, 772, 800, 828, 856 pt tall across tonight's runs, and the bind and teardown checks then report "another process's window is on top" even when the run's window is the only one on layer 0 (route-ab attempt 4, with Tailscale and Terminal hidden). The likelier cause is the growth, not another app. Residue.
  Evidence: `m2b/hold3/lanes.txt`, `winlist-*`, `route-ab-stopped-a1..a3`, `route-ab`.
- Observation (M2b hold 2b, Korean in Chromium): **`r k` by pressKey under Korean 2-Set composes 가 in Chrome and in Electron**, in both lanes. The matrix's expectation is `rk` under both sources (unicode-plan, PRESS_KEY). Whether that expectation stands is a spec question for the expected file; see Outcomes.
- Observation (M3 hold, the attestation): **the task lifecycle answers some js calls itself, with no `_meta`.** A call rejected before dispatch (`task_ending`, `cancelled`) never reaches a runtime, and an in-flight call the connection drops (`connection_failed`, `connection_closing`) never gets an engine's answer. M3.3's "every js result attests" rule refused the first of these live; the rule now distinguishes them (Decision Log "M3 hold").
  Evidence: `m3/hold/probe-lifecycle/`.
- Observation (M3 hold, the suite on the mini): **the mini's whole `npm test` does not pass outside CI's environment.** Under the mini's default Homebrew Node 26.4.0, the operator-console test of `test/spike-engine-measure-groups.test.mjs` fails deterministically, though it passes under Node 24, CI's pin. Under accept-native's suite environment with Node 24.22.0 first on PATH, 11 other environmental cases fail, among them tz-probe, maws-live-probe, the topology cases and native-pipe's /tmp listing. So accept-native's item 1 cannot be the unit-suite authority on that Mac; the hosted gate is.
  Evidence: `m3/hold/README.md`, item 1.
- Observation (M3 hold, the TextEdit fixture): **with items 5 and 6 unselected, the fixture still runs but its steps go unreported, and it left its own TextEdit running.** When it cannot confirm that its close left TextEdit windowless, it leaves the TextEdit it launched running. The put-back stopped that TextEdit by pid (5 off-screen windows, no autosave or saved state).
  Evidence: `m3/hold/README.md`.

## Decision Log
