- Decision (2026-10-18, verification call at authoring): the spec gets an independent design review and, since the execution section has more than one milestone, the buildability review of the execution section in the same round, both re-run after the round's findings are folded until a round yields nothing worth changing; a `doperpowers:critique` debate runs on the design after M0 revises it from measurements and before M1a starts, because the input, settle and intervention choices are novel and the cost of being wrong is a milestone; every milestone's PR gets a `doperpowers:reviewer-high` whole-branch review; M5 is the acceptance sitting on the mini.
  Rationale: the siblings' council (#115) with the critique moved to where the design is measured rather than guessed.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 1 folded): the engine speaks the facade's method vocabulary, not the vendor's IPC shapes; comparison A runs on the vendor host with cua's service loaded, and B and C are defined; the socket lives beside the bundle and a service never shuts down an engine it did not launch; input is posted to the element's owning process, route 3 is the SPI post with active-state events, and the activation question is M0's first probe; settle waits for AX notifications with the vendor's 1 s floor; intervention is keyed on the driven app; typeText routes by input source; goldens compare modulo index renumbering; the "beat" on speed is a 10% margin; the forbidden list is enforced in the engine and the engine directory is denied to cells; policy and approvals land before the first app-scoped method; the baseline's whole plan runs early; M6 supplies cua's own module tree; the Ghidra step builds its natives; the selection table, the Linux method list, the real harness command lines and the corrected paths.
  Rationale: the design review (fable) and the buildability review (opus) of 2026-10-18 showed the first draft's comparison, oracle and intervention contract could not produce the proof they claimed, and that the earlier "one JS client for both engines" reason for the vendor's wire shapes was false. Alternatives rejected then: a cua-native engine schema was adopted over the vendor's; an event tap for intervention was rejected (another permission, no gain); keeping text rendering in the engine was rejected again.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 2 folded): one approved engine per Mac outside every `CUA_HOME`, served in place by every session and harness (`--approved-engine`), replaced only with the owner present, with M0 probe (f) measuring what re-raises the capture confirmation; the launch relay runs `open -n -g`, the engine exits when its socket is already live, `buildEngine` stops a replaced engine and fails visibly on `busy`, the client checks version and build; interventions need HID evidence and the driven app's involvement together, so the engine's own effects never fire it; secrets never take the paste route (`concealed`), every pasteboard write carries the concealed and transient marker types, and the paste fallback is reported; settle is capped at 5 s and reported; the approval memory lives in the supervisor; comparison A is "engine and service" with `cua/serviceMs`; the baseline gains `setup.activate` and measures the driven-app-frontmost regime; the concurrent-use probe and the input-source switch are the approved bundle's `--probe` mode, gated by the front-window probe; renumbering covers the trailer and the removed-ID ranges; the Screen Recording relaunch waits for idle; `CUA_SKY_SERVICE` is the one slot; `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=always` is refused; the selection table gains the two `engine_not_installed` rows (default without an engine warns and runs the vendor until M6); the Chrome scene is a harness-started second Chrome instance resolved by pid and the Electron scene is a harness-built fixture app, so no scene process is the owner's; `resolve_app` accepts a pid; the spike runs the real facade and host through a direct supervisor launch; M0's promote rule is total and its forks go to the owner; M1 and M2 are split by verification strategy; M6's acceptance repetition omits the vendor-side items; the Ghidra steps and the CLI forms are the real ones.
  Rationale: the round-2 design review (fable) and buildability review (opus) of 2026-10-18 showed the baseline harness stops at its first key cell on a non-activating engine, LaunchServices reuses a running instance of the same bundle id, the round-1 intervention rule fired on the engine's own window changes, every new on-disk engine copy would re-prompt for capture and stop the mini, the default flip was undefined without an engine, and the owner's Chrome and an owner Electron app cannot be scenes under the privacy constraint. Alternatives rejected: keeping machine-wide HID idle alone or app-keyed signals alone (both are required); per-home engine copies; MAWS as the Electron scene (an owner product, and its e2e holds).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 3, design, folded): `approved.json` is written only by `cua engine approve` after a successful screenshot with the owner at the console, `buildEngine` writes `pending.json`, and the configured identity is a second reinstall trigger; a harness instance of an ambiguous app is bound as `pid:<n>` through the facade's one `app` string, and an ambiguous bare name is `ambiguousApp`; the intervention rule drops the key-window clause; `concealedInputUnavailable` is a distinct error name the wrapper renders as its own sentence, and a concealed `paste` is refused; `engine.lock` makes the socket claim atomic; the default-engine row without Node 24 warns until M6; every live run begins and ends with `cua engine stop`; non-interference adds a resident activation watcher, the concurrent-use cell places the post inside the action's interval and has a sheet variant; finding-elements targets come from the fixtures' ground truth; a 6 MiB `Skyshot` budget; declines are remembered; `setup.activate` sits right after `bind.getApp`; the beat is read in-cell; the input source is saved in the run directory and restored by cleanup.
  Rationale: the round-3 design review (fable, 2026-10-18) found the capture gate had no writer and the Chrome scene could not cross the facade, and that three measurements would have passed without proving their claim.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 3, buildability, folded): the scene processes carry bundle ids no owner process uses, Chrome for Testing at a harness path and the Electron fixture with its own id, addressed by path through the unchanged facade on either engine, and `pid:<n>` is withdrawn; the harness refuses while a foreign process runs a scene's id and the key guard compares by pid; the selection table is total (default with `CUA_REPL_HOST=cua` acts as explicit, the comparison-A row is not explicit-only, `engine_retired` from M6); harnesses never call `buildEngine` and refuse unless the checkout's `build` equals the attested and approved one; runs stop the engine only through `shutdown` under the admission lock with `status.connections`; the own host's relay passes `PATH` only; `accept-native` items 12 and 13 stay pinned to the vendor engine until M6 retires them; the fixtures and the vendor-lane capture (`measure.mjs --lane vendor`) are M0's; M2b gives the parity and matrix harnesses the approved-runtime and approved-engine route on macOS; out-of-process targets are M1a's; acceptance 3 checks focus through `--probe focus`; the concurrent-use pair is named; the probe's signature, result channel and exemption are defined; the Ghidra step names the real list path and output.
  Rationale: the round-3 buildability review (opus, 2026-10-18) found the harness instance of a shared-id app could not be named on the vendor lane, the table had holes a plugin update would fall into, and eight steps an executor would get stuck on.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 4, folded): `buildEngine` stages the bundle beside the approved location and moves it into place only after the running engine's `shutdown` succeeds, so a `busy` answer leaves the approved bundle, `approved.json` and the engine untouched; `cua engine build` is M1b's entry point and the mini's signing prerequisites are M1b's owner steps; `typeText` under a Latin source keys every character the layout can produce with the layout's virtual key and the Unicode string together (Unicode-only events for the rest), the per-context input preference makes a background driven app's source unknown (paste, or `concealedInputUnavailable`), and the per-app fallback list is withdrawn; the pre-action guard applies to every input kind while the driven app is frontmost; the probe posts from an HID-state source, is launched with `-g -W`, and two positive intervention cells join the matrix (owner-run items if HID-tap posts do not reset the counter); the selection table gains the default-engine vendor-host row and a refusal precedence; `cua/serviceMs` is the wrapper's total around `handleRpc` on both lanes with `cua/engineMs` on cua's; acceptance 5 reads the beat from the report or the owner's parity decision; the sheet variant drives TextEdit with the Electron fixture as the user window and both variants read the user window back through `--probe focus`; the foreign-process refusal covers Chrome for Testing and the fixture only; M0 records raw `Skyshot` trees and one action sequence per scene for M1a's goldens; the M0 steps gain the approve line and the vendor lane's real command; Chrome for Testing is pinned by version and SHA-256 and the fixture is re-signed ad hoc, with the `electron` pin in M0; `cua/actionInterval` carries the action's interval; the `approve` request prompts and captures without a bound app.
  Rationale: the round-4 design review (fable) and buildability review (opus) of 2026-10-18 found a build order that wedged every engine update on the mini while a session was open, two typeText conditions the engine could not observe, a guard narrowing that typed into the user's keystrokes, no test of `userIntervened` after M0, a missing table row, no way for M1b to build the engine it proves, a probe launch that took focus, and a sheet variant on apps without sheets. Alternatives rejected: a bundle-id list of apps that ignore Unicode events (the ruling is any ordinary app); assembling in place and recovering by hand.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 5, folded): the method matrix runs in two regimes, frontmost (the vendor's outcome as reference) and background (the fixture's ground truth as reference, including the launch path), and the non-interference and concurrent-use cells are background-regime cells judged by outcome as well as by the three oracles, so an engine whose background posts land nowhere fails; after M6 the reference is M5's attested cua triple, checked in as a baseline with `compare --same-engine`, and PRs touching the engine or its service rerun the live parts against it; the pre-action refusal cell has a validity rule on `cua/receivedAt` and `cua/hidIdleMs`, and the debounce is named as cua's value until `F` recovers the vendor's; a source switch during a keyed `typeText` stops the request with a bounded error and the Korean matrix has a row for it; the explicit-`cua` no-Node row requires an approved engine; `cua/serviceMs` separates transport on single-call rows only; the approved location has no version segment; identity resolution and its refusal are M1b's with the `signingIdentity` setting and the keychain unlock as owner prerequisites and a signing check in the Concrete Steps; `cua engine approve` is a loop (prompt, shutdown, relaunch, capture) refusing while connections are open, and `cua engine status` exists from M1b; the spike carries `approve`, `shutdown` and a minimal `--probe keystroke|input-source`; the generated Swift policy file is committed under `native/engine/` with a test; the acceptance lines name the mini and a scratch home; `--probe focus` enables `AXManualAccessibility` on the named pid.
  Rationale: the round-5 design review (fable) found that the non-interference cells passed an engine whose background input landed nowhere, which the spec itself named as Chromium's case, that nothing judged speed after M6, and that a mid-action source switch corrupted keyed text silently; the round-5 buildability review (opus) found M1b's build step had no identity and no unlocked keychain, and that a running engine cannot see its own Screen Recording grant. Alternatives rejected: a bundle-id list for the Electron read-back (`app.setAccessibilitySupportEnabled` in the fixture bypasses the engine path); versioned engine directories.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (review round 6, design, folded): neither the approve loop nor the in-session relaunch depends on a running engine observing its own Screen Recording grant (the approve loop relaunches on the operator's console confirmation; the service relaunches an engine with no other connection on the next screenshot after a `permissionsPending`), and probe (f) asks whether it can; `status.connections` counts connections other than the requester's; the post-M6 gate checks in M5's triple, matrix and parity runs and gives parts 1, 3 and 5 same-engine forms with `build`, `engineVersion` and the commit as permitted moves, run per release that changes the engine or its service, since each rerun costs a capture confirmation; the launch-path cell covers only scenes the engine can launch into their fixed state and a self-activating family is recorded for the owner's acceptance; the mid-action source check reads `TISCopyCurrentKeyboardInputSource` directly, counts characters, and fails as `inputSourceChanged`; the intervention-fires cell has the interval validity rule and `userIntervened` carries the interval; the pre-action cell schedules the post with `keystroke --at` and an all-invalid cell is NO VERDICT; `--probe focus` waits up to 1 s after enabling `AXManualAccessibility`. From the buildability half: every input result and input error carries the four times in its meta, and the pre-action refusal is `userIntervened` with the still-interacting sentence, as the vendor's; acceptance 8 installs the scratch home from the archive with the owner present, acceptance 1 sets the engine explicitly; M0's copy step stops the spike engine and stages the bundle in `buildEngine`'s order; the signing check is re-runnable; M1b builds in the harness checkout at the sitting's commit; `approve` is in the protocol's method list.
  Rationale: the round-6 design review (fable, 2026-10-18) found the round-5 approve loop asserting the opposite of the Permissions paragraph about grant observability, the asker's own connection counted against `approve`, a post-M6 gate only part 1 could consume, a launch-path cell reaching scenes the engine cannot launch, and a notification flag that could not flip mid-request; the buildability review (opus) found the wire shapes without the fields the pre-action cell validates on, an acceptance command on an empty home, and the M0 copy overwriting a running engine. Both judged the section buildable once folded. Alternative rejected: a per-PR live rerun after M6 (each needs the owner at the console).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-18, design revised from M0's reading, `R/F-helper-logic.md`): the design follows the vendor's recovered mechanisms where the earlier text inferred them wrongly, and labels cua's departures. Adopted from the vendor: background clicks are ⌘-flagged pid-posted AppKit-made mouse events with `CGEventSetWindowLocation`, not an SPI post; `AXPick` before `AXPress`, right clicks synthesized; keys, scroll and modifiers from an HID-system source; typeText one key press per UTF-16 unit with key code and Unicode string, 250 µs apart; route 3 is the fake-activation pair (AppKit-defined subtype-1 event plus an activation-point click) for keyboard actions, web views, held keys and Catalyst, with the SLS/CPS calls optional; the pressKey table is the vendor's exact names; the primary window falls back to the last of `AXWindows`; the 20-attribute prefetch with the private hierarchy call priced against the public fetch in M0; enablement sets one of the two attributes, never both, and the VoiceOver preference is never touched; width 100 then the visible subset, 200 levels; indexes 0-based with sibling-structural inheritance, re-fetch only after layout change or destruction with the eleven-attribute match; settle as a 250 ms quiet period sampled at 100/250 ms with the ten notifications and the busy-walk veto, no floor, deferred to the next observation; paste with `public.rtf`, current-host-only, the 2 s read wait plus the 2 s effect poll, restore on every path; the diff's single live header, id-keyed, budget equal to the full tree's node count, baseline advancing on every observation; the header without `App=` and the element-line grammar; the intervention tap with per-event attribution and a 5 s debounce. Kept as cua's own, each labelled in the design: the per-element messaging timeout and traversal deadline, the node, value and `Skyshot` budgets, the never-reused index rule and the lineage check, the Unicode-string fallback for unkeyable characters, the Concealed/Transient pasteboard markers, stopping an in-flight sequence at the first attributed event and refusing at once within the debounce, the reported settle cap, and the HID-idle fallback if the tap needs a grant the engine lacks.
  Rationale: F found no SkyLight post in the binary, HID-source keys, no settle floor, index reuse, tap-based intervention that never stops an action, and a 5 s debounce; three of the spec's mechanisms rested on the wrong inference and would have been measured against a vendor that does something else. Alternatives rejected: copying the vendor's index reuse and its 100 s wait (both worse for the model); the private hierarchy call as the only prefetch route (priced first, public route kept as the fallback).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f.
- Decision (accepted by the owner, 2026-10-18): the engine's peer policy is uid plus the sandbox deny and the engine-side forbidden list; any process of the owner's uid outside the sandbox can reach the engine without an approval.
  Rationale: an unsigned Node cannot carry a code-signing requirement, and the vendor's ancestry check is what cua is removing. Alternative: a per-launch token passed through the launch relay, which the relay cannot pass today and which any same-uid process could read from the service's memory anyway.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, maws-3f; accepted by the owner the same day ("받아들임"), who in the same answer accepted the scene decisions of entry 3 (a harness-owned Chromium process with its own user-data directory and a harness-built Electron fixture, so no owner app or content is observed); the Chromium process became Chrome for Testing with its own bundle id in round 3, which keeps the property the owner accepted.
- Decision (2026-10-18, M0 spike pre-flight): the spike's service, like M1a's will, resolves the app (`resolve_app`) before every app-scoped call and asks one elicitation per (connection, app), answering repeats itself as the supervisor's approval memory will from M1a, so the cua lane's in-cell numbers carry the round trips the designed service has, and an elicitation never crosses SSH to the harness more than once per app; the vendor lane's per-call policy round trip is the vendor's own cost and is kept. On the MacBook, before the owner's grants exist, nothing may raise a permission dialog: the engine's start-up touches neither the Accessibility prompt nor capture, `status` reads the grants with `AXIsProcessTrusted()` and `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess()` (neither prompts), and the unit tests exercise only framing, the 8 MiB bound, `ping`, `status`, `list_apps`, `resolve_app` of running apps and the stand-in renderer, against a debug build run directly under `CUA_ENGINE_SOCKET` (skipped off macOS or without a Swift toolchain); observation, capture, input, `approve` and the probes run first in the mini sitting. Route 3 is built as the second probe once `F` names the soft-linked functions.
  Rationale: the promote rule compares in-cell medians, which must include what the real service will do per call, and the owner alone answers grants.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-18, M0 spike, T1's review): the `Skyshot` and `Node` shapes in Interfaces gain what the revised Observation section fetches and the renderer prints, so the sitting records goldens in the shape M1a consumes: the optional attributes of the 20-attribute set, the width rule's `shown` descriptor, and `menuBar` (the top-level menu-bar items, numbered in the lineage after the window's nodes); indexes are 0-based over the nodes the engine emits, since it does not render. The request-time prompts on a first observation and screenshot stay as the Permissions paragraph states (a reviewer asked for preflight-only failure there; the spec's flow prompts where the grant is first needed, and the harnesses refuse capture cells against a pending build, which is what keeps a mini run from meeting a prompt). The engine's settle (deferred to the next observation) reports the notifications it counted, so probe (c′)'s notification rates come from the engine.
  Rationale: the goldens are recorded once per owner sitting; a shape M1a must change costs another.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-18, M0 spike, T1's re-review): three labelled departures and two interface additions. (1) Enablement is set only on web-shaped apps and the vendor's named bundles (`com.apple.iWork*`, `com.spotify.client`), never on other AppKit apps, where `AXEnhancedUserInterface` left set changes window behaviour (the purpose's promise not to interfere); the matrix checks one iWork app and Spotify for parity. (2) A retired index answers `The element ID is no longer valid…` where the vendor answers `<n> is an invalid element ID`, because in cua a retired index was a real element that vanished (indexes are never reused); a never-issued index keeps the vendor's sentence. (3) The settle's busy walk reads through the observation's fetch route and hands its tree to the observation, as the Settle paragraph says, rather than a second public walk. Interfaces: `servesAsTitle` becomes `servesAsTitleFor` (the indexes a title serves) and `shown` gains `ranges`, both because the renderer's rules need them. **Open for critique (Decision Log entry 1):** neither enablement attribute is ever unset, so a driven web app stays in full-accessibility mode until it relaunches; whether the engine releases it (and when) is the critique's question.
  Rationale: the reviewer's findings on fa6a80ac; the goldens are recorded once per sitting.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-18, M0 spike, scope without input): the owner writes the engine's input half by hand on `feat/117-input` (the agents' attempts were stopped by a safety classifier, Surprises); until it merges, `measure.mjs` keeps every input cell behind one switch (`CUA_INPUT_METHODS_BUILT`) and reports it as "not run: input methods not built". The mini sitting proves what needs no input: the promote rule's speed half (`getAXState` with the stand-in render and `getScreenshot`, on all four scenes against the vendor lane), probes (c), (c′) and (f), the raw-`Skyshot` and vendor-text goldens on the fixed states, and the settle notification counts. Deferred with the input half: probes (a), (b), (d) (including the tap and HID-reset checks) and (e) (including the concealed and per-context cases), the action-sequence goldens and the TextEdit task cells on the cua lane; the promote rule's input half is judged when the owner's branch lands, in a second sitting.
  Rationale: the owner's ruling of 2026-10-18; the speed half is the fork the promote rule names first.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-18, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-19, M0 spike, the dispatching session's ruling): the scenes' Finder refusal counts only Finder windows on screen (layer 0, at least 100 × 100 points, `kCGWindowIsOnscreen` true); off-screen ones are reported in preflight's output by number and bounds, without titles, and do not refuse. No accessibility read of the owner's Finder.
  Rationale: Finder itself holds an off-screen layer-0 window of document size (1067 × 631) that it recreated at once after a relaunch on the MacBook (184776 after pid 736 → 2909), so it is not the owner's; the refusal exists so the run's Finder window is the one the scene's setup brings to front and the engine observes, and an off-screen window is never the focused or main window. Alternative rejected: counting minimized windows through Finder's accessibility window list, which reads the owner's Finder and still cannot see windows on other Spaces. Review of the change follows the run (it sends no input).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's ruling.
- Decision (2026-10-19, M0 spike tuning, the dispatching session's go): the web-area wait runs once per process, until a web area has been seen there, not on every Chromium observation, and the engine caches app resolution; settle at rest is investigated before any change (the vendor returns at about 300 ms when nothing is posted, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §8; the MacBook measured 460–800 ms). The next MacBook window adds the vendor lane on the same machine, so each tuning iteration is judged against that machine's vendor; the mini sitting stays the measurement that counts.
  Rationale: the shakedown's per-observation costs (Surprises, 649af498) are the promote rule's speed half.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-19, revising the M0 spike pre-flight entry, the dispatching session's ruling): the service resolves the app (`resolve_app`) on the first app-scoped call per (connection, app string), not before every call, and keeps the identity for the connection; it re-resolves only when a result's `app` identity (bundle id or pid) differs from the cached one or the engine reports the app not running. The engine resolves for itself on every call (0.2–0.6 ms) and enforces the forbidden list on every `execute` from its own policy copy, so the cached path skips nothing that guards. The protocol is unchanged, and this is M1a's shape. Alternative rejected: folding resolution into `get_app_state`, which would observe before the policy's approval. Also ruled: the engine reads per element only what the vendor's per-role reads and the renderer's grammar need (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §1, `R/sky-behaviour.md` §23), proven by rendering equivalence (the stand-in renderer's text identical from the reduced and the full read set on all four scenes); reads are dropped, never Node fields the goldens carry. **Open for critique (Decision Log entry 1):** starting the settle's busy walk at the start of the wait and keeping its reads when no counted notification arrives before the quiet point (departs from the vendor's order, can miss a late value change), and checking quiet at exactly 250 ms rather than on the 300 ms tick (departs from the vendor's timing).
  Rationale: the tuning round's diagnosis (Surprises): the in-cell gap is the service's extra relay round trip per call and the per-element read set, not resolution.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's ruling.
- Decision (2026-10-19, M0 spike, mini sitting, tuning iteration 1, the dispatching session's call under the promote rule's in-sitting iterations): `get_app_state` starts the window capture on its own thread as soon as the window's frame is known and joins it after the walk, so an observation with a screenshot costs the longer of the walk and the capture rather than their sum; the capture's own timings merge into the observation's under their existing names, with the wait recorded as `screenshotJoinMs`. The capture reads only the window element, its pid and frame, taken before the thread starts, and its own lock-guarded shareable-content cache; the walk is unchanged. M1a keeps this shape.
  Rationale: the first series' TextEdit observation was walk then capture in sequence (55.9 + 49.7 ms), and neither depends on the other.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's call (2f1bf2cf).
- Decision (2026-10-19, M0 spike, mini sitting, the dispatching session's ruling; tuning iteration 2): the screenshot matches the vendor's size, the window frame times the pixels per point of the display under it (`CGGetDisplaysWithPoint`, the display mode's pixel width over its width), and a coordinate click, which arrives in screenshot space, is divided by the same scale where it becomes a window point (`Input.swift`'s x/y path, the one place). The Screenshot paragraph's "scaled to points" was meant as the vendor's size and is wrong about it on the mini; the intent, an unchanged model-facing API whose image is the one the vendor would give, stands. **Open:** the vendor's exact sizing rule. The mini's scenes came out at pixel size, while the MacBook baseline's 1172 × 976 was read as points; whether the vendor caps or downscales above some size is read from its capture path after the sitting. Its capture does not go through ScreenCaptureKit: `SkyshotOperation.capture` calls `WindowServerSPI.captureWindowImagesInRect` (decompiled at 10025bbf0, caller 100eadeac) with an `imageSize` parameter, and SlimCore's `writeScreenshotToFile` (100eaf220) writes the file; that path is also a later speed lever, not taken in this hold.
  Rationale: the model must see the image the vendor would give it; a smaller image is a model-facing change and makes the `getScreenshot` speed row unequal.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's ruling (b1039a31).
- Decision (2026-10-19, M0 spike, the mini sitting's verdict, the dispatching session's reading of the promote rule): **PROMOTE.** In one sitting against fresh vendor triples, iteration 2's TextEdit in-cell medians are below the vendor's with equal images (`getAXState` 43.1 against 50.7 ms, `getScreenshot` 45.8 against 53.4), and route 3 carried click and pressKey to TextEdit and Finder with the frontmost application and the topmost window unchanged and no activation seen by the device-side watcher (iteration 1's build; iteration 2 changed only the capture size and the x/y click mapping). The first series' DISCARD FORK is superseded by the in-sitting iterations the rule allows. Chrome is recorded per family: every guarded cell refused because the scene window was not the app's front window, and a background `type_text` by route 3 landed 0 of 8; undiagnosed (the refusals may be the harness's), so the rule's per-family branch applies: route 4 for Chrome unless the diagnosis finds a route, and whether that is acceptable is the owner's call before M1a. Electron took background click and key in the first series. *(Corrected 2026-10-19 from the outputs, Surprises, the records-reading entry: Electron took the background click by route 1 only; every key into Electron crashed the engine and is unmeasured; the Chrome refusals were the harness's title check and hit the vendor lane identically. The verdict stands: the promote rule's input half names TextEdit and Finder, where it holds.)* Still to follow under the same rule: the design sections on input, settle, intervention and install revised from the measurements, then the critique debate of entry 1.
  Rationale: spec L221 (Plan of Work, "Promote or discard"); the evidence in Surprises (iterations 1 and 2).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's ruling.
- Decision (2026-10-19, the design revised from M0's measurements, the step "Promote or discard" requires before the critique of entry 1). Each change, with the strongest alternative and why it lost; every figure is the mini sitting's unless named otherwise (`spike/summary.md`, `spike/iter1*`, `spike/iter2*`, `spike/approve`, `spike/probe-f`).
  1. **Input routes.** Route 3, the fake activation with no flag, is the engine's route for every synthesized click and every keyboard action into an app that does not believe it has focus; route 2, the ⌘-flagged pid-post, is its fallback when the activation guard fails and the plain post for an app that already believes it has focus; route 1 where the element advertises the action; route 4 last and always reported. Alternative: the vendor's rule for clicks (⌘-click for every ordinary background click, the fake activation only for its reserved cases, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.4), about 40 ms cheaper per click (the guard measured 38–52 ms) and parity with today's outcomes. Lost because the ⌘ flag reaches the app and changes a click's meaning (selection toggling in lists and tables, link following, new tabs), which the method matrix's background regime scores by outcome, and because M0 showed route 3 delivering click and key on TextEdit and Finder with the frontmost application and the top window unchanged and no activation seen (`spike/iter2-input`); 40 ms is a tenth of the settle that follows an action. The sitting read route 2's delivery, not its effect, so whether a ⌘-click and a plain click produce the same outcome on the scenes' targets is a delegated unknown (U10); if the matrix finds them equal everywhere, the default can revert to the vendor's rule for the 40 ms, an M2a decision. Route 3's fallback is route 2 rather than route 4 because a forced route-2 key landed on both families without activation, where route 4 activates by definition.
  2. **Coordinates.** A coordinate is in the pixel space of the lineage's last screenshot and is divided by that observation's scale, kept on the connection's lineage state (the dispatching session's ruling from the code review of the sitting's commits; the vendor applies the observation's scaling factor at click time, §5.2). Alternative, the sitting's build: the display's scale under the window at click time, the same number unless the window moved to a display of another scale between observation and click. Lost because the model's numbers are in the image it saw. A fix wave implements the ruling on its own branch; the path is live-tested before M1a (U2). *(Refined by the post-fix-wave entry below, 2026-10-19: the stored screenshot's per-axis scale, the current window origin, the three refusals.)*
  3. **Screenshot.** The display's pixel size through ScreenCaptureKit, captured beside the walk and joined after it. Alternative: the vendor's capture, a private window-server call with an `imageSize` parameter, a possible speed lever. Lost because the public API met the vendor's size (1220 × 1720, within 0.2% in bytes) and beat its in-cell time by 14% (45.8 against 53.4 ms), and a private capture call would be one more `dlsym`'d SPI without a measured need. The vendor's exact sizing rule stays open (U1).
  4. **Fetch route and read set.** The public per-element fetch; the private `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` route dropped from the design. Alternative: keep it feature-detected for large trees. Lost because it was slower on all four scenes, Finder-200 included (267.7 against 340.7 ms), and on the MacBook, with no tree where it paid. The per-role read-set reduction ruled on 2026-10-19 (the pre-flight revision entry) is withdrawn: equal text on TextEdit, Finder and Electron, one line different on Chrome, and no saving (−5 to −10 ms). `valueMarkdown` stays as designed (0.4–3.1 ms measured).
  5. **Settle.** The vendor's order (the busy walk after the quiet point) and timing (the check on the sampling tick) kept, with the measured cost at rest recorded in the section (no notification in any sample; engine totals 328–550 ms by scene, the quiet period plus one busy walk). Alternative: the two cheaper orders of the pre-flight revision entry, the busy walk at the wait's start and the check at exactly 250 ms, saving 10–137 ms per post-action observation on a quiet app. Lost for now because the cost is parity with the vendor's own rule and the TextEdit task cells already beat the vendor's by 30% (2,755 against 4,028 ms), while the first order can miss a value change the ten notifications do not carry; both stay open for the critique as that entry records them.
  6. **Intervention.** The listen-only tap stays the mechanism of record, the HID-idle test the fallback, and the choice is a delegated unknown for the next input sitting (U5); `status` gains the listen preflight; the claim that the engine's own effects never fire it is withdrawn as unproven. Alternative: decide the fallback now, since the recorded rows read "the tap could not be created" in every pass. Lost because those rows are uninformative either way (the harness unwrapped neither a refusal nor a success; Surprises (4), corrected 2026-10-19 after the fix wave), so whether the engine can create the tap is unknown rather than negative, a plain process holding the grant creates the tap on the MacBook, and the fallback cannot attribute input to an app: a click on the driven app's background window would not count and the user's typing in their own window would while the driven app is frontmost, which breaks the purpose section's promise, where the tap's per-event attribution keeps it.
  7. **Approve and install.** The approve step leads the by-hand grant path (the pane, "+", the bundle's path) and polls `status`, relying on neither a prompt nor the running engine's read of its grants; every engine update is assumed to raise the capture confirmation and runs with the owner at the console. Alternatives: rely on the prompts (none appeared on the mini: `approve.json`'s first request read both grants false, and the owner added the bundle by hand); or install each build at a new path to dodge the confirmation, since the new-path copy raised none on the mini (f3). Lost because the prompt's absence and the confirmation's trigger are both unexplained and the latter reversed on the MacBook (U7, U8), and because versioned directories were rejected in round 5 for leaving a stale engine serving with its grants beyond `cua engine stop`'s reach.
  8. **Process model.** The vendor-host lane's pre-launch clause dropped: plain `open -a` did not activate the engine (probe (c), three of three).
  9. **Chrome.** The per-family rule applies, route 4, pending the owner's call; but the refusals came from the harness's window-title check and hit the vendor lane equally (Surprises, the records-reading entry), so the diagnosis (U3) precedes the call rather than following it.
  10. **Records corrected** (Surprises, the records-reading entry): Electron's background key is unmeasured and its click went by route 1, which corrects the PROMOTE entry's "Electron took background click and key"; `probe.d.noFire` is vacuous; probe (d)'s operator steps never ran; the vendor's exposure of web content on the mini's Chrome scene is unconfirmed; the first series' Accessibility read is ambiguous.
  **Acceptance and the milestone cut are unchanged**: no measurement forces a change. Contingent on the unknowns and left as written: acceptance 1's permission keys gain `inputMonitoring` if the tap is chosen (U5); acceptance 8's onboarding item, "grants them again at the prompts", becomes a by-hand step if U7 finds the prompts never appear for this engine; acceptance 7's probe-(d) clause stands.
  **Delegated unknowns**, each with the move that resolves it and the milestone it gates:
  - U1 (cleared per the dispatching session, 2026-10-20; the reading's record joins M1a's goldens). The vendor's screenshot sizing rule (pixel size on the mini against the MacBook baseline's 1172 × 976), and whether its image is the window's frame or its visible rect for a partially off-screen window (added by the critique, 2026-10-19): read from the decompiled capture path (`captureWindowImagesInRect`'s `imageSize` and its caller), checked against both machines' sizes; gates M1a's goldens, before they are recorded. **Resolved 2026-10-19** (the decision below): the window's bounds times its screen's backing scale, rounded up, uncapped, with a legacy downsizing fit that is off server-side; the image covers the whole bounds; the 1172 × 976 baseline was the mini's.
  - U2 (closed 2026-10-20: exact on the aimed lines, the three refusals as ruled; "Screenshot"). The coordinate click live: one cell on the mini's 2× display clicking by coordinates on the fix wave's build, the caret or selection read back through the tree; gates M1a.
  - U3 (closed 2026-10-20: the harness's stray `(en-US)` argument; Chrome's background input delivers, the route-4 fork not needed; "Input"). Chrome's background input: the next sitting reads the Chrome scene window's title through the harness (the harness's own window, so it may be logged), corrects the check, then runs probes (a), (b) and (e) on Chrome; gates the owner's route-4 call and M1a.
  - U4 (closed 2026-10-20: delivered by every route, the click by route 1). Electron's background key: probes (a) and (b) on Electron with the key-focus fix; gates M2a's route table.
  - U5 (decided by the owner 2026-10-20: Input Monitoring required, the listen-only tap, detection off without it; "User intervention"). Probe (d)(i), the tap (revised 2026-10-19 after the fix wave): probe (d) re-run with the fixed harness (b3e3e9ee), whose rows carry the engine's answer, `errorName` and sentence with the listen preflight's state; only if that run still reports no tap, a hand-made tap creation on the mini with the TCC log streaming (`log stream --predicate 'subsystem == "com.apple.TCC"'`), with and without the Input Monitoring entry; then (d)'s first checks, whether the engine's own posts reach the tap or reset the HID idle counter and whether the probe's HID-tap post is seen; gates M2a's intervention mechanism and acceptance 7's two positive cells.
  - U6 (closed 2026-10-20: both inputs attributed to the driven app inside the action's interval). Probe (d)'s operator steps, a Screen Sharing keystroke during a long `type_text` and a click into the driven app's background window: the next input sitting with `--operator`; gates M2a.
  - U7 (closed 2026-10-20: the request is denied by `universalAccessAuthWarn` within about 40 ms with no window; onboarding by hand, three grants; "Install, signing and permissions"). Why no grant prompt appeared on the mini: one `approve` on a fresh bundle id with the TCC log streaming and System Settings' list state read before and after, the owner having run `tccutil reset Accessibility io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine` (and the same for `ScreenCapture`) at the console before the controlled sitting so the request is a first one (added by the critique, 2026-10-19); gates M4's onboarding text (the approve step does not depend on the answer).
  - U8 (closed 2026-10-20: no confirmation on any of the three moves, grants kept; replayd's `SCAlert` lines are the signal, the plist is not). The capture-confirmation asymmetry (a same-path rebuild prompting, a new-path copy silent on the mini; neither on the MacBook): one controlled sitting per Mac with both moves and the TCC log, and replayd's `ScreenCaptureApprovals.plist` read before and after each move (added by the critique, 2026-10-19); gates M4's install text (M1b's `buildEngine` assumes the worst case).
  - U9 (closed 2026-10-20: the four-scene triple in "Screenshot"; TextEdit and Electron behind by the capture floor, Finder and Chrome far ahead). Finder, Chrome and Electron speed on the iteration-2 build: the four-scene triple against a same-sitting vendor triple (the first series had Finder 293 against 459 ms and Electron 86.7 against 57.4 with the capture still in sequence); gates M2b's per-scene speed cells, not M1a, since the promote rule was judged on TextEdit as written.
  - U10 (closed 2026-10-20: a ⌘-click fails on TextEdit and adds to Finder's selection; route 2 leaves the defaults). The ⌘-click's outcome against a plain click's on the scenes' targets: the method matrix's background regime, with route 2 forced; gates M2a's default between routes 2 and 3.
  - U11. pid 45110, the one engine start of the sitting with neither an exit line nor a crash report: the unified log at the next sitting; gates nothing, recorded so it is not lost. **Resolved 2026-10-19** (the dispatching session, from the mini's unified log): launchd records the exit as SIGSEGV at 14:56:25Z ("exited due to SIGSEGV | sent by exc handler[45110], ran for 398642ms"), and its crash report is `cua-engine-2026-10-19-235627.ips` (pid 45110, captureTime 23:56:25.87 KST) with `SLPSGetKeyFocusProcess` on the stack: one of the 27 key-focus crashes, missed by the exit check because the report's file mtime is later than its capture time.
  Rationale: the sitting's outputs, read against the running records (Surprises); the promote rule's requirement that these sections be revised from measurement before the critique.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, the design-revision agent for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-19, after the sitting review's fix wave, 1be26dd1): two corrections to the revision entry above.
  (1) **Probe (d)'s evidence restated as unknown.** The fix wave found that the harness never unwrapped a successful `probe_tap` reply either (`{ok: true, result}`), so `tap.created` was undefined whichever the engine answered: a tap the engine created would have produced exactly the recorded row, "the tap could not be created" with `listenAccess: null`, and would have been left running, the second start then answering `tapBusy`. The recorded `probe.d.tap` and `probe.d.hidReset` rows are therefore uninformative either way, and "the tap failed in every pass, with and without Input Monitoring" (item 6 above; Surprises (4), both corrected) is not supported: whether the engine can create the tap is unknown. U5's resolving move is now re-running probe (d) with the fixed harness (b3e3e9ee), which carries the engine's answer, its `errorName` and sentence into the rows, and a hand-made tap creation only if that run still reports none. Item 6's choice stands on its other reasons.
  (2) **Coordinate clicks, the dispatching session's ruling, recorded.** A coordinate is placed in the pixel space of the screenshot the connection's last screenshot-bearing observation of that app returned, divided by that observation's stored per-axis scale and offset by the *current* window origin; it is refused before anything is posted, as `noScreenshotForCoordinates` (the sentence in c13759a4, Interfaces), when the connection holds no such observation, when the request names a lineage that is not current, or when the app's primary window is no longer the one in the screenshot (`CFEqual`); an observation without a screenshot keeps the stored one, and `disableDiff` clears it. Declared difference from the vendor: it applies the observation's scaling factor and the *observed* `visibleRect` origin (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.2), so it clicks relative to a stale rect where cua follows the moved window or refuses. Alternative: the vendor's rule as is, for parity. Lost because a click placed by a stale origin lands silently on whatever now sits under the old rect, while a click placed in the current window at the model's image coordinates is what the model meant, and a refusal naming a fresh observation is what it can act on. Implemented in c13759a4; the live cell (U2) stays due before M1a; the error joins Interfaces.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, the design-revision agent for maws-3f, on maws-3f's ruling.
- Decision (critique of the revised design, 2026-10-19: the `doperpowers:critique` debate entry 1 promised, run on the design as revised from M0; converged; the dispatching session ruled on every point and adopted all but A2, which is the owner's). Each ruling with the critic's argument and the strongest alternative:
  - **A1 Routes.** Critic: route 3 was made the default on the user's side alone (the frontmost application, the top window, the watcher), its effect on the driven app never measured, and the cheapest outcome-neutral route, a plain post with no ⌘ and no fake activation, never measured at all; and "route 3 for every synthesized background click" misdescribed the sitting's build, whose coordinate clicks went by route 2 with ⌘ when the app is inactive (`Input.swift`'s x/y path). Ruled: the ladder 1 → 2′ → 3 → 2 → 4, each family's rung by measurement; route 2′ defined and measured (U12); route 3's default provisional until U12 and U13; U13 names the two mechanisms by which route 3 could touch the driven app (the activate/deactivate hooks run per action; the activation pair's click at `AXActivationPoint`, which on the MacBook read (0, 982), the bottom-left corner, on a Finder window that does not list the attribute, and attribute-unsupported, −25205, on Safari's and Chrome's windows) and its three checks (hit-test each scene window's activation point and record the element under it; a route-3 action after `selectText` with the selection read back; the Electron fixture counting activate, focus and blur per driven action); the activation lifetime (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §23.18) decided from U13's data; the `SLSDisableUpdate` hold released after the pair or capped well under the poll's 1 s. Alternative: keep route 3 as the default on the sitting's evidence and measure 2′ later. Lost because a default whose driven-side effects are unmeasured is a guess the matrix would discover at M2b, and route 2′, if it delivers, is cheaper and free of both mechanisms. U12 and U13 gate M2a's route table, not M1a.
  - **A3 Enablement.** Critic: once-per-process enablement is defeated by a process that resets the attribute or relaunches, and the attribute is never released, so a driven web app stays in full-accessibility mode after cua is done (the T1 re-review entry's open item). Ruled: re-check per observation and set only when the attribute does not read true (the vendor's rule); release as a declared difference: remember that cua set it, which it does only when it read false, set it back to false when the engine's last connection that observed that pid closes, and never write it if it read true before cua did (VoiceOver or another client). Alternative: never release, as the vendor (parity; no risk of unsetting another client's attribute). Lost because the purpose is not interfering, and the read-true-before guard removes the other-client risk. *(The per-observation re-check is withdrawn for an attribute the engine owns, 2026-11-01, Decision Log #244: written once per owned process, as the vendor; the release and the read-true-before guard stand.)*
  - **A4 Intervention.** Critic: U5 jumped from the listen-only tap to a third grant and then to HID idle, skipping the tap kind the vendor itself runs, an active filter (options 0, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.5), which may be creatable under Accessibility alone where a listen-only tap is not. Ruled: U5's order is the listen-only tap under Accessibility alone, then an active default-option tap under Accessibility alone whose callback reads three fields, enqueues and returns every event untouched, re-enabling on `tapDisabledByTimeout`, then Input Monitoring, then HID idle; the design prefers whichever needs no third grant. Alternative: Input Monitoring as the second step. Lost because it costs a by-hand grant on every Mac for a question the vendor's own precedent may answer without one.
  - **A5 Debounce.** Critic: observing at once and clearing the flag at once lets a re-query taken while the user is still working clear the lock, so the next action posts into the user's work. Ruled: observe at once; clear the re-query flag only if the debounce has expired when the observation completes. Alternative: refuse the observation during the debounce (the vendor waits it out). Lost because the model's own re-query after a refusal would then be refused.
  - **B notes.** Tokens scoped by the lineage the service passes in `execute`, the engine's guard with the service's lineage check first (alternative: the service-side check alone; lost because the protocol already carries `lineage` and a stale token across a reconnect is the engine's to refuse); tokens retired after one absent revision, N = 1, since the vendor's previous-revision matching gives a returning element a new identity anyway; the engine-wide paste lock a FIFO held from snapshot through restore, covering `type_text`'s paste route; U1 also settles frame against visible rect for a partially off-screen window; the first-call document tells the model to observe after an action before clicking by coordinates (the sheet case); the wrapper's classification admits `noScreenshotForCoordinates` and its sentence names `getScreenshot`; the element-click descendant gate adopted (the vendor's nested-content test, §5.1); U7 gains `tccutil reset Accessibility io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine` as an owner action before the controlled sitting and U8 a read of replayd's `ScreenCaptureApprovals.plist` before and after each move.
  - **C gaps.** The forbidden list checked on the target element's owning pid before posting (an out-of-process panel served by a forbidden process); route 4 reachable only through a per-app override in `~/.config/cua/engine.json` (`routes: {<bundle id>: 4}`, default none), with a meta and `doctor` signal when route 3's guard fails three actions in a row for an app; scroll never arms the settle (the vendor's rule, §8.1); a `type_text` stopped by an intervention reports its entered count in meta; the engine checks the deadline on receipt and cancels a settle or launch wait at the deadline; the `SLSDisableUpdate` hold bounded as above. Each was a place where the design was silent and the engine would have had to choose.
  - **A2, the owner's (the spec stays neutral).** The uid-only peer policy (the accepted entry of 2026-10-18) and the mini-style install were accepted under "cua stays internal". If distribution to other people is in scope, M4 gains a peer-token trusted-ancestor policy setting and a prebuilt Developer-ID, notarized bundle whose `build` is the release artefact's hash. **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-20: internal, without foreclosing distribution.** #117 keeps the uid-only peer policy and the mini-style install. Distribution is follow-up #174, blocked on M4. Two things keep it open: (1) the peer check is one function, with the policy read from a setting whose only value in #117 is `uid`, so the trusted-ancestor policy and its peer token can be added as a second value; (2) the approved-engine attestation keys on an opaque `build` hash, so a prebuilt, notarized bundle can supply its release artefact's hash in place of a local build's. Alternative: distribution inside #117's M4. Lost because no one outside the owner uses cua yet, and a Developer ID plus notarization adds an account and a release pipeline that #117's purpose doesn't need.
  New delegated unknowns, both gating M2a's route table: U12, route 2′ on the four scenes with the activation watcher and an outcome read-back; U13, route 3's driven-side effects, the three checks above (both closed 2026-10-20 by the second sitting: U12 the route matrix, U13 the activation point, the selection and the Electron counters; "Input" and the entry below). U11 is closed the same day (the unified log: pid 45110 was a key-focus SIGSEGV whose crash report the exit check missed by file mtime; the U11 line). The next input sitting, which carried U2–U6, U9, U10, U12 and U13, ran on 2026-10-19 from branch `feat/117-sit2` and is merged (f3b2ae8e); its runbook, outputs and notes are under `spike/sit2/`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, the design-revision agent for maws-3f, on maws-3f's rulings.
- Decision (U1 resolved, 2026-10-19): the vendor's screenshot sizing rule, read statically from the pinned helper (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §15, which gives the function addresses):
  - **The rule.** `updateSkyshot` reads Statsig dynamic config `406601636`, and the size function computes `CGWindow.bounds` × `CGWindow.scale`. The scale is the best-matching `NSScreen`'s backing scale factor, or 2.0 when none matches. The size passes as `imageSize` to `SkyshotOperation.capture`, whose ScreenCaptureKit path sets the stream configuration's width and height from it, each side rounded up. No max-pixel-size scaler is on the path, and the JPEG writer sets only the quality (0.8).
  - **The legacy fit.** With `should_use_legacy_screenshot_sizing` on (code default false), the image is fitted to a long side of at most 2048, then a short side of at most 768, in points when `should_normalize_screenshot_to_point_resolution` holds (default true). Read on both Macs on 2026-10-19, the cached config (rule `default`, version 9) carries quality 0.8, JPEG and point normalization, and no legacy key.
  - **The image covers the bounds**, stored as the observation's `visibleRect`. An off-screen window goes through SkyLight's `captureWindowImagesInRect` and is redrawn to the same size, truncated rather than rounded up.
  - **The measurements fit.** At 2× the rule gives exactly the four measured sizes: TextEdit 1220 × 1720, Finder 1840 × 872, Electron 1440 × 1120, and the baseline's 1172 × 976. The legacy fit cannot produce a short side above 768, so it is ruled out for every one of them.
  - **The premise was wrong.** The baseline was taken on the mini, as `docs/evidence/2026-10-14-native-baseline/run-1/meta.json` records (`"device": "mini"`, Mac14,12, scale 2), so the MacBook has no recorded vendor size.

  Ruled: cua keeps ScreenCaptureKit and the screenshot ruling of the same day. M1a aligns the two places it differs, the screen's backing scale factor (2.0 when no screen matches) and rounding up, and re-reads config `406601636` on the mini before recording the renderer goldens. Alternative: keep the display mode's pixels per point and rounding to nearest, which are identical at 2× with integral bounds. Lost because parity in the fractional and multi-display cases costs two lines, and the goldens are compared byte for byte.

  Still open, none gating M1a:
  - which screen supplies the scale when a window spans displays (the reducer was not read);
  - which window supplies the bounds upstream of `updateSkyshot`;
  - whether the model API downsizes on its side;
  - OpenAI may flip the legacy flag server-side; the re-read before the goldens catches that.

 
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, maws-3f.
- Decision (second input sitting, 2026-10-20: the mini, 2026-10-19 19:17–20:42Z, build b99ea967 at the approved location, the harness of `feat/117-sit2`, merged as f3b2ae8e; every figure below is from `spike/sit2/` and its `sitting-notes.md`). The rulings, each with its strongest alternative:
  1. **Keys by route 2′.** `press_key` and `type_text` go by the plain post, no fake activation. Measured: route 2′ delivered keys on all four scenes, three of three by outcome, with zero became-active, resigned-active, window, page or text focus and blur events on the Electron fixture, where route 3 fired one of each per key. Alternative: route 3, the vendor's rule for every keyboard action. Lost because it fires the app's activation hooks on every key for no delivery gain.
  2. **Synthesized clicks by route 3; the clicks' ladder 1 → 3 → 4; route 2 leaves the defaults.** Measured: route 3 is the only click route that delivered on all four scenes (element and coordinate clicks, three of three each); route 2′ failed on TextEdit (the caret unmoved) and on Chrome (the checkbox and the focus unchanged); the ⌘-click failed on TextEdit and changed Finder's outcome (`["003"]` → `["003", "005"]`, an addition to the selection where a plain click selects one row). When route 3's guard fails the click is refused as `activationFailed`, not retried with ⌘, because ⌘ changes what a click means. Alternative: a per-app route-2′ list (Finder and Electron took it). Lost because there is no general predicate for which apps accept a plain post, and a wrong entry fails silently; the vendor's ⌘ retry lost on the Finder outcome.
  3. **Route 3's activation click is skipped when the window's `AXActivationPoint` lies outside the window's frame**, a declared difference from the vendor. Measured: TextEdit's, Finder's and Electron's points lie 16 points below the top edge inside the frame and hit the window itself; Chrome's is (−1, 1441), the screen's bottom-left corner, and the hit-test there answers Chrome's menu bar; route 3 delivered on Chrome without the click. Alternative: post the pair wherever the point is, as the vendor. Lost because a click into Chrome's menu bar is a real click the model never asked for.
  4. **The activation stays per action in M2a.** Measured: the hooks fire once per route-3 action (the Electron counters) and a selection set through `selectText` survives the activation and a following key on TextEdit, Chrome and Electron; with keys on 2′ only synthesized clicks pay for the pair. Alternative: a believed-active state kept across a burst, as the vendor keeps it (A1(iii)). Stays open, to be decided from M2b's matrix rather than guessed.
  5. **U5, decided by the owner (2026-10-20): intervention detection requires Input Monitoring, through the listen-only tap.** Measured: both tap kinds are created under Accessibility alone and receive nothing (0 of 1 own, 0 of 3 driven); with Input Monitoring both receive everything (1 of 1, 3 of 3); the HID idle counter is reset by the engine's own posts (420–783 ms after a post, 31–37 s before), so the HID-idle fallback cannot see the user during an action, the case intervention exists for. Without the grant the engine runs with intervention detection off, and `status`, `doctor` and the action meta (`cua/intervention: "off"`) say so; acceptance 1's permission keys gain `inputMonitoring`; onboarding grants three permissions by hand. Alternatives: the active tap (the same grant, and a callback in the event path that could delay the user's input); the HID-idle fallback (blind during an action); refusing to run without the grant (lost: every other promise holds without it, and the state is reported).
  6. **U7, closed.** The engine's Accessibility request is denied by `universalAccessAuthWarn` within about 40 ms with no window shown (TCC's stream, 20:27:50.98Z, the fresh id `io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine.u7`); the inference, marked as one, is that an `LSUIElement` app launched with `-g` has nothing the prompt can be presented for, which explains the first sitting's missing prompts, and the fresh id showed the engine's own id is in no special state. The fresh id's first capture after approve's relaunch raised replayd's `SCAlert` user-acknowledgement alert (20:38:30.74Z); the owner reports Allow, replayd logged "refused" at 20:38:41.79Z; both kept. Onboarding is grants added by hand, and M4 gains one lead: the approve step's requests from a foreground launch. Alternative: keep requesting from the background launch and hope. Lost on the stream.
  7. **U8, closed; the owner's "every update needs someone at the Mac" fork resolves favourably for this evidence.** The same-path replacement (b99ea967 over e3547c25), the staged same-path rebuild (a25da09a) and the new-path copy raised no confirmation and kept both grants; replayd logged no `SCAlert` line, tccd no "Notifying for access", the console showed no window; the approvals plist carried nothing at four reads. `buildEngine` expects no confirmation for a same-identity rebuild and detects one through replayd's log if it appears. Alternative: keep assuming the owner at every update (the first sitting's rule). Lost because three moves in one controlled hold raised none and the detection covers a recurrence; the first sitting's asymmetry stays unexplained and unrepeated.
  8. **Closed by the sitting:** U2 (two coordinate clicks exact on lines 0 and 8, exact after the window moved, the two refusals as ruled); U3 (the first sitting's refusals were the harness's stray `(en-US)` argument, which Chrome opened as a second tab, e0751a31; Chrome's background clicks by route 1 where advertised and by 3 or 2 but not 2′, keys by every route, probe (e)'s Latin typing 7 of 8; the owner's Chrome route-4 fork is resolved as not needed); U4 (Electron's background key and click delivered by every route); U6 (the owner's keystroke over Screen Sharing and the click into the background window were both attributed to the driven app inside the action's interval, 6 of 6 and 1 of 1, the engine's own 10,009 events counted as its own; the rule is M2a's); U9 (the four-scene triple in "Screenshot": Finder and Chrome far ahead, TextEdit and Electron behind by the capture floor of 55–58 ms, the private window-server capture the later lever); U10, U12 and U13 (items 1–4).
  9. **Acceptance changed** by the owner's decision: item 1's permission keys gain `inputMonitoring`. The milestone cut is unchanged; M1a's gates (U1, U2, U3) are cleared and M1a is next.
  10. **Open notes that gate nothing:** the vendor's Chrome tree on the mini shows 61 elements against the MacBook's 45 with empty page containers (`spike/sit2/u9/vendor/run-*/goldens/chrome.text.txt` against the MacBook entry in Surprises), so the "vendor exposes no web content" finding needs re-reading from those goldens before it is cited again; *(closed 2026-10-19, M1a.3: re-read from those goldens, the vendor exposed both Chromium pages fully on the mini, Chrome's and Electron's controls line for line with cua's; "vendor exposes no web content" stays only as the MacBook first-series observation, cause unknown)* A1(iii), the kept activation, as in item 4; the phase-5 vendor lane's capture confirmation for the scratch home's helper copy went unreported.
  11. **Residue:** `codesign` over SSH fails with `errSecInternalComponent` because the login keychain is locked in an SSH session even after the owner's `security set-key-partition-list` at the console (`spike/sit2/phase0.txt`), so M1b's prerequisites gain an unlock step (superseded 2026-10-20 by M1b's signing route: the GUI-domain sign and the owner's Always Allow); `grants-watch.mjs` must redact the certificate's common name itself (the sitting's streams were redacted on collection); `assemble.mjs`'s main-module check should compare real paths; phase 3 ran 13 minutes over its 30-minute estimate (the dispatching session's timing).
  Rationale: the sitting's outputs, read against the rulings; every claim above has a row or a log line under `spike/sit2/`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, the design-revision agent for maws-3f, on maws-3f's rulings and the owner's U5 decision.
- Decision (M1a pre-flight, 2026-10-19): M1a runs as five tasks (Progress), M1a.1 → M1a.2 → M1a.5 in this worktree and M1a.3 and M1a.4 beside them in their own worktrees, since they touch disjoint files; M1a.1 is reviewed before M1a.2 dispatches, and all four before M1a.5, which consumes them. Five gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **Route 4 and `cua/routeAdvice` are M2a's.** M1a's text says "the routes M0 promoted" (1, 2′, 3), and M2a's Decisions carry route 4's reachability by the per-app override; route 4 activates an app by definition and no scene needed it. M1a builds the clicks' ladder 1 → 3 with `activationFailed`, and does not read `routes` from `engine.json`. Alternative: build route 4 now behind the override; lost because it would ship unmeasured and unreachable.
  2. **The withdrawn spike switches leave the engine.** `prefetch: "hierarchy"` (with the private `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` resolution) and the reduced `readSet` go, since the design withdrew both; `spikeRoute` stays as the forced-route spike tool the Input section keeps for route 2 (Interfaces' value `2`); `probe_tap` and `probe_spike` stay until M2a's tap and `UserProbe` replace them. `scripts/spike/engine/`'s client, service, render and `measure` stay M0's record at M0's wire and are not ported; its scenes, fixtures and `front-window.swift` are M1b's to promote.
  3. **The numbering lives in the service and is passed to the pure functions.** Interfaces write `render(skyshot) → string`, but the model's indexes persist per lineage (never reused, new elements take the next unused index), so the service keeps per lineage the token → index map, the next index and the retired set, and passes it in: `render` and `diff` stay pure functions of their inputs and the goldens pin them.
  4. **The peer check is one function selected by `peerPolicy`** in `~/.config/cua/engine.json`, whose only accepted value in #117 is `uid` (absent means `uid`; any other value stops the engine at start with a sentence naming the setting), so #174's trusted-ancestor policy is a second value (the owner's A2 answer).
  5. **M0's goldens are re-keyed, not re-recorded.** The renderer goldens come from the second sitting's four-scene captures (`spike/sit2/u9`, both lanes on the same scene states in one sitting); their raw `Skyshot` trees carry the spike engine's indexes, which were stable exactly where a token is, so each index is mapped to a token mechanically (Interfaces, Node). The vendor's sizing config was read at 17:30Z on 2026-10-19; goldens committed on a later day re-read it first (the Plan of Work's M1a).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.1, engine core, 2026-10-19; 41de5400, review fixes f04664ac): the gaps the engine core settled.
  1. **Tokens are `"t<n>"`**, n the lineage's monotonic allocation number, opaque to the service; the prefix makes a service that forgets to translate (sending an index as a string) a malformed token, `invalidArguments`, rather than a coincidental resolution on a fresh lineage. M0's goldens re-key index n to `"t<n>"`.
  2. **An element token requires `execute.lineage`** (`invalidArguments` without it; Interfaces' `lineage?` is optional only for requests with no token), and `element_index` sent to the engine is refused with a sentence naming `element`. A well-formed token that is retired, never issued or from another lineage answers the vendor's "no longer valid" sentence; `<n> is an invalid element ID` is the service's, for indexes it never issued (M1a.5).
  3. **Invalidation is watched per owning process**, the app's and each panel service's whose elements the observation read; a layout change in any watched process invalidates every element (conservative), and a destroyed element invalidates itself (the vendor's rule), and a watch registered only after the read invalidates that process's elements alone until the next observation. The vendor watches the app only (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §3.5), which would never re-validate a panel's elements after the panel's own layout changed. A re-fetch updates the element's owning pid.
  4. **The screen behind the scale** is the one whose frame intersects the window's bounds most (the first on a tie; 2.0 when none intersects), read from a screen list the engine caches on the main thread at start and on every screen-parameter change, so a capture never waits on the main thread; `Skyshot.screenshot.scale` is that backing scale, while a coordinate click still divides by the stored image-over-frame ratio per axis. Which screen the vendor's reducer picks for a window spanning displays stays unread (U1's open list).
  5. **The deadline**: a request whose deadline has passed on receipt answers −32001 (`errorName: deadlineExceeded`) before its method is parsed; a settle still running at the deadline is cut there, starts no busy walk, answers `timeoutReached` (−10005) and stays armed for the next observation.
  6. **`peerPolicy`** is read from `$HOME/.config/cua/engine.json`; absent or `"uid"` runs the uid check, any other value or a file that is not a JSON object stops the engine with exit 78 before the lock and the socket are touched (fail closed); the reason reaches only the unified log under LaunchServices, so M1a.5's connect diagnostic points there.
  7. **The withdrawn switches** went with their only evidence (`readCensus`) and the hierarchy-only parameters; the other M0 measuring switches (`valueMarkdown: false`, `focusableSelectable: false`, `webAreaWait: false`, `settle`) stay, unsent by the facade.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.4, `src/` integration, 2026-10-19; 38425feb..146b6db8, review fixes cd773dae, merged 59a43ae0): the gaps the integration settled.
  1. **ApprovalMemory** remembers an accept only when the client's answer carries `_meta.persist` (`session`, or `always`, kept for the connection only), so `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=none` keeps meaning "ask every time"; a decline is always remembered and carries `content.source: "computer-use-persisted-state"` as a remembered accept does; cancels and failed asks are never remembered. The key is `tool_params.app` among `connector_id: "computer-use"` elicitations, so M1a.5's policy puts the resolved bundle id there, as the vendor's does. An answer that arrives after the asking worker ended is still the connection's: `workerEnded` keeps that worker's open client elicitations so the answer is recorded, and the EOF drain still cancels any left unanswered. Interfaces' ApprovalMemory line is read with the design paragraph's "accepts and declines alike".
  2. **The own host's elicitations carry `mode: "form"`**, as the vendor host's do and the harness matcher requires (`scripts/accept/lib.mjs`); this touches every own-host elicitation, the browser surface's included (Surprises).
  3. **`concealed`** is marked on macOS only (`sky_linux` gets its pinned shape). `concealedInputUnavailable` renders a fixed sentence per method: for `type_text` it names the input-source condition generically (a fixed, value-free sentence cannot carry the source id), for `paste` it says a secret is never pasted and names `typeText` and `setValue`. `inputSourceChanged`, `noScreenshotForCoordinates` and `activationFailed` on a substituted request keep the generic "may have been partly entered" sentence with the name shown.
  4. **`cua/serviceMs` is M2a's** (its Touches carry it for `src/services/sky.mjs`); M1b's comparison A judges wall time.
  5. **The seatbelt deny** covers the engine directory for the kernel only (the worker reaches the engine through the supervisor's relay), under both `HOME` and the account's real home; the hosted gate's 208 fingerprint checks are unchanged in all four profiles, so the baseline is not re-recorded (the `sandbox-live` gate is the authority). The engine directory constant lives in `src/runtime/layout.mjs` for M1b's `engine.mjs`. The relay's `-b <bundle id>` form is unchanged (`-n` on a bundle id would spawn duplicate instances of ordinary apps); `src/services/engine` is trusted only when the sky wrapper is registered on macOS.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.3, renderer, diff and goldens, 2026-10-19; 6fa99387, review fixes 50658ad0 and 76d71da4, merged 0edb075d): what the goldens settled and how they are judged.
  1. **Renderer rules pinned by the second sitting's vendor text** (marked `[golden]` in `render.mjs`, not read in the vendor's code): Booleans print `on`/`off`; numbers print as `%.16g` with trailing zeros trimmed; the menu bar prints as one `menu bar` element of its items by title, the first (the Apple menu) dropped by position; `AXShowDefaultUI` and `AXShowAlternateUI` are hidden everywhere and `AXCancel` on menu and menu-bar items; an element whose own line would print nothing dissolves into its children (Finder's radio group); `AXUnknown` prints its role description; disclosable elements carry the `Expand`/`Collapse` pseudo-actions (E §2.1); Rows and Columns print nothing for a one-row or one-column span (the Skyshot's ranges are half-open); `AXRaise` prints the English `Raise` on a Korean Mac. Inferences, not pinned: the flatten and merge separator (a space), the blank line before the selected-text note, the `lo-(hi-1)` form for spans above one (F §2.4), and the flattened role words (`radio buttons`, `rows`; E §5.2), which are not implemented.
  2. **How the goldens judge** (part 3): matched lines are compared whole, part values included, with only the loopback port normalized; Finder, whose captures were redacted to `⟨n⟩` at M0, is compared by line length with each `⟨n⟩` counted as n characters; every expected difference is declared with its exact text, an exact count, a fault and a cause, and an unneeded or over-counted declaration fails; the diff's removed IDs and `~` lines are mapped through the vendor's before text; every golden file is checked against its source capture. Faults are `engine` (expiring at M1b's re-recording, M1b's text), `redaction` (Finder's hidden `_NS` identifier prefixes and toolbar custom actions; Finder's real identifiers and action descriptions are unverified until its golden is compared as full text) and `engine or renderer` (Finder's rows print 12 characters for `Open, Confirm` where the cells print 12 for `Open`, so the vendor may drop `Confirm` by a rule cua lacks; decided by M1b's full-text comparison). Action descriptions are the system's, localized (ko on the mini).
  3. **The numbering for M1a.5** is `render.mjs`'s: per lineage, a kept token keeps its index, a new token takes the next unused one, an absent token's index retires for good, a new lineage starts at 0, and focus or selection tokens outside the tree are numbered after it. The menu bar's parent element is the renderer's synthetic `#menuBar` until M1a.2b settles whether the engine emits the node; an index naming it is not actionable, which M1a.5 answers before forwarding.
  4. **Part 3's 10% rule needed no entry** (0 added lines; Surprises); its escape hatch cites a dated Decision Log entry or fails.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.2, engine input, 2026-10-19; 7354c39c, review fixes 8e695c51, 7329cc35 and 2050558a): the gaps the input routes settled, each read against the vendor's code where it could be (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §5.8).
  1. **Route 3 is reported for every synthesized click**, also when the app already believes it has focus and no activation runs; `activationGuardMs` is present only when a guard ran. A route-1 action is always reported as route 1 and never activates.
  2. **The guard's refusal and the front.** When the user's app loses frontmost during the guard and the front went to the driven process or the bound app (the engine's doing), the user's app is re-activated and the click refused; when a third app took the front, the click is refused and the front left alone, since bringing the old app back over the user's own switch would itself interfere (a departure from the vendor, which re-activates in both cases, §5.5). The sentence reports a restore only when it succeeded. "Nothing posted" on `activationFailed` means none of the action's events: the activation pair is posted before the guard, which watches its effect, and is withdrawn after.
  3. **The `SLSDisableUpdate` hold** is released by a timer at 100 ms from its start, whatever the guard is doing, and otherwise at the guard's end; the guard's exit test stays M0's three stable 10 ms samples, a window ordering above the user's answered with `AXRaise` and failing only at the 1 s timeout.
  4. **The route-1 gate** hit-tests against the owning process's application element. The element's own `AXWindow` (the app's primary window as fallback) carries route 3's activation and the click's window fields, so a panel's process gets its own window. A failed route-1 action is `actionFailed`, never a fall-through to route 3, which could click twice.
