  5. **The visible frame** is the vendor's walk (`FUN_10078e49c`): the element's live frame cut by each clipping node from the element up (a scroll area, a group with a page-scroll action, and the window, sheet, menu or popover where it stops), plus `AXWebArea` as cua's own, and by the element's own window. A synthesized click is refused as `cannotClickOffscreenElement` when the region is empty, when a clipping-role ancestor's frame or the element's own live frame cannot be read (the observed frame is never used), or when the hit-test at the point answers something unrelated to the element (a row under its list's column header, a sticky page header). Departures from the vendor: the point is the midpoint of the shown part (the vendor takes the frame's midpoint only when the region contains it); with no clipping ancestor cua keeps the frame where the vendor answers nil (a menu-bar item has none); a frameless ancestor refuses only when it has a clipping role (the vendor's walk answers nil for any); nothing is scrolled into view (the vendor scrolls in `prepareToInteract`'s `positionElement`, a state change deferred to M2a with `scroll`); and the reach check is cua's own. A hit that answers nothing or only a container proceeds with `pointUnconfirmed: true` in the meta, since neither confirms a cover; M2b's matrix watches for apps with coarse hit-testing.
  6. **Menu-bar items are refused** as `menuBarItemUnsupported` before anything is posted, every click shape and forced route. The vendor opens one by activating the app and recording its menu, never by a press or a synthesized click (§5.8); that path is M2a's, built with the observation of the recorded menu. An `AXPress` on a menu-bar item is not a substitute: it may enter the app's menu tracking and block (reported, unmeasured).
  7. **The forbidden check** covers the owning process's bundle and every bundle enclosing its executable (an autofill helper inside a password manager's `.app` is refused by the manager's id; frameworks excluded), and runs before routing, `type_text`'s input-source choice included. `concealed` wins over `spikeKeyed`; a concealed `paste` is refused as `concealedInputUnavailable` now. Forced spike routes for keys keep M0's meaning (2′ and 2 post without activation, 3 activates first).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.2b, the engine derivations the goldens refuted, 2026-10-19; 85341dae, review fixes 907d1213 and 0862bf2d): each predicate read from the vendor's code, with its address in the engine's comments (`Derivations.swift`), none fitted to the goldens; the hypotheses are in Surprises.
  1. **Node derivations** follow `FUN_10074cb90`: `disclosable` is AXDisclosing supported and settable, its value `expanded`; on Chromium `settable` is the role rule of `FUN_100751614` (combo box, incrementor outside a date or time field, text area, text field; radio button with tab button), taking the parent's role; `valueType` is `FUN_10074a794`'s (a switch or toggle checkbox Boolean, then `CFNumberIsFloatType`, CFBoolean, CFString, CFDate), so integral floats print `float`; `selected` is AXSelected, else AXTraits bit 3; `selectable` is AXSelected supported and settable, never on a menu or menu-bar item (`FUN_10077d1e4`), and AXSelectable is not read; a window without a URL reads AXDocument.
  2. **The header title** is the window server's name for the window, even when empty, AXTitle only when there is none (`FUN_10076e174`), `Desktop` for Finder's desktop-level window; an absent AXTitle is null, so `Unknown` comes from the renderer as the vendor's `llmDescription` defaults it. The window server's name needs Screen Recording: without the grant the engine falls back to AXTitle, as the vendor's process would.
  3. **Focus** is the app-level AXFocusedUIElement only (`focusedUIElementContext` 0x100774a9c); `timings.focusRead` records why a read named no node, a diagnostic.
  4. **Action descriptions** follow `FUN_1007816a0`: a fixed English word for 18 actions (`FUN_100783260`; Press, Pick, Increment, Decrement, Confirm, Cancel, Show Default UI, Show Alternate UI, Raise, Show Menu, Scroll to Visible, the four page scrolls, Delete, Expand, Collapse), then a custom action's `Name:` text (`FUN_100783928`, NSScanner semantics), then the system's `AXUIElementCopyActionDescription`, then the action's name. The Skyshot carries `actionDescriptions` with null for the first two (the renderer holds the words; the system is not asked for them), cached per walk by (pid, action). The system's descriptions are localized (ko on the mini).
  5. **filterActions** is `FUN_100782c24`, in the renderer (`keepsAction`): `AXShowDefaultUI` and `AXShowAlternateUI` never; `AXCancel` not in a Catalyst app, nor on the menu bar or a menu item; `AXPick` not on a menu item; `AXConfirm` not on a text field; a page scroll only along an axis whose scroll bar the element answers; `AXIncrement` and `AXDecrement` not where the value is settable (witness +0xb8, `isValueSettable`). The engine carries `Node.scrollBars` and `app.catalyst` (`isCatalystApp` 0x100704c88, `_AXExhibitsMacCatalystBehavior`; that this is the context's flag is an inference) and leaves `actions` unfiltered. This supersedes M1a.3's item 1 on actions: the menu-bar-item half of "`AXCancel` on menu and menu-bar items" had no evidence, since the vendor clears every menu-bar item's actions. **M2a's secondary-action validation uses the same predicate**, as the vendor refuses an action filterActions removed as "not a valid secondary action" (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §5).
  6. **The menu bar** is the AXMenuBar node followed by its top-level items without index 0 (the Apple menu, never read) and without any whose title contains `Debug` (`addMenuBarWithImmediateItems` 0x1007569b8), each read through its listed attributes (`FUN_1007542cc`), and a menu-bar token re-resolves through the same read over the same candidates; the renderer prints the items with their actions cleared. cua's own choice: a bar whose read fails gives no menu-bar nodes, an item whose read fails is skipped (the vendor builds a node from whatever the read returns). A click on the bar's node is refused as `menuBarItemUnsupported`, whose sentence names `pressKey` with the app's shortcut. This supersedes M1a.3's synthetic `#menuBar`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-19, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1a.5, the service, 2026-10-20; 4fa99fa3, review fixes 813bd229): the gaps the service settled.
  1. **Errors** carry the engine's own `errorName` when it sends one, else the vendor's code-to-name mapping, and every engine error reads `Computer Use server error <code>: <reason>`, the helper's formatter (`R/A-contract.md` §1.5); this supersedes `R/sky-behaviour.md` §23 item 6's proposal of bare sentences for the rest (no vendor capture pinned either form; the harnesses match substrings). An index on an app this connection has not observed answers "no longer valid" (§8, "No observation yet"); a never-issued index on an observed lineage answers `<n> is an invalid element ID`. A forbidden app refused by the engine is re-raised as a plain `Error` with the engine's sentence, as the vendor's policy raises its refusal, and the policy also checks the resolved bundle id. A service-side refusal (a bad index) carries no engine meta.
  2. **The client** owns `withSuspendedTimeout` (around the connect, with any launch wait, and every request except the policy's `resolve_app` and `list_apps`, which run on the budget as the vendor's do), and its transport timer waits 2 s past the request's deadline, so the engine's own `timeoutReached` arrives first and the connection keeps its lineages. `cua/engineMs` is the method's own round trip, null when the method never reached the engine.
  3. **The policy's generated Swift** (`Policy.generated.swift`, from `scripts/generate-engine-policy.mjs`, so the module the trusted worker loads writes no files) carries only the forbidden sets, their sentences and the check; the risk table stays JS-only. The high-risk subtitle names "Computer Use" where the helper's names ChatGPT.
  4. **Screenshots** go to `CUA_ENGINE_SCREENSHOTS`, `<run>/<session>/screenshots`, which the launcher names on macOS computer launches and the engine creates (0700, refusing a symbolic link in its place, files opened `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` under random names), so the sandboxed worker creates nothing in any mode and a cell can only break its own observations; the run directory is reaped with the connection. A missing or relative setting fails `[engine_not_configured]` before anything is asked.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-20, M1a.5, the coordinating session's rulings): (1) **The hosted runner grants the engine Accessibility**, so part 7's untrusted-engine path is proven by a fake engine on hosted CI and live on the mini (M4's onboarding), not by the real engine on the hosted runner; the real-engine test stays, gated to GitHub-hosted runners and skipping where the grant is present (Surprises). Live accessibility observation on a hosted runner is residue for its own ticket. (2) **iPhone Mirroring (`com.apple.ScreenContinuity`) leaves the forbidden list**: the list is the vendor's (E §6), the vendor drives that app (its own bundle-id constant, an AppInstructions file, a 5 ms typing gap; F §6.2), and cua_repl on the vendor engine allows it today, so keeping it would add a refusal rather than carry one over; the owner may overrule; the owner confirmed 2026-10-20 (it stays allowed). (3) **A text observation needs Screen Recording**: the facade sends the same `get_app_state` for `getAXState` and `getScreenshot`, and the service asks for the screenshot on every observation, as the vendor captures on every one (F §4.8, §15), so an engine with Accessibility but no Screen Recording fails `permissionsPending` on a text observation too; that the vendor does the same is an inference (its `requiredPermissions` gate was not read). Whether a text-only fallback is worth a facade change is M4's question, with the permission prompts. (4) Screenshots are written into the session's run directory, as "Screenshot" says, never into a directory the sandboxed worker creates.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's rulings.
- Decision (2026-10-20, M1b's signing route): `buildEngine` signs through a one-shot launchd job in the user's GUI domain whenever it has no GUI keychain (every SSH session), and the owner's one-time step is **Always Allow** on `codesign`'s key prompt at the console, replacing `security unlock-keychain` per SSH session (Install, signing and permissions; M1b's prerequisites; Concrete Steps).
  Rationale: over SSH the login keychain is locked (the second sitting's phase 0 and again 2026-10-19T21:00Z: `errSecInternalComponent`); loginwindow unlocks it only for the GUI login, and an unlock does not carry across SSH connections (Apple developer forums threads 703523 and 712005), so the unlock route needs the owner's password at every rebuild, against `cua install --engine` needing no one at the Mac in the ordinary case. The GUI domain has the unlocked keychain but showed that the partition list alone does not admit `codesign` (Surprises, the probe of 2026-10-19T21:03Z). Alternatives: the owner types the password into a chained `security unlock-keychain && cua engine build` per SSH build, lost because every rebuild from M1b to M5 would need the owner; re-importing the identity with `security import -T /usr/bin/codesign`, the same effect but it needs the .p12 and its password, while Always Allow needs only the login password at a prompt the first build raises anyway.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b pre-flight, 2026-10-20): M1b runs as five tasks (Progress). M1b.1 and M1b.2 touch disjoint files and run side by side, M1b.2 in its own worktree; M1b.3 consumes M1b.1's `installedEngine` and dispatches after M1b.1 is reviewed; M1b.4 consumes all three; M1b.5 is the controller's, inside a CI-opt hold the dispatching session arranges. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **The capture-confirmation watch lives in `approveEngine`.** `buildEngine` never captures, so "the first capture of the new build" ("Install, signing and permissions") is the approve step's discarded capture: `approveEngine` streams replayd's unified log around it, and an `SCAlert` user-acknowledgement line means the console holds a confirmation; `approved.json` is then written only after the operator confirms at the terminal that it was answered (the same keypress the grant steps use), and a run with no terminal fails with a sentence naming the console. With all grants true and no `SCAlert`, `cua engine approve` after a same-identity rebuild needs no one at the Mac, which is what keeps `cua install --engine` unattended in the ordinary case (U8).
  2. **The signing identity is never printed.** `pending.json` and `approved.json` hold it on the device (they are state, not evidence); every command's output, `engineStatus`, harness `meta.json` and evidence carry only whether the bundle's signature matches the configured identity (`identityMatches`) and the signature's kind (`identity` or `adhoc`), never the name or hash. Acceptance 1's `engine.signature.identity` is read in M3's doctor the same way, as a match, not the value.
  3. **A bundle's `build` is read from the bundle**, written into its `Info.plist` at assembly (as `assemble.mjs` does for the spike), so `installedEngine` and the harnesses compare `approved.json`, the bundle on disk and the checkout's computed `build` without launching anything.
  4. **The vendor engine's attestation is the launcher's.** The vendor service emits no `cua/nativeEngine`, so on a vendor-engine connection the proxy attests `{engine: "vendor", version, build}` from the launch (the vendor runtime's release and the helper bundle's version), and on a cua-engine connection it passes the service's `cua/nativeEngine` through; a connection whose service and selection disagree is a harness refusal, not a silent label.
  5. **The live goldens recorder** is `scripts/native-matrix/goldens.mjs` (M2b's directory, which M1b opens with the fixtures): on the device, in one sitting, it records per scene the vendor's text through the vendor host and engine and cua's raw `Skyshot` straight from the approved engine's socket (the device's own uid, outside any sandbox), on the same fixed states, plus the one action sequence per scene, in the file layout `test/native-goldens/PROVENANCE.md` names; both lanes drive the promoted scenes (`scripts/repl-parity/scenes-mac.mjs`) in one run directory, since TextEdit's and Electron's goldens carry the run document's and the fixture's paths. Nothing it writes may carry content of a window the run does not own. *(Revised 2026-10-20 from M1b.2's review: this item first let the vendor half reuse `scripts/spike/engine/measure.mjs --lane vendor`'s golden cells, which drive the spike's scenes and would record Finder with its chrome showing.)*
  6. **Comparison A's lane is checked before the sitting**: cua's service loaded in the vendor trusted worker must see `CUA_ENGINE_APP`, `CUA_ENGINE_SCREENSHOTS`, `nativePipe` and `launchServices` (the M1a report's open question). M1b.3 proves it on the MacBook against a fake engine already listening at the bundle's socket path, so nothing is launched and no window or prompt appears.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.2, scenes and probes promoted, 2026-10-20; 21d120c8, 547fc6d2 on `m1b/scenes`): the gaps the promotion settled.
  1. **Finder's fixed state hides the sidebar, the status bar and the path bar**, the spec's first way, so Finder's golden is full text with nothing redacted: the sidebar names the owner's places, the status bar the disk's free space (which differs between two recordings), the path bar the folders above the run's. The toggles are chosen from Finder's `ShowSidebar`, `ShowStatusBar` and `ShowPathbar` settings and sent as keys through the engine; the owner's values are saved once in `~/Library/Caches/cua-harness/finder-preferences.json`, teardown toggles them back and `down finder` (or `restore-finder`) writes back any value still changed, so a run that dies is restored by the next. The status bar and path bar are global Finder settings, so the owner's other Finder windows lose them for the run's duration. Setup also deselects all (⌥⌘A).
  2. **A fail-closed check runs on the engine's `Skyshot` before anything is recorded** (`fixedStateProblems`): it refuses a window that is not the run's folder in list view, a second outline (the sidebar), a title, value or URL outside the file list that is not the run's, and a toolbar other than Finder's default set as M0's mini capture recorded it. Unverified until the sitting: that the three toggles and ⌥⌘A act as expected and that a hidden sidebar leaves the tree; each failure refuses rather than leaks, and if the sidebar stays in the tree after hiding, the redactor that keeps the AX vocabulary is the fallback.
  3. **The fixtures are single-sourced** under `scripts/native-matrix/fixtures/`: the spike's copies were deleted and its scenes and measure lane import the promoted modules, so there is one Chrome for Testing pin and one Electron builder (one source hash, one cache path; the mini needs `build-electron` again). Every file of Finder's run folder gets a fixed past modification time (2026-01-15T12:00Z, `fixedState.modified`), so its Date Modified column, where the action clicks, prints one absolute date in every recording. Finder's window frame cannot be pinned without an Automation prompt, so both lanes of a recording use one run folder (Finder keeps a folder's frame), and the sitting verifies the frame; a placeholder inside Finder's own toolbar is not run content.
  4. **The key guard compares by pid where the caller has one**, and the process form can also name a window (Finder's keys need it); the bundle-id form stays for callers with no pid yet (`scripts/native-baseline/macos.mjs`, M1b.4; `scripts/accept/textedit.mjs`, M3).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.1, engine install and its commands, 2026-10-20; 92d48bf8): the gaps the install settled.
  1. **The assembling script `build` covers is `src/runtime/engine-bundle.mjs`**, into which the spike's assembler moved; signing and install live in other files, so editing them never changes `build`. A checkout's `build` is computed from that module (the harnesses' build check, M1b.4), not from `scripts/spike/engine/assemble.mjs`, which stays M0's record.
  2. **`pending.json` and `approved.json` record the signed bundle's leaf-certificate SHA-1, or `adhoc`.** `identityMatches` compares a configured SHA-1 with that leaf, or a configured common name with `codesign`'s first Authority line.
  3. **The GUI-domain sign job** runs `node src/runtime/engine-sign.mjs --job`; its plist carries `HOME`, and `CUA_SIGNING_IDENTITY` only when the identity came from that variable. "No GUI login" means `launchctl print gui/<uid>` failed. The wait defaults to 45 s and is set by `cua engine build --sign-wait <ms>` or `CUA_SIGN_WAIT_MS` (the flag wins; at most 30 minutes; anything else refused as `engine_sign_wait_invalid`), so the attended first sign on a Mac is answered while `codesign` is still alive (an orphaned prompt outlives its requester, and whether answering it updates the key's access list is unknown); the job is always booted out. Inside the job `codesign` is bounded by the sign wait less a margin (`jobCodesignMs`), never a fixed timeout, and a `codesign` killed at that bound reads `engine_sign_prompt_waiting`. *(Revised 2026-10-20 by the whole-M1b review's fix, 1a3cb173, and the phase-1 sign fix, 1e4a5425.)*
  4. **The capture watch** is `log stream --level info` on the SCAlert class's own lines in replayd's log (`[SCAlert `, not `SCAlertStatsManager`'s per-stream bookkeeping) around every `approve` that can capture (the first one after a same-identity rebuild captures, since the grants already read true), to 2 s after each answer, keeping only counts that are summed; a capture whose watch could not start or whose stream ended on its own is treated as an alert seen (the operator confirms). The operator answers on stdin (Enter confirms, `n` aborts, end of input means no terminal; lines typed before a question are discarded), so `ssh mini '… engine approve'` keeps stdin attached (never `ssh -n`). With a grant missing and no terminal, approve fails at once; with a terminal the grant wait is capped at 15 minutes. Input Monitoring is led like the other two grants. *(Revised 2026-10-20 by the review fixes, 91ea7ef4, and the phase-1 detector fix.)*
  5. **The replaced bundle moves to `<engine dir>/previous`**; a build is skipped only when the bundle's `build` and signature both match what was asked; before the move `buildEngine` stops whatever engine answers at the socket. `engineStatus` carries a top-level `approved`; `installedEngine` is synchronous, never throws, and reads the signature with `codesign -d` on each call.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.4b, the live goldens recorder, 2026-10-20; 3d9a6c60, review fixes b797e1ae and 82aafbad): how the goldens are re-recorded.
  1. **A controller script and a device helper over SSH** (`scripts/native-matrix/goldens.mjs`, `goldens-device.mjs`), one engine connection per device command; cua's action sequence runs in one device command so its lineage holds from observation to click to observation. Scenes are recorded one at a time with that scene's app frontmost on both lanes (the driven-app-frontmost regime), so the vendor's activation does not split the lanes' states.
  2. **The same fixed state is established by a sandwich**: cua observes, the vendor reads, cua observes again, and the two cua renderings must be equal (three attempts); vendor text leaves the device only after cua's engine has checked that state for owner content. cua's action starts only from an observation whose digest equals the kept fixed state's (loopback port normalized), checked on the device before the click and again by the controller, with bounded resets (Finder deselect-all in the same window and run folder, the frame recorded and required unchanged; the other scenes restarted); a failed start records a bounded line diagnostic in `meta.json`, never a golden.
  3. **The goldens are the raw `Skyshot` without `timings`** (diagnostics such as `focusRead` go to `meta.json`). Both checkouts, the device's and the controller's, must be at the approved `build`'s commit and clean, since the start-state digest and `check` render with the checkout's renderer. A `source.json` beside the goldens makes Finder's full-text comparison automatic on install (`redacted: []`), and `rekey.mjs` cannot overwrite live goldens.
  4. **The expiry audit** (`goldens.mjs check`): a declaration is needed in a golden when removing it changes that golden's multiset of undeclared differences; expiring declarations (engine, redaction, engine or renderer) are reported `delete:` or `ENGINE DEFECT:`, and the controller edits `expected.json` in the sitting from that output. The sizing config is read twice, before the vendor lane and after the scenes with the run's helper alive.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.3, selection, host switch and attestation, 2026-10-20; 012fb69b, d1279687, review fixes dcfa3d7e): the gaps the selection settled.
  1. **`engine` in `~/.config/cua/engine.json` is an explicit setting** (reason `settings`): it is refused as `engine_not_installed` without an approved engine, never a silent fallback, since anything the owner chose is "explicit" in the table's sense. An unreadable file is `invalid_setting`; an empty `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` reads as unset. The engine settings and the approved engine are read from the ambient `HOME` (the same functions `cua engine` uses), never from `CUA_HOME`, so a harness that keeps the account's `HOME` sets `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` per lane rather than inheriting the account's file. `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` is ignored, not even validated, off macOS computer sessions.
  2. **The host reason for cua's engine on an unset `CUA_REPL_HOST` is `cua_engine`**; the default-engine rows without an approved engine warn with reason `engine_not_installed`. `installedEngine` runs once per connection, at its selection, and only when cua's engine is in question. `SKY_CUA_SERVICE_PATH` is never set with cua's engine, so nothing in either host can open the vendor helper. Doctor's `repl.host` row appends `; native engine X (reason): …`, which the selection oracle parses.
  3. **The vendor engine's attestation** (pre-flight item 4) is `{engine: "vendor", version: <the helper's CFBundleShortVersionString>, build: <the runtime release>}`, mirroring cua's (a bundle version and what was built); the proxy stamps `js` and `js_reset` results and the service's own `cua/nativeEngine` wins when present.
  4. **Both engine lanes are proven headless on every relevant PR** (`scripts/probe-engine-lane.mjs`, steps of the `sandbox-live` gate): cua's service in the vendor trusted worker (comparison A's lane) and cua's engine on cua's own host (the target row), each against a fixture approved engine with a fake engine at its socket, asserting the host and reason, the ping at the bundle's socket, `get_app_state`'s `outputDirectory` equal to `CUA_ENGINE_SCREENSHOTS`, and the service's attestation; each host's relay is shown to pass the client's `{applicationPath}` request to its supervisor's path check with a bundle that does not exist. A real cold launch through the vendor relay is first seen in M1b.5. The vendor trusted worker's `nodeRepl` exposes `env`, `nativePipe`, `launchServices`, `createElicitation`, `setResponseMeta` and `withSuspendedTimeout`, so cua's service needs nothing the vendor host lacks.
  5. **The selection lane's engine rows use a fixture approved engine** under its scratch `HOME`, so the live oracle never reads or writes the real engine location.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.4, the baseline harness, 2026-10-20; a939c72d, review fixes 69a90800 and 31ea5760): the gaps the harness settled.
  1. **`--engine` and `--approved-engine` are required on every macOS computer run**, the vendor lane included, and every lane sets `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` to its `--engine` in the serve's environment, so the account's `engine.json` never chooses the engine under the harness. Every lane refuses unless the checkout's `build` equals the approved `build`, so a sitting is one checkout and one approved engine; the cua lanes also require the attested `build` and version to equal the approved bundle's, and the vendor lane's attested build to equal the runtime release.
  2. **The engine stop at a run's start and end lives in `prepare.mjs`**, under the admission lock; a busy engine refuses the run and undoes the preparation, and a stop that fails at the end fails the run with the approved home still restored. `prepare.mjs` therefore also stops cua's engine for its other callers (`m2-mini.sh`, the spike's vendor lane).
  3. **"A cold row is first launch by this run"** (Verification system's opening paragraph) needs a reading: no `cold.*` row touches the engine, since the first cell returns before any engine call and cua's service answers `setup` without connecting. The engine's first launch by a run lands in `warm.getState`'s first sample on both lanes, judged by the median with the maximum reported. A cua engine that fails to launch writes no row: the run records `meta.engineLaunch` and stops, so a launch failure is never a timing.
  4. **Comparison modes** are one table: A (`--engines`) permits exactly `engine` and `service` to differ, B (`--hosts`) the host and the sandbox (its mode, launcher and `TMPDIR`), C (`--end-to-end`) both, labelled as two variables; all three require one sitting and one commit on both sides. A `bind.getApp.retry` is folded into the same sample's `bind.getApp` at compare time (the bind judged as first attempt plus retry, whichever side retried; the recorded rows unchanged), so a vendor-only retry does not fail A. `compare` prints `VERDICT:`.
  5. **`setup.activate`** waits up to 5 s for the run's TextEdit to be frontmost by pid and fails the run otherwise, naming every frontmost reading it saw (the Open panel's service among them). A run that fails before its scratch document exists ends the TextEdit it launched with SIGTERM, only after proving the pid is the run's: TextEdit's executable, started after the bind began (`ps` elapsed time), and the same start time at the moment of the signal; otherwise nothing is signalled and `meta.textEdit.quitError` says why. Rows record `cua/engineMs` where present (not judged) and `meta` the approved engine's `{kind, identityMatches}`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (whole-M1b review, 2026-10-20; reviewer-high on 40e44353..88a7a37f, fixes 1a3cb173): two findings, both fixed. (1) The goldens recorder's vendor lane neither set `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` nor attested the engine, so an account `engine.json` naming cua would have loaded cua's service on the vendor host and recorded cua against itself; the lane now sets `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE=vendor` in its preflight and serve and refuses before any scene unless the first result attests the vendor engine at the runtime release (a missing attestation refuses too), the dispatching session's bar: whatever `engine.json` says, a vendor golden is the vendor's. (2) The attended first sign's wait became settable (M1b.1's entry, item 3). Rule kept for later harnesses: every lane pins the engine it means and refuses on the attestation, never on the selection alone.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-20, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b.5 sitting, 2026-10-21 00:02Z–, the mini, maws-3f's rulings; 284a9220, 6956da3d, 86cc306d, 3fcc4e49): what the live sitting fixed in the harness and the engine (the engine's fix reviewed twice; the goldens and comparison A's verdict rest on the second, reviewed build).
  1. **The engine reopens a running app that has no primary window, as the vendor does, and waits for the window.** Comparison A's cua-engine runs could not bind TextEdit: launched in the background, TextEdit opens no window (its Open panel comes only on a reopen request), so the first `get_app_state` timed out at 5 s and the retry answered `noWindowsAvailable` at once, where the vendor's retry bound in 1.3 s (3 of 3). The vendor's preparation (`0x10014c494 → 0x10014c4f0`) calls its open helper (`0x100164860 → 0x1001648c4`: `NSWorkspaceOpenConfiguration` with `activates = false`, `allowsRunningApplicationSubstitution = false`) when the app is not running, has no primary window, or has a minimized one, then `waitUntilHasPrimaryWindow` (`0x1006fbdf0`) polls the three-step selection for 5.0 s (`0x100164ae8`) at 50 ms and throws `timeoutReached` (`0x1006fbf70`). The engine now does the same for the first two cases (`preparationOpen`, Skyshot.swift); live, the retry binds in about 1 s (00:39Z). It is not an activation, so the Design's rule holds, and the Design now names the reopen because it is visible.
     Alternative: the engine's route-3 fake activation to the windowless app (maws-3f's fallback had the vendor activated). Lost because the vendor's code shows a reopen, not an activation.
  2. **The vendor's reopen of an app whose primary window is minimized is not taken**: the reopen un-minimizes the owner's window, a visible change an observation should not make. A declared difference from the vendor; a scene that needs it (an app observed while minimized) reopens the question.
  2b. **Three more boundaries, from the fix's review (maws-3f's rulings, the second fix commit):** a primary-window read that *failed* (the app could not answer, e.g. `kAXErrorCannotComplete`) is not an absence: the engine never opens such an app, retries the read until the request's deadline and then answers `timeoutReached` — a declared difference, since the vendor's failed read reads as no window and reopens (cua does not act on an app it could not read); the preparation's reads are charged to the request's deadline and no open is sent once it has passed; and the open's own process is the one observed after it, so an app replaced between resolution and the open is followed rather than its dead pid polled (`prepareTarget`, `readPrimaryWindow`).
  3. **The harness's three fixes**: Finder's absent display keys are a saved state of their own, read as macOS's default and restored by deletion (macOS 27 words the missing key differently from 26, which the read did not recognise); Chrome for Testing gets a fresh profile directory on every launch, a restart included, since a restart on the same profile came up with the AI-mode omnibox placeholder (the eligibility answer the omnibox stores in the profile; Chrome for Testing is unbranded and fetches no variations seed, so no switch applies); and the baseline's bind check allows 1 s of `ps` etime slack, since etime subtracts whole seconds and read 10 s for the TextEdit the run's own bind had launched. The after-hold review then made ownership independent of that rounding: the run re-reads TextEdit's presence immediately before the bind and refuses one already running, the age check staying as a secondary guard (24c1f01f); and a Chrome scene that fails to start removes its fresh profile once its processes have stopped, or records it for `down` (b8ec2d76).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M1b closing wave, 2026-10-21, maws-3f's instructions after the hold; 22c92a99, 2acf166b, 0281bb97, 875631b6, 32036cb5): the harness changes that let the remaining two goldens record, done off the mini before the closing hold.
  1. **Finder's unset chrome defaults follow the running macOS's major version** (`sw_vers -productVersion`): the status bar is shown unset on 27 and hidden on 26; the sidebar is shown and the path bar hidden on both. Setup and restore both read the running OS's default, so a macOS update between a dead run's save and a later restore toggles the Finder that runs now; the saved record's version is informational. An unknown or unreadable version refuses `up finder` before any window opens; the key writes need no default, so `down` and `restore-finder` still put every key back, an unset one by deletion. Unconfirmed on a device: the sidebar's unset default on 27 (the mini sets `ShowSidebar=1`) and the three defaults on 26.
  2. **Every golden observation is a settled read; Chrome's fixed state is the filled page.** The sitting's Chrome mismatch was the page's accessibility values still filling (the heading level, checkbox and radio states empty in the kept state, filled on every restart), which both engines read alike: the engine's Chromium enablement is the vendor's (AXManualAccessibility, then a wait for the web area's presence, not its contents; F §9, `FUN_1006f1670`), so the fix is the harness's (maws-3f's ruling), and whether `get_app_state` settles on content stays M2a's question. A settled read takes full observations 500 ms apart until two in a row pass the scene's checks with one digest, within 10 s, each request given the remaining budget as its deadline and no answer accepted past it; Chrome's checks refuse `page_filling` until its web area shows the filled values, since the unfilled page can hold still past 500 ms. The first fixed-state read and the start of cua's action read through a wrong window or a filling page; the two reads after a vendor capture is written (`verify`) refuse either at once, so an owner's window seen there invalidates the capture rather than being outwaited (the wave's review, P1). The controller resets the scene on `start_state`, `unstable` and `page_filling`. Applied to every scene: Electron is Chromium and a Finder toggle can still be animating.
     Alternative: CDP's load event alone (the coordinator's first direction). Lost because the DOM was long loaded when the values were still empty.
  3. **`up chrome` waits for the page's own report of `load`** (a `sendBeacon` to the run's loopback page server, `connect-src 'self'`), up to 15 s, with the failed-up cleanup unchanged. Chosen over a CDP port, which any local process could use to drive the run's Chrome.
  1b. **Item 1 withdrawn by the 03:53Z hold (2026-10-21; 889ce621, 35c40e2a, fb8d073b, ce5b0669): Finder's chrome is read off its window, not predicted from its keys.** The hold recorded TextEdit, Chrome and Electron at the first attempt (`m1b/goldens-run-incomplete-84c6eeea/`) and Finder refused three times. Read in the run's own window by structure: with `ShowStatusBar` unset the window draws the status bar, yet the first ⌘/ wrote 1 with nothing changing and only the second hid it (Finder's toggle state and its window disagreed; the path bar's and sidebar's agreed); and a `defaults write` before the window opened changed nothing, since Finder reads its chrome keys only at launch. So setup observes the run's first window before any toggle and saves the owner's visible state beside the keys (the first record wins, so a dead run's next `up` keeps the owner's), then toggles each part only while the window still shows it, at most twice, refusing otherwise; teardown toggles each part until the window matches the saved visible state, then writes the keys back as before. The detectors are structural (`#statusBar`, an AXList outside the list for the path bar, an outline besides the list for the sidebar), never text. The review added two recovery rules: the restored marker is dropped before a toggle's key is sent, and a saved record that cannot say what the windows showed (no schema, or no visible state with keys moved) is refused by `up` and restored keys-only by `restore-finder`, which says the visible chrome needs the View menu. The version table and its `sw_vers` read are gone. maws-3f's design (the observation-driven teardown is theirs: replaying the toggles cannot restore an unset key).
  1c. **The goldens' last hold (04:52Z, 0b44493e) and the vendor's capped item count (maws-3f's ruling, 2026-10-21).** All four scenes recorded at the first attempt (`m1b/goldens-final/`); the first pass refused Finder only because the recorder compared window frames by their JSON text, whose key order the engine varies (0b44493e compares the four numbers). The expiry audit deleted 23 of the 26 corrections (the 3 left are TextEdit scene-state declarations); its one `ENGINE DEFECT`, TextEdit's `windowUrl`, was an expired correction pinning M0's path, which the audit misread because it counted a removal that only removes differences as a need (fixed in the close wave). What remained is one line in Finder's fixed-state and action-before goldens: the vendor renders the list as `(showing 0-21 of 100 items)` where the list holds 205 and cua renders `of 205 items`. The vendor's N is `childCount` (`availableSubsetDescription` 0x10063e220 through `get_childCount` 0x10079e94c) over a tree `_AXUIElementCopyHierarchy` prefetched with AXChildren capped at MaxArrayCount 100 (`R/F-helper-logic.md` §1.1), so it never exceeds 100. **Declared as a vendor defect, scoped to N alone** (the line otherwise equal, the ranges equal, the vendor's N exactly min(cua's N, 100), cua's N above 100); the engine and the Skyshot keep the true count.
     Alternative: the renderer prints `min(N, 100)`, which is parity in text with no declaration and no rebuild. Lost because the vendor's number is wrong: it tells the model a 205-item list ends at 100, so a model looking for item 150 would believe it is not there, and the owner's rulings ask for parity with the vendor and for beating it at finding elements; reproducing a wrong count on purpose serves the first against the second. M1b's expiry rule is for engine-fault corrections, and this is the vendor's fault, declared as part 3 declares expected differences.
  1d. **The recorder's put-back gains the owner's front app, and its meta Finder's chrome record (the close wave, b5923385, 013f4eaa; maws-3f).** After the 04:52Z hold Finder was frontmost where Terminal had been: every scene activates its app. `record` now reads the front app before anything is prepared and re-activates it at the end when the same process still runs it and it is not a scene app (`cleanup` likewise, from preparation.json); meta.json keeps its bundle id, never its pid. meta.json also carries Finder's saved visible state and its setup and teardown toggle counts (booleans and counts only); the 04:52Z recording predates both, so its Finder evidence is the first-attempt pass, `frames.same`, and the window observed after teardown.
  4. **AutoStop's tests arm deadlines that cannot race the next line** (a 0 s deadline fired before `cancel()` on a loaded hosted runner, ci-gate 39531662385).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (landing, 2026-10-21, maws-3f): **M0, M1a and M1b land on main together, as one squash-merged PR**, after M1b closed. This departs from the Plan of Work's "each milestone lands as its own PR". The reviews that rule asks for ran: M1a's whole-branch reviewer-high (a807827a..bd1136f1), and reviewer-high on M1b (b2ccdc3b..84c6eeea), which found no material defect. The delta to 702b04d9 changes only harness, tests, goldens and docs, and its reviews were reviewer-medium on the Finder fix and reviewer-low on the close wave, both correct after their fixes. From M2a on, each milestone again lands as its own PR, on a branch cut from main.
  Rationale: M1a's goldens carried engine-fault corrections that only M1b's live recording could expire. A main holding M1a alone would have pinned corrections the design said were temporary. Squash also keeps the `native/engine/.build` blobs that 3fcc4e49 committed (about 217 MB, untracked in ccbb8242) out of main's history.
  Alternative: rewrite the branch to drop `.build` from history and land with a merge commit, keeping the per-commit history on main. Lost because the rewrite would change every commit hash the spec, the reports and the evidence cite. Those commits stay reachable through the PR's head ref (`refs/pull/<n>/head`) after the branch is deleted.
- Decision (M2a pre-flight, 2026-10-21, on branch `feat/117-m2a` from main 5941e988): M2a runs as five tasks (Progress). M2a.1 → M2a.2 → M2a.3 run in this worktree one after another, since all three touch the input path (`Input.swift`, `Methods.swift`, `Routes.swift`); M2a.4 touches the observation path and the wrapper and runs beside them in its own worktree once the vendor reading below lands; M2a.5 is the controller's, inside a CI-opt hold maws-3f grants, on the build the whole-milestone review has passed. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **Two questions carried from M1b are read in the vendor's code before M2a.4 builds them**: whether the vendor settles a `get_app_state` that no action preceded (its UI-settle wait `0x1006f7408` and every caller on the observation path, the preparation's launch and reopen included), and how its primary-window selection and capture treat a minimized window. The vendor's rule is adopted for the first; a deviation is its own entry.
  2. **The minimized primary window** (M1b.5 item 2 left it waiting out 5 s and failing): the engine never un-minimizes the owner's window. It observes a minimized primary window in place when the Accessibility API exposes its tree, **with no screenshot** (`screenshot: null`, the reason in the result's meta): a minimized window is not composited, so any off-screen capture is at best its last frame before minimizing, which no longer shows the window's state the model would act on (maws-3f, 2026-10-21); a request that cannot be served without the window on screen (a synthesized click or drag, a coordinate) is refused with a sentence naming the minimized window as the cause, while the Accessibility routes (an advertised action, `set_value`, `select_text`) still act. Where the tree itself is not exposed, the observation is refused with that sentence rather than timing out. The vendor's reopen of an app whose primary window is minimized (it un-minimizes the owner's window) is declared as an expected difference. M2a.4 builds it and M2a.5 checks it live on the TextEdit scene's own window.
  3. **The spike's `probe_tap` and `probe_spike` methods leave the engine** with M2a.1, as the M1a pre-flight entry scheduled; `scripts/spike/engine/` stays M0's record. `spikeRoute`, the forced-route tool, stays.
  4. **`--probe input-source` must select an input mode** (Korean 2-Set, `com.apple.inputmethod.Korean.2SetKorean`) as well as a keyboard layout and read it back, since part 6's `pressKey` row under each source (M2b) is driven by it (Surprises, MAWS P4-B2); M2a.5 checks the selection and the restore to ABC live, reading the per-context input preference before the switch and restoring it with the source.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2a vendor reading, 2026-10-21; the questions of the M2a pre-flight entry, read from the decompile with three helper readers and re-checked in the disassembly; the local record is the workspace's `m2a-vendor-reading.md`, folded into `R/F-helper-logic.md` and `R/sky-behaviour.md` by M2a.4). Each ruling adopts the vendor's rule unless it says otherwise:
  1. **First-observation settle.** The vendor settles an observation that no action preceded in one case only: its preparation opened the app (a launch, or the non-activating reopen of an app whose primary window is missing or minimized); then `waitUntilHasPrimaryWindow(5.0, 0.05)` and `waitForUIToSettle(delay 0.05, notificationDelay 0.25, scroll events off)` at `0x100164c08`, the settle's tree discarded. Otherwise `needsUISettleBeforeSkyshot` starts false (`0x1000181f8`) and the first observation captures at once, web-shaped or not, and nothing on the observation path waits for web content to fill (the web-area wait checks presence; the busy walk runs only inside a settle). **Adopted** (the Settle paragraph): the engine settles after its own launch or reopen, never otherwise, and adds no fill wait; a scene state that must be filled is the harness's to wait for, as M1b's settled read does. F §8.1 and §8.6 missed this site.
  2. **Minimized windows.** The vendor's primary-window selection never filters `AXMinimized`; only the preparation reads it, reopening an app whose primary window reads minimized, and the un-minimize is AppKit's reopen default (inference); every `get_app_state` runs the preparation, so the vendor effectively never observes a minimized window, and actions never reopen. Its capture takes an off-screen `SCWindow` by window id through SkyLight (what a minimized window's image holds is undetermined). With Stage Manager on, every vendor observation writes `AXMinimized = false` on an off-stage window and may perform `AXAddToStage`, its only `AXMinimized` write. **cua's rule is the pre-flight entry's item 2 unchanged**, and two expected differences are declared: the vendor's reopen of a minimized app, and its Stage Manager writes (cua never writes `AXMinimized` or performs `AXAddToStage`; an off-stage window that reads minimized falls under the minimized rule).
  3. **`perform_secondary_action`** matches the action's description (`Increment`, the word the renderer prints), exactly, first hit, against the element's live, **unfiltered** actions plus the pseudo-actions `SAIExpand` and `SAICollapse`, which toggle `AXDisclosing`; an unknown action answers `<action> is not a valid secondary action for <element>` with `<element>` the decimal element index. **Adopted**; this supersedes the M1a.2b entry's item 5 sentence that M2a validates by `filterActions` and the Plan's M2a text, revised.
  4. **`set_value`** checks settability, focuses the field (`AXFocused`, with a synthesized-click fallback), coerces the string to the current value's type (numbers through an `en_US` `NSNumberFormatter`, Booleans from `on`/`off` or `boolValue`, a switch or toggle checkbox forced to Boolean), writes, and re-fetches without reading the value back; the search-field auto-submit (a synthesized Return) is off for `node_repl`-class clients, cua's kind. **Adopted**, no auto-submit.
  5. **`select_text`** searches the rendered Markdown value first, then the attributed or plain value; case-sensitive, `prefix` and `suffix` immediately adjacent, exactly one match required; Markdown syntax at a match's edge skipped inward; UTF-16 offsets; `selection_type` `text`, `cursor_before`, `cursor_after` giving `(L, N)`, `(L, 0)`, `(L + N, 0)`; settability checked first, then the field focused as in 4. **Adopted.**
  6. **`scroll`** is one wheel event in pixels, `round(pages × max(element or window length, 100))`, `wheelCount` 1 with the horizontal delta in the second wheel, no search for a scroll area. **Adopted, one difference declared**: cua posts a horizontal scroll with two wheels so its delta is read (the vendor's may not scroll horizontally; M2b's matrix checks: if the vendor's one-wheel horizontal scroll moves nothing, it is declared a vendor defect as Finder's capped item count is, otherwise the two are equivalent and nothing is declared; maws-3f, 2026-10-21).
  7. **`drag`**: down, three dragged events (start, midpoint, end) each with its own event number, up, all aimed at the start point's window; the vendor ⌘-flags them. **Adopted with the route table's difference**: a drag into a background app takes route 3's activation like a synthesized click, never ⌘.
  8. **Element middle click**: the vendor turns it into a left click. **Not adopted**: cua synthesizes a middle click at the element's point by route 3, since a middle click means something else (a new tab on a link); a declared difference. Route 3's activation must then deliver no left-button event at the element's point: only the middle button's down and up land there, pinned by a unit test (maws-3f, 2026-10-21).
  9. **`positionElement`** (scroll into view): `AXScrollToVisible` (the action, or the attribute on cells), else the scroll bars' values, else `AXMakeScreenRectVisible`, then a poll of up to 0.5 s every 0.1 s whose timeout is swallowed; before a synthesized element click, a secondary action, `set_value` and an element scroll, not `select_text`. **Adopted.** `clickingMayCauseSelection` (the element or an ancestor below the application is selectable, or lists `AXSelectedRows`, `AXSelectedCells` or `AXSelectedChildren`) only reserves the vendor's route 3, which cua takes for every synthesized click; recorded, not built.
  10. **The menu bar.** A plain left click or `AXPick` on an `AXMenuBarItem` whose children include an `AXMenu` presses nothing and opens nothing: the vendor fakes activation for the observed window and records the item; while it is recorded, observations root the walk at that item (the item, its menu, the menu items), keep the window's header and take no screenshot; the record clears on a successful element click or secondary action, on `AXCancel` of the item, on `menuClosed`, on a new record and on deactivation; no `AXCancel` or Escape is sent. F §5.8 corrected (the walk consumes the recorded item). **Adopted, with no activation**: a background app's menu items are read and pressed through Accessibility without it, and cua's activation is per action; the check that decides it (maws-3f, 2026-10-21) is M2a.5's: on the background TextEdit scene window, an item that AppKit validates against the key window's responder chain (Edit > Select All, or Format > Font > Bold with text selected), its enabled state and its effect compared with the vendor's on the same item; if cua's reads disabled or does nothing, the menu path takes activation, the vendor's held fake activation where cua can hold it without bringing the window to the front, else route 3's per-action activation. Items the app itself handles (About, Settings) cannot show the problem. The vendor's sentence for an unknown secondary action is kept verbatim.
  Still open, run time only: a minimized window's SkyLight capture, the vendor's horizontal scroll, lazily filled menus under the recorded root.

- Decision (M2a.1, intervention, lock screen, route 4, the probe, 2026-10-21; aa3531c0): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The lock check runs on every `execute`**, observations and every input method included (its refusal carries the input meta on all of them); `ping`, `status`, `approve` and `shutdown` are exempt so the harness and the doctor work while locked.
  2. **The tap's lifetime**: started on the first driven app when the Input Monitoring preflight passes, torn down when no connection drives any app (start and teardown atomic with registering a drive). **The re-query lock** is per connection, a cleared ledger sequence per app: "user changed" means an attributed event newer than the last observation that cleared it, the vendor's sticky flag made per connection. The clear is taken at the tree read and applied only when the observation answers (a failed capture keeps the lock); the debounce's expiry is judged at the read too, so a debounce that expires during the capture leaves the lock set and the next observation clears it, a conservative reading of "expired by the time the observation completes" (the task's review).
  3. **`hidIdleMs`** is the milliseconds since the last event attributed to the driven app, **null when none has been**; with detection off it stays the machine-wide HID idle (Interfaces revised: nullable).
  4. **`entered`** is omitted for a concealed request (it would reveal the secret's length); otherwise it rides on the `type_text` result and on the error that stopped it. In-flight checks run between units and between key presses (never between a key's down and up) and between route 3's activation and its click.
  5. **The call order for every input method**: read the guard (`interventionGuard`), apply the meta, refuse (`checkBeforePosting`), then check in flight between events (`checkInFlight`), so a pre-action refusal still carries its attributed `hidIdleMs`. M2a.2 and M2a.3 keep it.
  6. **Route 4** applies to synthesized clicks only, activates the bound app's process, posts, and restores the previous app and its key window, skipping both when the app is already frontmost; `routes` in `engine.json` accepts only the number 4 per bundle id, anything else stops the engine with exit 78 as `peerPolicy` does; `routeAdvice` counts route 3's guard failures per bundle id engine-wide, a success resetting it, and `status` lists the apps for the doctor. Unmeasured live: whether an `LSUIElement` engine's activation is honoured under macOS 14's cooperative activation, and whether a canvas app needs a pause before the restore (M2a.5).
  7. **The wrapper** tells the two `userIntervened` sentences apart by their fixed openings: the still-interacting refusal always says nothing was entered; the user-changed one depends on whether anything was posted.
  8. **The probe**: `focus`'s `valueHash` is SHA-256 over a string or attributed-string value, null for any other type; `input-source` exits 3 unless the source it reads back is the one it selected.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2a.4, settle, the minimized window, enablement, `cua/serviceMs`, 2026-10-21; 87571ece on `m2a/settle`): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The open settle** (the vendor-reading entry's item 1) samples every 50 ms, discards its tree and reports `capped` as the action settle does; the request's deadline cancels it as `timeoutReached`.
  2. **The minimized window.** "The tree is not exposed" reads as a minimized window listing no `AXChildren`, checked before the walk and refused as `windowMinimized` (the wrapper admits the name). Otherwise the observation proceeds with no screenshot and `screenshotOmitted: "windowMinimized"` on the Skyshot, surfaced as `cua/screenshotOmitted`; the facade and the model's text are unchanged, and the input refusal is the sentence the model acts on. A click, drag or scroll at a point in a minimized window (an element click judged by the element's own window; a scroll always, being a wheel event at a point) is refused with a sentence naming `setValue`, `selectText` and `performSecondaryAction`. **Keys and typed text are not refused**: they go to the process, whose menu key equivalents act with no window on screen; whether plain typing lands is M2a.5's live check. The live check decides it (maws-3f, 2026-10-21): with the window minimized the hold types into it and reads the document's value through an observation without un-minimizing, and tries one ⌘ equivalent that needs the window (⌘A, the selection read back). If the value is unchanged, `type_text` and a `press_key` with no ⌘ modifier are refused with the minimized sentence when the target's primary window reads minimized (a deterministic precondition on the read already made, unit-tested, needing no second hold), ⌘ chords still passing unless ⌘A also showed no effect, in which case they are refused too; if the value changed, the build stands with the evidence recorded.
  3. **Enablement** is re-read on every observation and set unless it reads true; an unreadable value is set but **not owned**, so never released (the critique's A3 limits ownership to a value cua read as false; corrected from the task's first build by its review); a release that fails keeps ownership for a later retry; ownership persists through later reads of true; the release runs when the last connection that observed the process closes and at shutdown, skips a pid that is now another launch, and forgets the web area so a re-enable waits for the page again. *(Amended 2026-11-01, Decision Log #244: an attribute the engine owns is neither read nor written again until the release writes it back; one that read true or was unreadable is still read at every observation.)*
  4. **`cua/serviceMs`** is the wrapper's time around the whole `handleRpc`, secret read included, on success and on error, set on macOS only (the Linux parity surface stays unchanged). The baseline harness records `cua/engineMs` but not `cua/serviceMs`; M2a.5 adds the one row field (recorded, not judged) so comparison A's transport separation is readable, a harness touch the Plan's "does not touch the harnesses" yields to, since the design names the separation as comparison A's.

- Decision (M2a.2, the remaining methods, 2026-10-21; f4934c59, 423c44f6 on `m2a/methods`): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **Scroll into view runs before every element action**, route-1 clicks included (F §16.8: the vendor's element click scrolls first whichever route follows, and the route-1 gate's hit-test means something only once the element shows), and does nothing when the element's frame midpoint already shows. Unread in the vendor and chosen here: the scroll-bar fraction from the scroll area's `AXContents` frame (a target before the view aligned to its start, after it to its end, longer than the view to its start, clamped), and a page-scroll secondary action pressing the bar's `AXDecrementPage`/`AXIncrementPage` child before the action itself.
  2. **`set_value`'s coercion** takes an integer field's parsed number as is, parses a date as ISO 8601, and refuses with value-free names (`invalidNumber`, `invalidDate`, `unsupportedValueType`, `valueNotSettable`); the focus fallback click reports the method's route (1) with its guard meta. **`select_text`** searches the Markdown only where the observation would have rendered it (a text field or area, value at most 2,000 UTF-16 units).
  3. **`scroll` with neither element nor point** aims at the primary window's centre (the vendor's virtual cursor has no counterpart); every scroll goes by route 2′ (the vendor activates nothing for a scroll); an element with no visible point is refused as `cannotClickOffscreenElement`.
  4. **The menu-bar record** posts nothing, reports `inputRoute: 1` with a null interval and arms no settle; it needs a plain single left click (any other menu-bar click keeps refusing, the sentence naming the plain click); `menuClosed` clearing is not built, since cua never opens the menu. A menu-rooted observation retires the window's tokens for that revision (N = 1), so the window's indexes renew after the record clears, the vendor's identity rule.
  5. **Route 3's activation spares every non-left synthesized click's target** (right as well as middle): no left event lands on the element's visible frame (for a coordinate click, the hit element's frame, else a 1-point square at the point), generalizing maws-3f's middle-click check.
  6. **The unknown-secondary-action sentence** is finished by the service with the model's index (`<action>` bounded to 256 characters); the engine's own text ends "for the element".
  7. Not built, as M1a left the coordinate click: the vendor's `AXPress` on a menu found under a coordinate click's or drag's point.
  8. **From the review's fixes**: `set_value` re-checks its element after the write matching without the value (F §16.4), taking the replacement handle or failing; when scroll into view reports a scroll, the target is resolved again at one boundary, its owning process re-checked against the forbidden list; the focus fallback click keeps its interval and arms the settle once posted; every write and perform goes through `InputMeta.posting`; `select_text` arms the settle once its write is made, accepted or not. The wrapper admits the names a concealed `set_value` can stop under, `invalidNumber` and `invalidDate` among them: the model learns only that the secret does not parse as the field's type, which it needs to stop retrying it there.

- Decision (M2a.3, paste and `type_text`'s paste route, 2026-10-21; 04b462fc on `m2a/paste`): the gaps the task settled.
  1. **The engine-wide FIFO paste lock** covers route 3's activation as well as snapshot through restore, as the vendor's serial queue does; waiting for it is bounded by the request's deadline (`timeoutReached`), and the in-flight intervention check runs as soon as it is taken.
  2. **The interval** covers only the ⌘V and Return posts (through `InputMeta.posting`): a paste stopped before ⌘V keeps both times null. The activation goes to the app's process and primary window, ⌘V to the process owning the focused element (the vendor's split); a paste's activation failures do not count toward `routeAdvice`.
  3. **`type_text` by paste** pastes each line and presses Return for each newline under one snapshot and one restore, reports `entered`, and checks no source between lines; `paste` carries no `entered`. Every ⌘V and Return press posts all four of its events, so modifiers are always restored.
  4. **New error names**, each with the vendor's sentence verbatim: `clipboardWriteFailed`, `clipboardReadTimedOut`, `clipboardConflict`.
  5. **Formats with Apple frameworks only**: Markdown to HTML through Foundation's Markdown parser and its presentation intents; HTML to RTF and plain text through AppKit's import on the main thread, bounded at 5 s, converted before the pasteboard is touched. Every pasteboard call runs on the main thread. Recorded costs: the HTML import may fetch an `<img>` URL inside the bound, and the first `html` or `md` paste after an engine launch takes about 4 s while the import warms up.

- Decision (menu activation held from the record, 2026-10-21, maws-3f; revises the M2a vendor reading's departure "the menu bar is recorded with no activation"): **cua takes the vendor's held activation for a background app's menu.**
  **The hold's evidence** (M2a.5 hold item 3; `m2a/live/menu/result.json`): in a background TextEdit, the vendor's Edit > Select All and Format > Font > Bold both acted and read enabled. cua's results:
  - switch off: Select All read disabled, and Bold was not found;
  - switch on, with route 3's activation around the press: both still read disabled and had no effect (route 1, guard 36 and 54 ms).
  **Why per-action activation failed:** it came after the menu had already worked out its enablement. The app computes item enablement when the recorded menu is observed, and the vendor's activation is already held at that point. Without it, cua's menu commands did nothing on a background app while the vendor's worked: a functional regression, and parity wins.
  **The owner's front is never taken.** The fake activation sends the app an activation event and keeps the user's app in front; it is not a real app switch.
  - **Trigger and span as the vendor's** (vendor reading §4, items 2 and 6). Route 3's fake activation (`safelyEnforceActiveState`; the user's front app is kept in front) runs only at the menu-bar record: a plain click or AXPick on an AXMenuBarItem with an AXMenu child. It never runs on an ordinary observation. It holds through the recorded menu's observations and the item press.
  - **Release, a declared difference in the owner's favour.** The vendor deactivates only at its lifecycle end. cua posts route 3's deactivation when the menu interaction ends: after the item press, at the first request aimed elsewhere (`resolve_app` included), when the record clears, or after a **120 s idle bound** (revised by maws-3f from 10 s).
  **Why 120 s.** Holding costs almost nothing: the activation is fake, the owner's front is never taken, and the vendor holds forever. Releasing early costs the feature, because a press that arrives after one slow model turn does nothing, and thinking turns often run 30–90 s. The other three release points end almost every real interaction; the bound only catches an abandoned menu, and for that case 120 s against 30 s makes no difference to the owner. A press after an idle release is never a silent no-op: it gets a clear answer and presses nothing.
  **Activations are shared per target process and reference-counted across connections** (af91e109), so one connection's release never withdraws another's held menu. Front-app restore is activation-only. No AX touches any other app.
  - **Verification.** Unit tests cover the span and the release. The live check is in M2b's matrix against the vendor, on Select All and Bold. `spikeMenuActivation` is removed (9c58dd0e).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2b pre-flight, 2026-10-21, on branch `feat/117-m2b` from main 885d7d8e; brief from maws-3f): M2b runs as six tasks (Progress), and M2a's residue (its report's Residue section, the "M2a.5 hold" entry, the held menu activation) becomes matrix cells. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **The first hold comes first and may precede the matrix.** M2b.1a adds a key-code-only variant of `--probe keystroke` (`--key-codes-only`: the same key codes and flags from the current layout, no `CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`) and runs the HID composition row in both variants in one hold under Korean 2-Set, the TextEdit scene frontmost and key, recording per variant the AX value (가, rk or ㄱㅏ), the probe's JSON and the layout id, beside paste live. MAWS P4-B2 reads it: with key codes only, `rk` means the input method was bypassed and 가 that it composed. M2b.1b adds the rest of the residue on the same live harness (`scripts/native-matrix/live*.mjs`, M2a.5a's): route 4's live cells, `select_text` on a minimized window read a second time so the write and the read are told apart, the enablement release judged by Chromium's own accessibility mode (never the attribute's readback), the held menu activation against the vendor on Select All and Bold (replacing the switch-on lane, whose switch is gone), and the horizontal scroll against the vendor's one-wheel scroll (item 6 of "M2a vendor reading"). These cells are also reachable as `native-matrix.mjs cell <name>`, and `summary` reads their results.
  2. **Route 4 is driven without the owner's `~/.config/cua/engine.json`**: through a prepared home the engine is launched under, or through the request, whichever the engine's launch path allows without touching that file on the mini.
  3. **The matrix drives both lanes through the real facade on a host** (`--host`, `--engine`), as the baseline harness does, so its speed cells are in-cell; the device-side oracles (front window, activation watcher, `--probe focus`, `--probe keystroke`) are separate processes, as part 5 defines them. M2b.2 builds the framework, the method matrix in both regimes, the speed and finding-elements cells, the non-interference oracle with the key guard's user-window case, `compare` and `summary`; M2b.3 adds the probe-driven cells (concurrent use and its sheet variant, the two positive intervention cells), the Korean and Unicode matrix with its source and per-context save and restore, and the settle cell; M2b.4 the `native-mac` parity suite with `--engine`, the approved-runtime and approved-engine route on macOS, the darwin native command and the modulo-renumbering diff with its `summary` line. M2b.4 touches disjoint files and runs beside M2b.2 and M2b.3 in its own worktree.
  4. **Holds.** Every GUI step on the mini runs inside a CI-opt hold maws-3f books, from the checkout `~/Developer/GitHub/cua-parity`, with `pgrep -x SecurityAgent` and `pgrep -f 'claude auth status'` empty before it starts (friction tracker #177), and ends with the put-back M2a.5 defined (engine stopped, no scene app or Chrome for Testing, Finder's keys and in-memory chrome, the input source and per-context preference, the front app by `lsappinfo`), whose readings go to maws-3f, who releases the hold.
  5. **Review voiding.** A hold's result is voided only by a finding that changes what the measured path binds, captures or returns; every other finding is fixed afterwards or logged in `tech-debt-tracker.md`. A whole-milestone reviewer-high runs before the PR, which lands by squash, references #117 and carries no Closes line.

- Decision (M2b.1a, the HID composition row, 2026-10-21; 96a36d01, 2132efe4, b7161e6d): **three variants, and the physical key answers P4-B2.** Under Korean 2-Set the current keyboard layout can be the hidden `com.apple.keylayout.2SetHangul`, in which key codes 15 and 40 type ㄱ and ㅏ and r and k exist only with Option (read from the layout data on the MacBook, not live), so "key codes from the current layout" (the pre-flight entry's item 1) could post Option-r, as M2a's row would have. The row therefore runs three variants in one `live.mjs paste` invocation, each from a document emptied with `set_value` and read back empty, its commit Return posted the same way as its keys: `unicode` (today's: layout key codes with the character's Unicode string), `keyCodesOnly` (`--probe keystroke --key-codes-only`: the current layout's codes and flags, no string) and `ansiKeys` (`--ansi-keys`: press_key's fixed table, r → 15 and k → 40, no modifier flags, no string; a character outside the table, or one it keys only with a modifier, is refused with nothing sent). `ansiKeys` is what a physical keyboard sends and what the vendor's `pressKey` posted, so it is the reading that answers P4-B2 (rk: the input method bypassed; 가: composed). The probe's JSON reports its `mode`, the current layout and every press (key code, flags, string), and each variant records `keysUnmodified`.

Gaps settled:
  1. **Route 4 runs under a prepared home**: the approved engine is launched with `open --env HOME=<run dir>` (Config.swift reads `$HOME` first), whose `engine.json` names only TextEdit's route; the cell sends nothing unless the engine's `status.routes` shows the override was read, and stops that engine before removing the home. The owner's `~/.config/cua/engine.json` is only read (its mtime checked). Delivery is judged by the direct text-view read (a readable selection before, a readable caret after), never by an observation's absent selection; route 4's meta gains `keyWindowRestored` (its tech-debt row closed).
  2. **`--probe text-view --pid <n>`** reads a harness text view's selection range, scroll bars and roles directly by Accessibility (no text), and `--set-horizontal`/vertical writes the bars for a mid-way start; it tells `select_text`'s write from the engine's read on a minimized window.
  3. **Enablement's release is judged on Electron by Chromium's own mode**: the fixture records `accessibility-support-changed` and polls `app.isAccessibilitySupportEnabled()` without ever enabling it. Chrome for Testing is recorded as not judged: no mode read exists there that neither opens a tab nor itself enables accessibility.
  4. **The menu cell** compares cua's held activation (the default since "menu activation held from the record") with the vendor's lane on Select All and Bold; the switch-on lane is gone.
  5. **hscroll** runs on TextEdit with a page wider than its window (`scenes-mac up --layout wide-page`), each lane started at 0.5/0.5 with the text area's midpoint visible (so element-scroll preparation moves nothing), and judged by direction: as cua's → equivalent; none → the vendor's one-wheel defect, declared; the opposite sign → not declared, a failure.
  6. **Verdict boundaries**: a cell missing valid evidence from any mandatory lane or item is incomplete (exit 1, `complete: false`); `minimized-select` counts only `windowMinimized` with nothing posted as the refusal, `timeoutReached` and `userIntervened` as no evidence.

- Decision (M2b.4, the comparison is exact on indexes, 2026-10-21; m2b/parity a4fe7aea; confirmed by maws-3f): **the native-mac diff and the renderer goldens' judging compare accessibility text exactly, indexes included**, revising part 3's "modulo index renumbering". The rendered grammar cannot support the normalization soundly: render.mjs, like the vendor, prints the window title, the role word (raw AXRoleDescription) and the title, Description, Help, Value, Details, Placeholder and ID parts raw, newlines included, so no line after the header is certainly an element line, and every renumbering rule ends up renumbering value text. Four review rounds each found a real difference passing as `0 unexpected differences`, the last on the recorded TextEdit texts: value lines `200 apples` (vendor) against `300 apples` (cua), with the element indexes unchanged, were read as element lines and normalized away; and with one declared added element shifting later indexes by one, `200 apples` against `201 apples` compared equal. **Rejected: renumbering normalization** (indexes replaced by order of appearance, or a bijection inferred from element-shaped lines, with depth and unseen-index heuristics), for those cases. **Structural alignment is not available either**: the vendor lane gives text only, so there is no tree on that side to align lines by their kind. Fail-closed is the rule: an index difference always shows. The four recorded goldens and their action diffs compare at 0 unexpected differences under it (both engines number alike today). Cost: a future declared element addition, or the engines' numbering diverging after a removal, surfaces every later line until a declared shift is applied, which is not built (tech-debt-tracker.md, "declared index shift", with that trigger).
  Also from M2b.4, **the one change M2b makes under `src/`, model-facing**: comparing trailer text exposed that cua's focused-element trailer printed the element's `(showing 0-21 of 205 items)` suffix where the vendor's recorded trailer does not; `src/services/engine/render.mjs` (3fd0d717) drops the suffix from the trailer line (the tree line keeps it), and Finder's cua golden is re-pinned.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2b.4, the native-mac suite, 2026-10-21; 605f94c0..df50c355, merged 754802f7): what the suite settled besides the exact comparison above.
  1. **On macOS `repl-parity prepare` places the clone, checks the approved engine and writes a journal**; each run takes the admission and stops the engine itself. `prepare` requires `--commit` (Concrete Steps corrected).
  2. **The suite runs the goldens' cells plus the settle cell on the four scenes**; settle passes only when the observation 2 s after the action is equal to the one right after it, window and focus/selection trailer included (the no-change sentence alone is not enough, since the renderer appends the current trailer to it); it is scored on both engines and declarable per engine.
  3. **Every lane result is checked against `--engine`** (the attestation), the run stopping at the first mismatch; accessibility texts leave the device only after cua's side has verified the state for owner content; a prime cell runs before cua's side reads, so those reads do not disturb Chrome's tree under the vendor lane.
  4. **The expected file** (`scripts/repl-parity/expected/native-mac.json`) takes vendor, scene-state and departure faults only, a departure citing the Decision Log; it carries Finder's capped item count and the goldens' three TextEdit scene-state entries. `diff` prints JSON then `summary: N unexpected differences`, requires the vendor run first and both runs on one harness commit and device. When the two lanes return different forms (a full tree against a diff), that is a `form` difference, never declarable.
  5. **Recovery keeps its obligations**: a scene whose teardown or down failed stays in the run's account for a bounded final retry; with a scene still up, or the engine not stopped, preparation cleanup is deferred and the journal kept, and `goldens.mjs cleanup` (the printed recovery) keeps admission and journal and exits non-zero until every scene is down and the engine stopped. `prepare.mjs`'s `cleanupRun` releasing admission when its own engine stop fails stays the baseline's documented rule.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M2b.2, the TextEdit launch cell, 2026-10-21; confirmed by maws-3f): **TextEdit's launch-path cell is not run on the mini**; it is an owner option outside the verdict, worded "TextEdit launch under the engine: provable in a scratch macOS user if wanted". The engine launches a not-running app through NSWorkspace with no `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState` (the scene launcher passes it; the engine's launch path does not and should not), so TextEdit can restore the owner's windows, and with nothing to restore it can open its iCloud Open panel; either way the first observation, which always captures, would see and write a screenshot of owner content before the run's document opens. No isolated route exists without touching the owner's TextEdit state, which the harness never does (nor probes). The launch path itself is app-agnostic, and the Electron fixture's launch cell proves it; how TextEdit behaves at launch is not the engine's to prove. Launch recovery journals its intent on the device (bundle id, device time, a pid snapshot) before the launch act is dispatched, so a controller that dies mid-launch leaves the launched process recoverable.
