- Decision (M2b.2, the native matrix, 2026-10-21; 99158f82..4670a1af on `m2b/matrix`): what the matrix settled.
  1. **One result shape** (`scripts/native-matrix/cell-result.mjs`, schema `native-matrix-cell-1`): an envelope `{cell, device, started, finished, lanes, complete, verdict, problems, rows, …}` and rows `{section, id, engine, sample, ok, invalid?, declared?, interference?, …}`, one `result.json` per cell invocation whoever implements it (matrix cells and the live harness's residue cells alike). `verdictOf` is the one rule: a row counts toward pass when `ok` or it carries a validated `declared {kind, cause}` (listed as an owner item, never silent); an invalid row (a precondition not met) is excluded from every verdict; an incomplete cell is no-verdict, never pass or plain fail.
  2. **Each method case is three cells** (prepare, act, check): the act makes exactly one facade call, so its route and in-cell time are that call's; each check reads a distinguishable pre-state and the exact requested transition, with a nonce where a write could be stale, rejects a thrown action, and a refusal must match the expected error name or sentence with the state unchanged. Selection preparations read focus before selecting and the selection after, since the renderer prints selected text instead of the focus trailer. Drag-selection runs on all four scenes, the web text fields included. The app-with-no-window refusal runs on a harness stay-open AppleScript applet (the Electron fixture's hash is pinned by the goldens).
  3. **The non-interference oracle** runs on background and launch rows: the front app (lsappinfo), the top window's owner pid and bounds, the user window keeping keyboard focus (`--probe focus`, acceptance 3), and the resident `watch-activations`, whose liveness is read at each oracle reading; every activation is accounted for against device-clock windows (each js call prints `Date.now()` before and after), a harness step excusing only the activation of the app it meant to activate; a lost watcher record or an activation between steps invalidates the judged rows. Device-side Swift readers are compiled once per run. Paste and potentially-pasting `typeText` acts are bracketed by the sanitized system-alert watch, aborting without touching a prompt.
  4. **Launch rows** observe through `cua.computer.get_app_state`, so no tree text leaves the cell; the launch intent (bundle id, device time, a pid snapshot) is journaled on the device before dispatch, an early quit keeps every intent, and only a settle after recovery has stopped the serve and the engine clears them (3 s quiet within 20 s).
  5. **`summary` and `compare` share one judgment**: a run counts only with complete metadata (a positive `meta.run` and `meta.samples`, one attested lane with host, build and version) and as a full plan (`planId: full`); missing rows, samples or oracle readings are missing proof, NO VERDICT (exit 4); fewer than three attested runs a side, speed misses, finding parity and declared rows are owner items (exit 3); FAIL wins when both apply. The residue cells come from the live harness's `RESIDUE_CELLS` through `runCell` and `cellIo`; a missing one fails loudly.

- Decision (M2b.3, the probe cells, the Korean and Unicode matrix, the settle cell, 2026-10-21; 784f112a..ce96bb87): what the cells settled.
  1. **They run inside the matrix's own run, on both engines**, through its registry and result shape; the settle cell has one implementation, the native-mac suite's, which writes the matrix's result shape; an external cell exits by its result's verdict.
  2. **Probe timing.** Probes are scheduled on the device clock (`--at`), up to three attempts; a concurrent-use or intervention sample counts only when the probe's printed post lies inside the action's engine interval, a pre-action sample only when the engine's receipt lies within the debounce of the post; an all-invalid cell is NO VERDICT. The sheet variant measures its opening (`super+w`) as its own call, with its own interval and route, and the follow-up's receipt separately; the opening's real engine interval is kept, so the row may honestly end NO VERDICT live if that interval is too narrow to place a post in (the review found no sound widening). After a probe types into a scene the run waits out the engine's debounce (5.75 s) and observes once before driving that scene again, the intervention-fires row excepted. The oracle judges the concurrent-use rows (both variants) and the Korean rows behind a user window.
  3. **The Korean and Unicode matrix** reads every driven field and user window back as SHA-256 inside the cell; a source-dependent condition counts only when its prerequisites read as required (per-context read off, each target's source set and read with that target in front), and `--probe input-source --at` (an engine change) reports when the switch happened, so the mid-switch row tells a late switch from a missed one. A probe that will not exit holds every later source switch and restore across commands, through the device journal, until it is confirmed gone.
  4. **Hold 2 is split by run** (maws-3f): each vendor/cua pair runs in one sitting, runs 1 and 2 in one hold and run 3 in another; `run --run-number n` runs exactly run n, each run records its hold id (`--sitting`) and the device's load before and after, and `summary` judges per run number that both engines' results carry the same non-empty hold, commit and approved build, three run numbers a side still required.

All three variants ran under com.apple.inputmethod.Korean.2SetKorean, whose layout is com.apple.keylayout.2SetHangul. `unicode` (Option flags, Unicode strings) reads **rk**; `keyCodesOnly` (Option flags, no string) reads **rk**; `ansiKeys` (key codes 15/40, no flags, no string) reads **가**, complete with its Return.   7. **Horizontal scroll: no horizontal reading from either lane means anything.** Both lanes measured a page that could not scroll horizontally (item 8): the horizontal bar was disabled, and its written 0.5 read back without the content moving. The vendor's one-wheel scroll moved the page vertically (0.5 → 0, two samples); that stands as the declared single-axis vendor defect (Decision Log M2a item 6).
  8. **The hscroll adjudication (2026-10-21, 20:37–20:43Z, a scratch TextEdit on the MacBook, wheel events posted to its pid only).** On the scene's page, the horizontal bar read AXEnabled false, and writes of 0, 0.5 and 1 left the text area where it was: the cause is the scene and a hole in the harness, not either engine. TextEdit lays the page out on its own paper size, whatever `\paperw` says. With `\viewscale200` the page scrolls. On that page cua's exact event for `right` (pixel units, wheelCount 2, wheel2 +n, the window stamped) moved the bar 0.5 → 0, which is left. **A model-facing cua bug:** a positive second wheel scrolls left, as a positive first wheel scrolls up, so `scroll` left and right were inverted. Fixed in a5714898 (left +n, right −n on the second wheel). The scene got `\viewscale200` (71393af8). Both hscroll lanes now refuse, sending nothing and giving no verdict, a start whose bar is disabled or whose content did not move when the start was written (e43d32fd). The cell is re-verified in hold 2's margin. The vendor's signs, as recovered from its binary, put right at +n; should its scroll ever move the page horizontally, it moves left, and item 5 of the M2b.1b entry counts that as a vendor failure.

- Decision (the keystroke probe's key-spec mode, 2026-10-21): it serves MAWS P4-B2 (L6/L7), which posts letters and named keys in one ordered run. `cua-engine --probe keystroke --user-pid <n> --user-bounds <x,y,w,h> [--at <unix ms>] --keys <spec>...` takes press_key's key string, one space-separated combination per argument, parsed by press_key's own parser and table (`--keys r k BackSpace k`; option+Return is `--keys alt+Return`, in press_key's modifier names). Each spec is one unit, the front window checked before each; a chord posts as a keyboard does (a flags-changed event per modifier down with that modifier's key code, the key down and up carrying the flags, a flags-changed event per modifier up; every flag is the generic mask with the modifier's left-hand device bit, as a physical keyboard's, so alt+Return carries 0x80020), no event carries a string, and an unknown or malformed spec exits 5 before anything is sent. The report is mode `keySpecs`, with `keys` and the flags-changed events per unit in `flagsChanged`. The harness's launchProbe admits `+` so the chord keeps press_key's spelling. Probe-only; nothing model-facing changes.

- Decision (2026-10-22, the release's re-enable; confirmed by maws-3f): **once the engine has written a process's attribute back, the next observation of that process sets it again and owns it, whatever it reads.** WebAccessibility remembers each process (pid and launch date) whose attribute a release wrote back false; the next observation's enablement writes true even when the attribute reads true, takes ownership (so the next close releases it again) and clears the memory, and since it set the attribute the observation counts as enabling (`enabledNow`), so the existing web-area wait runs. A value cua never owned (it read true before cua touched it, or was unreadable when cua set it, so was never written back) is still never written (A3). A relaunch that reuses the pid is another process and is not remembered. A failed write-back keeps ownership, so it is not remembered either. **The process identity is its generation** (amended the same day on the review of 447bb209): LaunchServices' launch date, else the kernel's start time (`sysctl` `KERN_PROC_PID`, `kp_proc.p_starttime`), since an app launched without LaunchServices (a dev `electron .`) has no launch date and its key was the pid alone, so a remembered write-back outlived the process and a replacement reusing the pid was written true, owned and released, an attribute cua never owned. When neither can be read, the write-back is not remembered (the earlier rule: a value that reads true is not written). The same generation keys the whole per-process table (the enablement entries, their observers, the web areas seen), not only the write-back memory, so one key function names a process everywhere and the release's still-the-same-process check compares the same identity; for an app with a launch date nothing changes. Unchanged: the once-per-process web-area tuning of 2026-10-19 (a write-back already forgets the web area), the wait's bounds, and the release itself apart from recording the write-back. This is not a reversal of 2026-10-19.
  Rationale: the mechanism, from evidence already held. M2a.5's Surprise: Chromium's attribute reads true on the next connection after the engine wrote it back, because the readback is AppKit's and not Chromium's mode (Surprises, "M2a.5 hold, enablement"). In hold 2b's native-mac pair (`m2b/hold2b/native-mac/{cua,vendor}/texts/electron/full.txt`), cua's side-read connection enabled the Electron fixture, owned the attribute and released it at its close, so Chromium's mode dropped. The lane's connection then read true and set nothing, so `enabledNow` stayed false and nothing re-enabled the page. cua's first Electron read showed the window holding an empty container where the vendor read the full page. A wait on every observation would only poll 1.1 s for a web area that never comes, and its 100 ms lead would be a floor under every Chrome and Electron read (the vendor's medians are 75–84 ms). Alternative rejected: withdrawing the 2026-10-19 once-per-process wait (521eb9d8 on `m2b/web-wait`, withdrawn), for those reasons.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (2026-10-22, hold 3's harness, merged at 5e783bc0, engine build 0f6a95a2 with the re-enable): **Korean by its layout.** Wherever the matrix establishes the Korean source (the Korean condition, the composition's user window, the per-context condition's driven scene), it selects or reads 2-Set and then refocuses the target: another run scene is brought forward, then the target again. A row counts as Korean only when the read after the refocus shows id 2SetKorean and layout com.apple.keylayout.2SetHangul; otherwise it is invalid, naming both readings. For this, `--probe input-source` now reports `layout` beside `id` (read-only, additive). A reading taken before the refocus is recorded as evidence only.
  Rationale: Surprises, "a source switch made while a text field already has focus". The TIS read-back alone accepts a state in which the input method never engaged.
  Also in the same merge: Finder's activation raises the run's window from behind Finder's own with `open -a Finder <run folder>`, never when it is off screen, and the put-back writes Finder's keys back with the window still open. The sheet variant's cleanup waits out the debounce and falls back to TextEdit in front. Refusals and midSwitch are judged by the engine's message, since the REPL hands cells a plain Error. An open composition is ended (ABC, then Korean with the refocus) before a composing condition starts. The offline midSwitch re-judge leaves rows without the message unjudged.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (route 3 for key equivalents and element actions, 2026-10-22; amends M2a's route table and the Design's "Input"; engine cc027094 on `m2b/route3`, build b3d32315): **a ⌘ chord in `press_key` (a menu key equivalent), and an element's own action or attribute write (a click carried by `AXPress` or `AXPick`, `set_value`, `select_text`, `perform_secondary_action`), take route 3 when the app does not believe it has focus; typed text and plain keys stay on route 2′.** `cua/inputRoute` is 3 for these actions, and `activationGuardMs` rides in the meta as before whenever a guard ran.
  **The evidence.**
  - M2b's matrix, three runs on the mini with interference clean: cua's background actions on an app that does not believe it has focus were ineffective where the vendor's worked. Electron's `click.button` by `AXPress` read `pressedOnce` false, with cua's in-cell time at 9 ms against the vendor's 111 ms; Electron's `setValue.text` and `selectText.text` failed the same way. TextEdit's `super+a` into a background window left the existing selection unchanged. ⌘ was not dropped: the flags-changed, key-down and key-up events all carried `maskCommand`.
  - For ⌘ chords this is vendor parity: the vendor activates before every keyboard action into an app that does not believe it has focus (F §6.1). For element actions it is not: the vendor creates the app's `SyntheticAppFocusEnforcer` on the first element interaction (`prepareToInteract`, F §5.1), but its static reading shows no activation before an ordinary accessibility action, `set_value` or a secondary action (F §5.1, §16.3, §16.4). The element-action part is cua's proposed remedy, a measured departure pending hold 3's A/B (below), not parity. Once activated, the vendor keeps the believed-active state until its controller's lifecycle ends (F §5.5, §16.9 item 6).
  - M2a.5's findings point the same way. ⌘A into an inactive window had no effect (M2a.5 hold, item 2), and a background app works out its menu items' enablement as an inactive app, which per-action activation came too late to change (Decision Log "menu activation held from the record"). A key equivalent is resolved through that menu.
  - Route 3's cost is known. Its guard ran 37–58 ms in both input sittings, with the frontmost application and the top window unchanged. In run 3 the vendor's fake activation passed the matrix's non-interference oracle.
  **Not a reversal of U12.** Route 2′ for keys was measured on typed text (U12), never on key equivalents or on accessibility actions: the second sitting's natural-route clicks on the Electron fixture's button and the Chrome loopback page's checkbox went by route 1 and were read only as `treeChanged: true`, not by the press counted (the Design, "Input", route 1). This fills cases that measurement never covered. Typed text and plain keys stay on 2′, because U12 delivered them on all four scenes, so the enforcer is not required for them. The vendor's activation before every keyboard action (F §6.1; `R/sky-behaviour.md` §13 "Focus first", §14 "Entry") stays a declared difference for them. (`R/sky-behaviour.md` §14's cua line, "Fake activation before keying … as the vendor does", predates the second sitting and is stale.)
  **Lifecycle: held, as the vendor's enforcer holds it** (`FocusHold.swift`). The first such action of a connection on an app runs route 3's activation with its guard, which keeps the user's app frontmost and answers a window ordered above the user's with `AXRaise` or re-activation. The actions after it run inside the activation. A process serving a panel of the app is activated in its own process under the same hold. The release posts route 3's deactivation (subtype 2, only when the app believes itself active but is not). It comes at three points:
  - when the connection closes, the vendor's instance removal;
  - before the first request on the connection aimed at another app;
  - after 120 s with no request observing or acting on the held app, the menu span's bound.
  The vendor reading names only its controller's lifecycle end (instance removal, app termination, an AppModify deactivate whose trigger was not read) and no idle bound. The second and third release points are therefore the conservative choice where the reading is silent. Each release costs only a re-activation at the next action, never the action.
  The activation is the process's. `HeldActivations` shares it between the connection's hold and a recorded menu's span (the menu span's class is generalized as `ActivationSpan` for both), and the last holder's release withdraws it. Inside a recorded menu's span an action stays route 1. A per-action activation (a synthesized click's, a drag's or a paste's) leases the same activation for its action's duration (the review entry below, item 2), so neither a click amid the interaction nor a hold ending mid-click withdraws an activation the other relies on.
  Why held rather than per action:
  - it is the vendor's lifecycle, which is the parity at stake;
  - the menu finding showed an app working out its state as inactive before an activation that came only at the action;
  - an action whose effect lands after the call returns could meet a per-action deactivation first (inference: Chromium performs `AXPress` in its renderer);
  - the activation hooks fire once per interaction rather than once per action (U13 counted one pair per activation).
  **Open, for hold 4.**
  - The static reading shows no activation on the vendor's path for an ordinary (non-Catalyst) accessibility action, `set_value` or a secondary action, only the enforcer's creation (F §5.1, §16.3, §16.4). The vendor's delivery of the Electron rows is therefore not yet explained by its activation alone. The 111 ms may include its cursor overlay's press (F §5.1, inference). Hold 4 reads whether route 3 delivers these rows on cua; if it does not, they return to diagnosis, not to route 4.
  - (Corrected the same day by the review entry below.) A real activation and deactivation of the held app is observed: the vendor's activation-event tap callback (`FUN_10072fd24`) updates both the believed-active and believed-focus state through `FUN_100739c78`, so the vendor re-activates after a real cycle; cua makes the hold stale on the workspace's notifications and re-establishes it at the next action. The earlier sentence here, that the vendor shares the gap, was wrong.
  **maws-3f's ruling on the element-action part (2026-10-22).** It stays a hypothesis that hold 3 decides by an A/B on Electron's `click.button`: the natural route against a forced route 1, each read with a poll of at most 2 s for the press. If forced route 1 delivers three of three, route 3 for the click comes out before the PR, and ⌘ chords keep route 3. The writes (`set_value`, `select_text`, `perform_secondary_action`) are decided from run 1's value and selection evidence; where that does not settle them, route 3 stays for them.
  **Rejected alternative:** diagnose the failures in hold 3's run 1 first, then fix them in a hold 4. That costs a sitting that cannot change the fix's direction: the failing cases are exactly the ones the vendor's enforcer covers and U12 never measured, and route 3's guard and non-interference are already measured.
  Tests: `KeyRouteTests` (the ⌘ chord against plain keys, focus believed or not; typed text unchanged; the element action's route) and `FocusHoldTests` (one activation shared across actions, the panel's process, the release points, the idle bound, a failed guard and a user event holding nothing, a per-action activation leaving a held process alone, sharing with the menu span); the engine suite 476/476.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (the focus hold's review, 2026-10-22; reviewer-high on 95634321..c2049262, "incorrect", three important findings and one minor, all fixed in d16ede60 on `m2b/route3`; fix 3 by maws-3f's ruling): what changed.
  1. **A real activation cycle makes the hold stale.** If the user brings the held app forward and leaves it, the real deactivation clears its synthetic active state, and the hold kept answering "held" so later actions skipped the activation they needed. The vendor does not share the gap (its tap callback `FUN_10072fd24` updates its beliefs on real activation events, through `FUN_100739c78`). The engine observes the workspace's did-activate and did-deactivate notifications; a held process's activation is then stale, the next action re-runs it with its guard (its time in the meta), and a stale activation's last release posts no deactivation, the system having withdrawn it already.
  2. **Per-action activations lease the process's activation.** Synthesized clicks, drags, pastes and forced keys sampled whether a hold existed, which kept nothing alive: another connection's hold could end mid-click and post the deactivation under it, and a click that began before a hold withdrew the newly held process at its teardown. Every route-3 activation now takes a holder reference in `HeldActivations` for its action's duration; only the last release deactivates.
  3. **The activation click only on an inert point, for every route-3 activation** (maws-3f's ruling, replacing the review's first proposal). The click at `AXActivationPoint` goes only when a hit-test there answers the window itself or chrome that is no control (an inert role, `AXWindow`, `AXStaticText`, `AXImage`, `AXGroup`, `AXToolbar` or `AXSplitGroup`, advertising no click-invoked action); otherwise the subtype-1 event alone carries the activation, as for Chrome's point outside the frame. No fallback to route 1 for a skipped click; the only refusal stays a failed guard's. The meta's `activationClick` records "posted" or the reason (`noPoint`, `outsideFrame`, `spared`, `control`, `hitTestFailed`). This covers M2a's synthesized-click route 3 too: a click at the point landing on a control would invoke it before the model's action.
  4. **The entry above is reworded**: ⌘ chords are vendor parity (F §6.1); the element-action part is cua's proposed remedy pending hold 3's A/B, with maws-3f's ruling recorded there.
  Tests: `FocusHoldTests` (a real cycle through the production observer re-establishes the hold; a stale hold releases without a deactivation; another process's cycle leaves it alone; a hold ending mid-click waits for the click's release; a click ending after a hold began leaves the process active) and `ActivationClickTests` (posted on the window or non-control chrome; skipped on a control, outside the frame, with no point, spared, or with no answer; route 3's synthesized click delivering either way with the meta field). The engine suite 485/485.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (the focus hold's second review, 2026-10-22; reviewer-high on c2049262..43ee3e22, "incorrect", five P2 findings, all fixed in cb4514b8 on `m2b/route3`): what changed.
  1. **Staleness is a per-process real-activation epoch** (findings 1–3, one mechanism). The first review's stale mark could not tell a real event before an activation from one after it. A real event during a first activation's guard was lost, because the process was tracked only once the activation had run. A refresh consumed the old mark before its replacement's AX reads, so an event during those reads made the fresh replacement stale and skipped its deactivation. And a stale replacement's failed guard still posted one. Now every real did-activate or did-deactivate bumps the process's epoch, held or not. An activation reads the epoch right after it posts subtype 1 (or when it finds the app already believing it has focus). It is stale exactly when the epoch has moved since, and a stale activation is never deactivated, on its last release or its failed guard's cleanup alike. One residue: the workspace's notification arrives on the main run loop after the real event, so an event shortly before a post can be read as after it, and the release then skips a deactivation the app needed. The window is the notification's delivery latency.
  2. **An element whose actions could not be read is not inert** (finding 4). `AXUIElementCopyActionNames` failing used to read as "no actions", so a pressable group whose query failed was clicked. The click is now skipped as `actionsUnknown`; a query answering no value still counts as "no actions".
  3. **Per-action borrowers check for the user under the registry's lock** (finding 5). Synthesized clicks, drags, pastes and forced keys pass the in-flight intervention check into the acquire, as the holds do, and keep the later check before their events. So a user event while they wait behind another holder's activation stops them before the activation and its click; paste rechecks there after its own lock's check.
  Tests: the reviewer's three reproductions as `ReviewRaceTests`; `ActivationClickTests` (a failed action query skips the click; a user event while a click waits stops it before its activation, the check running under the lock); `PasteActivationTests` (the same for paste, with the pasteboard untouched). The engine suite 491/491.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.

- Decision (2026-10-22, hold 3's route A/B; approved by the owner): **a click's `spikeRoute` reaches the engine from model code only under `CUA_ENGINE_SPIKE_ROUTE=1`**, which narrows bd1136f1's guarantee ("only the vendor's input fields reach the engine: no spike switch from model code") to sessions without that variable. The route A/B (`cell route-ab`) must press the Electron fixture's button by route 1 alone against the engine's own route 3, and the matrix reaches the engine only through the product service, which drops the spike switches. So `src/services/engine/service.mjs` forwards a click's `spikeRoute` (exactly `1`, `2'`, `2` or `3`; anything else is refused before the engine is asked) when the worker's environment carries the variable set to 1, read once when the service is made; no other method's switch and no other switch is forwarded. The product launch never puts the variable in the worker's environment: `src/runtime/launch.mjs` drops it with every other ambient value, so a user's shell that exports it changes nothing. Only the harness serve (`scripts/native-baseline/serve.mjs`) adds it to its launch, through the serve's launch hook (`scripts/native-baseline/spike-route.mjs`), for cua's engine and when its own environment asks; only the route A/B's lane asks (`scripts/native-matrix/run.mjs`, `scripts/native-baseline/remote.mjs` nativeEnvironment). (The first version copied an ambient value in `launch.mjs`, so any serve started with the variable exported would have forwarded; the review of 48796e16 found it, and the hook replaced it.) With it, only the click's route can be chosen; the policy, the forbidden list and the approval run as for any click.
  Rationale: the A/B decides whether element actions need route 3's activation at all (the 2026-10-22 route-3 entries); without a forced route 1 it cannot separate the activation from the re-enable fix in the same build. The strongest alternative, serving the A/B through the spike service (`scripts/spike/engine/service.mjs`), lost because that service was last changed at M0/M1 and would put the comparison on a path neither the matrix nor the product runs. The auto-mode classifier refused the change at the executor as a security weakening; the owner approved it, and it was written in the coordinating session. Whether the switch stays as a documented test hook or goes after #117 is a tech-debt row.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, maws-3f.
- Decision (M3 pre-flight, 2026-10-21, on branch `feat/117-m3` from `feat/117-m2b` at 027179bc, M2b's code done and its holds pending; brief from maws-3f): M3 runs as five tasks (Progress). M3.1 touches `src/services/`, `src/mcp/` and the Linux harnesses and runs in its own worktree beside M3.2 (doctor) and M3.3 (verify and the probe library, its own worktree), which touch disjoint files; M3.4 consumes M3.2's doctor row and M3.3's probe library and dispatches after both are reviewed; M3.5 is the controller's: one CI-opt hold maws-3f books, on the build the whole-milestone review has passed, and the Linux parity run. Gaps settled in the spirit of the design:
  1. **Doctor reads the grants, launching only what it then stops** (maws-3f's ruling on this entry's first draft, which kept doctor passive): when no engine answers at the approved socket and the approved bundle is installed (its `build` and signature match `approved.json`), `doctor` launches it as the launcher does (`open -n -g -a`), reads `status`, and sends `shutdown` to the engine it started; an engine already running is read and left alone; a pending or unapproved bundle is never launched, and the row reports that state. Doctor is where onboarding is checked, and a passive doctor would read the grants as unknown in exactly the state it is run for (installed, nothing works yet). Checked in the engine's source before ruling: its start path (`main.swift`: the settings, the screen-scale cache from `NSScreen`, the socket server) and `status` (`AXIsProcessTrusted`, `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess`, `CGPreflightListenEventAccess`, `CGPreflightPostEventAccess`) neither prompt nor capture; the prompting requests and the capture live in `approve` and in the first observation, and the intervention tap starts only on a first driven app. The identity is reported as a match and a kind (`engine.signature.identityMatches`, `kind`), never the value (M1b pre-flight item 2); acceptance 1's "`engine.signature.identity` matching the configured identity" is that match.
  2. **`verify --bind <app>` binds only an app that is already running**, so the engine never launches it (a launched TextEdit can restore owner windows or open its iCloud panel, M2b.2's TextEdit launch entry); on the mini the hold brings up the TextEdit scene first, so the bind lands on the run's document. verify's report carries counts, process and socket identities and the attestation, never tree text, titles or screenshots; it answers only the approval it expects (`connector_id: computer-use`, `tool_params.app` the named bundle) and declines anything else.
  3. **Every lane pins its engine and refuses on the attestation** (the whole-M1b rule): `verify`, `probe-lifecycle`, `accept/textedit.mjs` and `accept-native` set `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE` from `--engine` and check `_meta['cua/nativeEngine']`; `accept-native`'s items 12 and 13 set `vendor` whatever `--engine` says, until M6.
  4. **On Linux the policy asks; it does not forbid.** The forbidden list and the risk table are macOS bundle ids and the design names no Linux list, so Linux approvals ask once per connection per key (the supervisor's memory, as on macOS) with the elicitation shape the harness matcher expects; a key is the window's `app` for the window-bound methods and the app id for `launch_app`, so a `launch_app` and the first action on the launched window may ask twice (two keys), which the design's key list implies (maws-3f agreed; the Linux rule says so only if the model would otherwise be confused). A Linux forbidden or risk list would be content with no source; if one is wanted it is #171's. Every Linux harness that drives a window method answers the approval it pins and declines anything else, so a harness can never pass by accepting everything.
  5. **The plugin is bumped** (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, a minor version) at the milestone's end: Linux approvals change a default behaviour every Linux session sees, which is plugin-visible under the Plan of Work's constraint, although that constraint names only M5 and M6.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3.1, maws-3f's rulings, 2026-10-21): three revisions of the design from M3.1's build and review.
  1. **Linux clipboard methods ask.** `clipboard_read` and `clipboard_write` act on a window (Copy and Paste), so they are app-scoped whatever their names, and the design's "the clipboard methods … ask nothing" was wrong about the pinned API (`sky-full-desktop-api.md`); `clipboard_release` asks nothing. Window methods are keyed by the app sky_linux's own `list_windows` names for the window id, never the model-supplied `window.app` (one `list_windows` per window action, its cost read from the cua-linux run's timings), and windowless input (`move_relative`, `drag_start` without a window) by `(desktop)`. Alternative: the design as written; lost because an unasked Paste into a window is exactly what the approval exists for.
  2. **The approval memory moves to `cua serve`'s MCP connection.** The supervisor's memory died with the runtime's idle stop (15 min by default) while the MCP connection stayed open, so a remembered app asked again mid-session, against the README's promise; M3 makes it visible, since every Linux session now asks. The key and semantics are unchanged (accepts with `_meta.persist` and declines remembered, a new connection asks again), tested across an idle stop. Alternative: keep it in the supervisor and correct the README; lost because the promise is the right behaviour.
  3. **README and onboarding lines M3 makes false are corrected in M3** (the `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST` `always` value, now refused; "no app asks" on Linux); the rest of the docs stay with M4 and M5.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f, on maws-3f's rulings.
- Decision (M3.2, doctor's engine row, 2026-10-21; 793195d6, review fixes a2b5993a): the gaps the row settled.
  1. **`engine.selected` is the engine sessions actually use**, taken from repl.host's own `replHostFor` selection (null when sessions are refused); the setting's choice is `engine.preferred {engine, reason}`. So acceptance 1's `engine.selected: "cua"` holds only when sessions drive cua's engine, and the default's fallbacks (no approved engine, no Node 24) read vendor with a warning.
  2. **Doctor launches only when the effective engine is cua** and the approved bundle carries the engine's bundle id (`io.github.ssfskim.cua.engine`, so the harnesses' fixture approved engines are never launched); it treats the answering engine as its own only when it reports this bundle's real path and build, and stops it only while the same pid answers; any other responder is reported (`launch.mismatch`, `launch.replaced`) and left alone, and its grants are not reported as the approved bundle's. With the vendor engine selected the row reads the approved location's files only (no codesign, no socket).
  3. **Status**: `fail` for refused sessions, an `engine.json` the engine would refuse, a foreign bundle, a status or launch failure, another engine at the socket, or Accessibility or Screen Recording missing; `warn` for Input Monitoring missing, an identity mismatch, ad hoc or no configured identity, `routeAdvice` naming apps, the default's fallback, or a launched engine doctor could not stop; `skip` when no session uses the engine; the probe's ability reported, not judged. Route overrides come from the running engine's `status`, else from `engine.json` read as `Config.swift` reads it. Sentences name `cua engine build` and `cua engine approve`; M4 brings this row and repl.host to `cua install --engine`.
  4. The approved location is read once per doctor run (one codesign), shared by the selection and the row (`readApprovedLocation`).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3.3, verify, probe-lifecycle and the probe library, 2026-10-21; c63f8cdc..33c53ad8 on `m3/verify`, merged d0a7ed29): the gaps the harnesses settled.
  1. **Every run pins its engine**: `--engine`, else `CUA_NATIVE_ENGINE`, else the launcher's default, always set in the serve's environment (`scripts/probe/pinned-engine.mjs`: `pinLane`, `selectLane`), so `engine.json` never chooses and a plain `node verify.mjs` follows M5's flip. The selection is predicted with the launcher's own `replHostFor`, refused before serving where the launcher would refuse, and every `js` result's `cua/nativeEngine` is checked: **any** mismatch stops the run at once (no further call or bind), the serve is closed, the report printed, exit 1.
  2. **"No `SkyComputerUseService` or `node_repl` started by cua"** is judged on processes started from cua's roots (`CUA_HOME` and the engine directory) during the session; a pinned helper already running beforehand is reported (`runningBefore`), not failed; other installations fall outside the roots by path. On cua's own host `allExecutablesRelocated` is null and `allExecutablesExpected` carries the verdict (accept-native item 4 reads it).
  3. **The engine socket's clients are enumerated by peer address**, not by the socket's path: on macOS `lsof <path>` lists only the server's ends and a client shows only as `->0x<accepted end>`, so every Unix socket `lsof -U` lists is read and each accepted end matched to its client; only the approved engine may hold the address and only cua's Node may connect; an accepted connection whose client is unseen is "the clients are unproven", a problem, never clean.
  4. **`--bind <bundle id>`** (macOS computer surface) binds only an app `lsappinfo` shows running (both `"pid"=n` and `pid = n`, one shared parser with the key guard), accepts only the policy's approval for that bundle, pins `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=session`, and keeps no result text; approvals are observed by behaviour (one ask on the first bind, none on the second, the elicitations declined counted per scenario in probe-lifecycle).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3.1, Linux approvals and the connection's approval memory, 2026-10-21; a5f7a494..a3d8813a on `m3/linux`): the gaps the task settled, besides "M3.1, maws-3f's rulings".
  1. **The Linux ask** comes after the wrapper's own refusals and before the secret read, and after `launch_app`'s inventory check; a `launch_app` without a string app is refused rather than passed through; unknown methods ask nothing (sky_linux refuses them); `drag_move` and `drag_end` ask nothing (the vendor refuses a handle that was not started). Linux approvals are low risk with no forbidden list (#171 named in the code).
  2. **The memory** (`src/mcp/approval-memory.mjs`, one per `createServer`) records only an answer the host itself would take: the whole reply is checked by the host's rule (`elicitationOutcome`: error precedence, the action, `content` and `_meta` plain objects) before anything is remembered, for accepts and declines alike.
  3. **Elicitation ids the client sees are the connection's own** (`cua-elicitation-<n>`), for every elicitation the proxy forwards: each pending ask keeps the runtime's id and its generation (an idle stop starts a new one), an answer goes back under the runtime's id only while its generation runs, a late answer for a stopped generation is recorded against its own app and never forwarded (it is the user's real answer for that app), and the runtime's `notifications/cancelled` is translated too. Without this a fresh runtime's request with a reused id received the old runtime's answer for another app.
  4. **The harnesses** list their window before binding and pin that app (`linux-native` also its entry id); the accept scripts pin `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=session`. `cua serve` refuses `CUA_SHIM_PERSIST=always` as a classified failure before any upstream starts, its sentence naming `hooks/cua-approve.sh`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3.4, the acceptance scripts, 2026-10-21; 310f4f0b, d0da881d, bf16bb42): the gaps the scripts settled.
  1. **Approvals by behaviour in the TextEdit fixture**: connection A binds twice and B once; an approval is accepted only while a bind runs and only for the bound bundle (`isAppApproval`), anything else declined and counted; a first bind that asks nothing is BLOCKED on the vendor engine and FAIL on cua's. The attestation is checked on every reply, the key guard's undelayed dispatch included, and one refusal stops every connection.
  2. **Every native item follows `--engine`**, secret delivery and `probe-secrets` included; only items 12 and 13 are pinned to the vendor engine, each reply's attestation checked as it arrives (item 13's `cellRunner` checks the raw reply before decoding it, a correctly attested but undecodable reply is recorded failed and the run continues). Because the secret fixtures serve with `HOME` set to a temporary store home, the selection would have found no approved engine there: `serve()` and `openConnection()` take an optional `engine` selection input that `replHostFor` uses (absent in ordinary `cua serve`, which is unchanged), and the fixtures pass the account's (`accountEngine()`).
  3. **The fixture's close is judged without observing TextEdit**: observing a windowless running TextEdit makes either engine reopen it, and TextEdit answers with its Open panel, which can list the owner's documents; so `super+w` is followed by a comparison of TextEdit's window numbers by pid (`front-window.swift`, no names, no permission), PASS when exactly the run's window is gone, and TextEdit is quit only when it has no window left.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-21, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3, the whole-milestone review, 2026-10-22; 95056ab7, a01ce577): **`verify --bind` binds only an app with a window on screen, the same before each bind**, which revises the M3 pre-flight's item 2 ("binds only an app that is already running"). A running pid was not enough: TextEdit keeps running after its last document closes, and an observation of an app with no primary window makes the engine reopen it and capture what it opens (TextEdit's Open panel, which can list the owner's documents). Before each bind verify reads the app's layer-0 windows from the window server (FrontWindowProbe through `readWindows`, which now carries `onScreen`), with no engine observation. It refuses before any js call when the app is not running, its windows cannot be read, or none of them is on screen (a minimized document, one on another Space, or an off-screen helper window such as Finder keeps does not count). Before the second bind it also refuses when the pid changed or a window read before the first bind is gone. The rule is `bindRefusal` in scripts/probe/lib.mjs. verify's new dependencies (window-close.mjs, key-guard.mjs, front-window.swift) ship in the package.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3, acceptance 6, 2026-10-22): **M3 does not claim acceptance 6; the `native-mac` diff at 0 unexpected differences is M2b's proof.** M3's Plan of Work lists acceptance 6 among what it proves, but the suite, its expected-differences file and the engine fixes it measures are all M2b's. M2b hold 2b's diff found 191 unexpected differences from two causes, which M2b is fixing, and its re-run in M2b's hold 3 follows M3's hold. M3 changes no cell the suite runs and no engine code. So M3's PR points to M2b's result rather than citing a run M3 neither produces nor can fix. M3 cannot land before M2b in any case: its PR is rebased onto M2b's squash. Alternative: cite hold 3's re-run if it reaches 0. Not chosen, because it would make M3's acceptance depend on M2b's fixes for a claim M3's code does not touch.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (M3 hold, 2026-10-22; 10d423b2, 09228121, dd72c0b1): **the engine attestation exempts only work the connection never dispatched, and probe-lifecycle's one abandoned cell is counted apart.** On the mini, `probe-lifecycle --engine cua` refused on scenario 2's late call. The task lifecycle rejected that call with `task_ending` (server.mjs `rejectionResult`, no `_meta`), so no engine answered it. `isLifecycleRejection` therefore exempts only `task_ending` and `cancelled` with no `cua/taskId`: in task.mjs those two codes never settle dispatched work. `connection_failed` and `connection_closing` can settle in-flight work, so they are never exempt in general. The one exception is scenario 3's stuck cell, which the scenario abandons on purpose and which probe-lifecycle declares abandoned. For that call only, a `connection_failed` or `connection_closing` rejection with no `cua/taskId` is accepted without an attestation and counted as `abandonedUnattested`. The count must be exactly 1, both in probe-lifecycle and in accept-native's lifecycle check; any other count fails. Reason: no engine answer exists to attest, and the scenario exists to prove that the connection fails closed. A result with `cua/taskId` always attests, whatever its code. The first version keyed the exemption to the missing `cua/taskId` alone; the review found that the lifecycle also rejects in-flight work that way.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-22, plan-executor for maws-3f.
- Decision (#200, 2026-11-01, `fix/200-background-raise`; amends route 3 in "Input"): **route 3's pointer actions post
  their events through a delivery guard that undoes the click's own raise inside one screen-update hold.** "Every route-3
  action delivered with the frontmost application and the top window unchanged" (the sittings) does not hold on the
  MacBook: a background route-3 click into TextEdit raised its window above the frontmost Chrome for Testing at the
  click and left it there, cursor on and off (#200's comments of 2026-10-31). The cause, read in AppKit on macOS 26.6.2
  (#200's report, `~/.local/share/200/` on the MacBook): subtype 1 arms `_mouseActivationInProgress` for one mouse down
  and makes no window key; the activation click consumes the arm (the window made key, nothing ordered), so the
  action's single left mouse down without ⌘ alone reaches a key window of an app that believes itself active, which
  `-[NSWindow _handleMouseDownEvent:isDelayedEvent:]` answers with `_NSDoDeferredWindowOrdering`. The activation click
  is the vendor's and has that purpose: an inactive app's window is never key, and a mouse down into a window that was
  not key is dropped unless its view accepts the first mouse, which NSView, NSControl and NSTextView's plain text click
  do not. No change outside the app both delivers such a click and avoids the raise: a second subtype 1 arms nothing in
  an app already active; a subtype 2 before it resigns the key window; AXMain orders the window front and keys nothing
  while the arm is set; AXFocused keys nothing. The vendor posts this click with ⌘ (not an ordering click, and not a
  caret move either, U10) and, for its clicks without ⌘, undoes the raise with a window-ordering observer
  (`WindowOrderingObserver`, event 808, `R/F-helper-logic.md` §17). So the engine restores rather than prevents: the
  activation's guard settles on one stable sample and keeps its hold open; the events go out; a delivery guard answers
  the target's window above the user's with the user's window raised back and the target's (or the bound app's) process
  in front with the user's app made frontmost again, until three stable samples, before the hold is released; a click
  inside a held or shared activation takes a hold of its own, and the guard checks the action's own window (a shared
  activation may have been made for another window of the process); never a second hold for one action; the hold is
  released at the 100 ms cap whatever the guard's state, and past it the guard repairs on until 1 s after the events
  unless the person gave input since them (`delivery.repairedAfterRelease`, `personInput`), then leaves the order as it
  is, reported (`delivery.capped`, `raiseLeft`, both read from its samples rather than from what its requests
  answered); a third app in front is the user's, never undone: the guard restores only to the app that was frontmost
  before the click, and only when the target or the bound app took the front. The counts are parameters
  (`activationGuardStableSamplesBeforeDelivery`, `deliveryGuardStableSamples`, `deliveryGuardMinimumMs`, 60 ms since
  the first live samples saw TextEdit raise 21 and 38 ms after the click). Costs: about 30 ms per route-3 pointer action
  plus the minimum wait (cua's M2b click cells 4–140 ms in-cell against the vendor's 229–1038 ms, `m2b/hold3`); the
  target ends second, above a third app's window that sat between (as the vendor's observer leaves it). Rejected:
  re-arming subtype 1 before the mouse down (arms nothing), dropping the activation click (the click is swallowed on
  views that refuse the first mouse), a subtype 2–1 pair (the same, with a visible resign), keying through AXMain or
  AXFocused (orders or keys nothing), the vendor's ⌘ (changes what a click means, U10), extending the 100 ms cap (a longer
  freeze). The cua_repl host notes now tell agents to leave the person's windows as they are rather than raise, activate,
  hide or unhide an app to reach a covered window (the 10-23 sessions' raises were such calls).
  Amended (the design session's ruling, after the first live check saw a raise 21 ms after a guard that had settled at
  14 ms): **the after-release repair is a safety net on every route-3 pointer action, not only past the cap.** When a
  delivery guard returns, an after-action watch (`AfterActionWatch`, Activation.swift) samples every 10 ms on another
  thread until 1 s after the events, under the guard's rules (the user's window raised back over the target's, the
  user's app made frontmost again over the target's or the bound app's, never over a third app, nothing once the person
  gave input since the events, ended by a refused re-activation); the action answers at the release as before, so the
  watch adds no latency, and `deliveryGuardMinimumMs` now decides only how many repairs stay invisible. Its repairs are
  flashes, made with no hold open, and the action that caused them has already answered, so they are recorded on the
  connection (`AfterActionWatches`) and carried as `afterActionRepairs` by every later input result and observation of
  it, each repaired action keyed by its delivery's `postedAt` (Interfaces), plus a `route3.afterAction.repair` signpost
  event each: cumulative and keyed rather than drained, because a cell's reply keeps only its last call's meta and a
  sitting must match each repair to its action. The watch ends when the connection's next execute begins, at its very
  start, and when any pointer action begins (before its activation); a sample runs under the watch's lock and the
  cancel takes it, so no repair starts once the next request has begun, at the cost of that request waiting out one
  sample in progress. Rejected: the cancel on the next action's guard alone (an observation or an element action, or a
  menu's deliberate activation, would run under a watch still answering the target in front), a drained per-call
  counter (lost when a cell makes two input calls), and folding the guard's own past-the-cap repairs into the watch
  (it would end each capped action at the cap with `raiseLeft` set and the repair reported elsewhere; left for a later
  change). Other connections' requests do not end a connection's watch.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235 for cua_repl's rulings on #200.
- Decision (#244, 2026-11-01, `fix/244-chromium-first-observation`; amends enablement in "Observation (skyshot)" and
  reverses the critique's A3 (2026-10-19) where it withdrew the once-per-process memory): **the engine writes the
  enablement attribute once per owned process generation.** Once it has set and owns the attribute, later observations
  of that process neither read nor write it, until the release writes it back or the process ends. The first step is
  unchanged: read, and when it reads false, write and own. A value that reads true or cannot be read is not owned and is
  read again at every observation (an unreadable one is then set at each, as before). The 2026-10-22 re-enable stands
  (after a write-back the next observation writes again, owned), and so do the release and its locking. An owned
  observation reports `enablementPrior` "owned": nothing was read. A3 kept the re-read so that it would catch a process
  that resets the attribute. On Electron that re-read cannot work, and the writes it causes do harm (#244's report,
  `~/.local/share/244/` on the MacBook; Electron v44.9.0, `shell/browser/mac/electron_application.mm`):
  1. **The getter is computed.** AXManualAccessibility answers `mode == kAXModeComplete`, and complete mode is created
     with kFromPlatform and kScreenReader added, so the equality never holds and the attribute reads false whatever
     happened. A re-read cannot see a reset.
  2. **Every write restarts the 2 s countdown to complete mode** (`enableScreenReaderCompleteModeAfterDelay:` cancels
     the pending one) and fires the app's `accessibility-support-changed`. The kit's session opens with two
     observations about 1.3 s apart, so complete mode could not land while cua kept observing, and every later cell of
     #200's final slot fired one more event in the fixture (about 60; the vendor's run none).
  3. **Electron's `_AXEnhancedUserInterfaceRequests` counter** goes +1 per true and −1 per false and is reset only when
     complete mode fires, so after a burst of writes the release's single false leaves it above zero and releases
     nothing.
  4. **The vendor writes once.** It holds one assertion per (app, kind), reference-counted, and only the count's 0→1
     step reads the attribute and writes true (`FUN_1006f2bcc`, `FUN_1006f05e4`). Its controller keeps the assertion for
     the session, and the release at count 0 writes false (`FUN_1006f00a8`). The fixture's record shows its single
     write per session.
  The trade: a process that resets the attribute itself keeps a missing tree until the next release, as with the
  vendor. Unchanged: the web-area wait and its once-per-process tuning. A consequence: an owned observation is not
  `enabledNow`, so a later observation of an Electron app may now adopt the settle's tree (`adoptsSettledTree`), as
  other apps' observations already could. That rule no longer asks whether this observation enabled, but whether the
  process was enabled since its settles, by any connection: `WebAccessibility` counts the engine's writes per process,
  and the observation adopts only when the count read before its settles equals the count after its enablement step.
  Otherwise (the review of 59862867) a connection that settled while another connection enabled the process and saw
  its web area found the attribute owned, wrote nothing, skipped the wait and adopted a tree read before the page
  existed; the per-observation write had hidden this. Alternative: keep the re-read and only stop writing what the engine owns. Not
  chosen: on Electron the read always answers false, so it would decide nothing, and the vendor does not read either.
- Decision (#244, 2026-11-01, the same branch; amends "Observation (skyshot)"): **every observation of an eligible app
  reads the application element's role before its enablement**: one `AXUIElementCopyMultipleAttributeValues` for
  AXRole, AXSubrole and AXRoleDescription, never cached, on the `EnablementAccess` seam
  (`WebAccessibility.observeEnablement`), timed as `appRoleRead` and carried as `appRoleReadMs` in `cua/observation` so
  the live run reports its warm cost. Chromium and Electron give a process kAXModeBasic (native APIs and web contents)
  the first time any client asks NSApp's `-accessibilityRole` (kSonomaAccessibilityActivationRefinements, which would
  cut that to native APIs, is off by default), and Basic mode alone exposes the page's tree. Without it the page waited
  for complete mode, at least 2 s after the write and past the 100 ms + 1 s web-area wait, so a freshly launched
  Electron app's first observation held no AXWebArea at all (#200's final slot). The vendor makes the same read:
  `ApplicationUIElement.init(pid:)` 0x100706c7c always runs the cache builder `FUN_1007542cc` (call at 0x100706d28),
  which outside a tree transaction fetches the element's attribute names and then the 20-attribute set (role first) in
  one request, and the observation's preparation constructs it before `enableAccessibilityIfNeeded` (`R/F-helper-logic.md`
  §1.3, corrected). cua reads only the three role attributes, and only on eligible apps, the enablement's own gate,
  where the vendor's construction runs on every app. **Declared difference: Basic mode lasts for the app's lifetime,
  including in the person's own Chrome.** cua's release writes back only the attribute, and nothing can release what a
  role read turned on; the vendor, and any assistive client that reads the role, leave the same. Two things stay open.
  That AppKit answers the multi-value request through `-accessibilityRole` is inference from Electron's source and the
  record (the attribute names read before it is the other candidate); the live repro decides it. A Chromium build with
  kSonomaAccessibilityActivationRefinements on would get only native APIs from the role, and its page would wait for
  complete mode; the longer wait after a write (#244's candidate C) is held for that case.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235 for the design session's ruling on #244.
- Decision (#237, 2026-11-01, `fix/237-engine-busy-after-end-task`; amends #200's entry above for a closed connection):
  **end_task closes the session's engine connection, and the engine finishes two things of a closed connection before
  its state goes; `status` names who holds the engine.** The service's connection lived as long as its trusted worker
  (up to the 15-minute idle stop after end_task), so `status.connections` counted a session that had ended its task and
  `cua engine build` and `approve` refused `engine_busy` (#237's evidence). Now end_task's turn-ended hook sends the
  session's `cursor {hide: true}` and closes the connection, both written at once and neither waited for; the next
  engine-bound request reconnects, as after any close (the client's connect: milliseconds against a running engine).
  What a task loses at that boundary is per-connection engine state: element indexes, web enablement (released at the
  close and written once at the next task's first observation, which waits for the page;
  `testATaskAfterEndTaskReEnablesOnceAndItsWarmObservationsWriteNothing`), the intervention re-query state and, with the
  last connection, the tap. App approvals live in the proxy and are not asked again; js_reset is unchanged. On the
  engine (Server.swift `unregister`), the connection leaves the open map at once, so `status` and shutdown's busy
  count drop at the close, and of its state:
  1. **Its after-action watch runs to its natural end**, under #200's rules unchanged (the repair window, the person's
     input, a third app in front, a refused re-activation): end_task follows an action closely, and a raise 200 ms after
     the events must still be repaired. `ClosedConnectionWatches` (Activation.swift) holds the connection's watches
     until the watch ends and then lets go, so the watch still holds its sink weakly and nothing holds it in a cycle;
     only the watches outlive the connection, not its lineages or anything else. The next `execute` on **any**
     connection ends every closed connection's watch first, as an execute ends its own connection's: a later client's
     work is never answered by an earlier client's watch. Requests that neither act nor observe (`ping`, `status`,
     `cursor`) leave it, as they leave a connection's own watch, so a reconnecting session's handshake or another
     session's `cua engine status` changes nothing.
  2. **A fade under way plays out before the cursor's panel goes** (CursorHub `closed`): the hide reaches the engine
     just before the close, and closing at once cut #235's 0.44 s fade. The fade is committed again under the close's
     generation, so a hide commit the close superseded on its way to the main thread still plays; a cursor shown and
     not fading (a client gone without end_task) goes at once, as before.
  3. **Everything else goes at once**, as before: the menu span and the focus hold (activations held for the client's
     next actions, which will not come; their withdrawal raises nothing the watch would answer), the connection's drives
     and with the last of them the intervention tap (the watch reads the person's input from the HID idle time, not the
     tap), and web enablement (nothing of the watch's reads it).
  **Shutdown while a closed connection's watch runs** (build or approve once nobody is connected): `stopAndExit` ends
  every such watch, each waiting out its sample in progress (one 10 ms sample at most), then exits; it neither waits for
  the watch's natural end nor exits mid-repair. A fading panel goes with the process.
  **Holders**: the engine reads each admitted connection's peer pid at accept (`LOCAL_PEERPID`; the uid check is
  unchanged) and keeps the session's label from its `cursor` requests, whatever the cursor switches; `status` adds
  `holders: [{pid, label}]` for the other connections, keeping `connections`, and shutdown's `busy` carries them; the
  CLI's `engine_busy` and `cua engine status` name them (`pid 42091 (MAWS)`), and an engine without holders still gives
  the count. The pid is the supervisor's that holds the socket; the label is client-asserted, a name to ask, never a
  credential. Rejected: the engine not counting a connection with no open task (a protocol change for task state, and
  a race between the count and the shutdown that a session would see as one failed call); closing at js_reset (inside
  a task, it would retire the task's own indexes); waiting in the client for the fade (end_task's reply would wait
  about half a second). Not covered: a session that never calls end_task still holds the engine until the idle stop;
  the vendor runtime's own connection lifetime across end_task was not read.
  Amended (the review of d409b72c..790295f3, three races): the client's `release` starts a new generation, so a
  connect still in flight at end_task closes its socket instead of publishing and the ended task's queued requests fail
  unsent; the close's first step (`Server.retire`) adopts the watch and leaves the open connections in one move under
  the server's lock, so nothing that finds the connection gone can miss its watch; and drains of the closed
  connections' watches are serialized, so none returns while a watch another drain is stopping can still repair.
  Amended (2026-11-01, the design session's ruling after an incident): **the engine's unit tests cannot act on the real
  desktop.** While fixing the races, a unit test ran an execute on a Server's own engine, which no test had stubbed or
  lock-screened, and the engine launched TextEdit on the MacBook. Every call by which the engine acts on the desktop or
  another process's GUI now passes one gate (`DesktopGate`, Sources/Engine/DesktopGate.swift): the app launch
  (`NSWorkspace.openApplication`), the re-activation (`NSRunningApplication.activate`), every CGEvent post (to a process
  and to the HID tap), Accessibility actions and attribute writes (`AXOps`' defaults, route 1's action, the enablement
  write, the probes' writes), the screen-update hold (`SLSDisableUpdate`), writes to the general pasteboard, the
  agent cursor's panel ordered onto the screen (the engine's own window, but it is drawn over the person's windows and
  ordered against them, so it counts), and every call that can put up a system dialog (the review of ce8fb2a1..2f9a83ef):
  the Accessibility request with its prompt and `CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess` (in `approve`, `requireAccessibility`
  and `requireScreenRecording`; refused, each answers the non-prompting preflight instead), and the screen's listing
  and capture (`SCShareableContent`, `SCScreenshotManager.captureImage`), which can raise the capture confirmation.
  The engine requests no other grant (it only preflights Input Monitoring, and creates its listen tap only once that
  reads granted). Reads that cannot prompt (observations, the window list, the preflights) stay outside. A refused
  call's name is built only once the gate is found closed, so the engine pays nothing for it.
  In the engine the gate is open and passing costs a flag read. It is closed in any process that has XCTest loaded, so
  the test bundle closes it by being loaded and no test can forget to; there a call that reaches the real system is
  refused (it does nothing, or throws `desktopGateClosed` naming the call where the call can throw), recorded by the
  gate, and made a failure of the running test by an observer the test bundle registers as it loads
  (Tests/EngineTestGate's constructor, Tests/EngineTests/DesktopGateObserver.swift). The proving test launches an app
  bundle that does not exist and checks the gate's record and the expected failure; the whole suite runs with no other
  refusal. CI's `engine-swift` job runs `swift test`, so the gate is closed there too; `--probe` and the debug
  executable the integration tests run directly never load XCTest and stay open.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for cua_repl's ruling on #237.
- Decision (#187, 2026-11-01, `fix/187-paste-clipboard-timeout`; amends "Paste" in Design and M2a.3's item 4): **the
  paste route answers by stage, tells the model that delivery is unknown, answers at once when nothing could confirm,
  binds both waits to the deadline, and records value-free diagnostics on every paste.** Both of the old throw sites
  came after ⌘V was posted and answered the vendor's one sentence, "Timed out waiting for the application to read the
  clipboard", which read as "nothing happened": the dogfood's Korean typeText raised it with the text already in the
  field, and the model's retry doubled it. The vendor shares the defect: its effect poll's timeout is remapped to the
  same case-5 sentence (`FUN_1001b534c`; `R/F-helper-logic.md` §7.5). **Declared difference from the vendor's single
  sentence:**
  1. **Split by stage.** No hand-over within the read wait stays `clipboardReadTimedOut`. A hand-over with no effect seen
     where a channel could have shown one is the new `pasteUnconfirmed` (-10005), an error, never a success with a
     warning: the hand-over names no reader, so a clipboard manager's read meets it with nothing pasted (no channel at
     all is item 3's case). A change count that moved still turns either into
     `clipboardConflict` (F §7.6).
  2. **The text contract.** The sentence itself carries what the model needs, since model code sees only the message
     (`_meta` is not content): the stage in plain words, that delivery is unknown, and to observe the field with
     get_app_state before any retry, never blindly. `clipboardReadTimedOut`: "Timed out waiting for the application to
     read the clipboard: ⌘V was sent, but no app read the pasted text in time. Whether anything was entered is unknown:
     observe the field with get_app_state before any retry, and never retry blindly." (a late read, or a late ⌘V
     after the restore, can still enter something). `pasteUnconfirmed`: "The paste could not be confirmed: ⌘V was sent and
     the pasted text was read from the clipboard, but no change was seen in the field. The text may have arrived:
     observe the field with get_app_state before any retry, and never retry blindly." type_text's paste route appends
     what was confirmed and that the failed line's state is unknown: " Confirmed entered: 3 of 8 characters (1 of 3 lines
     complete); line 2 is in an unknown state." (one line: "…; the line being pasted is in an unknown state.").
     `entered` still excludes the failed line, so the count is what landed for certain. The service passes the message
     through unchanged (`Computer Use server error -10005: …`).
  3. **No confirmation channel answers at once, gated on the read's timing.** When the baseline is empty (the focused
     element exposes neither a selected range nor a character count) and no trusted notification source is registered,
     nothing could show an effect, and the paste answers as soon as the read came, with no wait. "No channel" (cua
     cannot look) is not "looked and saw nothing", so the vendor's 100 ms blind success (the vendor never reaches it:
     its observer is always created) becomes **success with an unconfirmed note when a hand-over (of any representation) came at or
     after ⌘V's post** (a read after ⌘V is positive evidence of delivery, with none against it; an error would cost every
     Korean type_text into such a field an observation and stop multi-line text at every line), and **`pasteUnconfirmed`
     when the only read came before ⌘V** (a foreign reader's: the provider is not asked again for the target). The
     success's text reaches the model as a content item of the tool result: the engine's result carries `note`, and
     the service passes it to `nodeRepl.emitContentItem`, since the facade returns nothing of an input's result to model
     code. paste: "Pasted, unconfirmed: the app read the text after ⌘V, but this field can't be read back, so the change
     couldn't be seen. Observe the field if the next step depends on it." type_text goes on to the next line and counts
     them: "3 lines pasted, 1 unconfirmed: the app read each after ⌘V, but this field can't be read back, so the change
     couldn't be seen. Observe the field if the next step depends on it." Such lines count in `entered`; a later line's
     error says how many of the complete lines were unconfirmed ("2 of 3 lines complete, 1 of them unconfirmed").
     `meta.paste` carries `confirmed: false`, the `stage` and `unconfirmedLines`. A registered trusted observer keeps the
     2 s wait and answers `pasteUnconfirmed` at its end. **A Chromium or Electron
     process's notifications are trusted only while #244's enablement holds for it** (`WebAccessibility.enablementHolds`:
     the attribute owned by the engine, or written or read true at the process generation's last observation, and not
     written back since; the role read's Basic mode alone does not count), since its page posts no `AXValueChanged` with
     web accessibility off; otherwise its observer is untrusted, counts as no source, and its notifications never
     confirm. Chromium and Electron are told by the Info.plist's principal class (`AtomApplication`,
     `BrowserCrApplication`), not by web-shapedness, which Safari's URL scheme also meets.
  4. **Diagnostics on every paste, failure or success** (`meta.paste`, carried as `cua/paste` and kept in the native
     matrix's engine meta), for the last line pasted or tried: which wait ended it and how (`readWait`, `effectWait`
     with "deadline" and "noChannel"), each wait's time, the first hand-over's time against ⌘V's post, signed, so a read
     before ⌘V (a clipboard manager's) is visible, the main thread's lag (a block queued on it at ⌘V: how long it waited,
     or at least how long when it had not run at the answer, since the hand-over is served there), the restore's outcome
     (restored, skipped because the count moved, or not needed), the notification source and whether the baseline was
     empty. Never the clipboard's contents, the field's text or its reading. **3a's predicate, `keyFocusAtPaste`,
     recorded only:** read just before each ⌘V, it holds when the app is frontmost or the window server's key-focus
     process (`CPSGetKeyFocusProcess`) is the app's or the keys' target's, and the app's focused element lies in the
     window route 3 prepared (`primaryWindow`, the app's focused window first). The inputs ride beside `holds`: two
     process reads and two Accessibility reads, no event. It is the cheapest analogue of the vendor's
     `targetForKeyboardEvent` after `safelyEnforceActiveState` (`FUN_1001b382c`), which cua does not run; hold 3's
     Chrome background rows are the suspected case of an app "active" with no key focus. It never refuses in this
     change: refusal, under #188's preparation family ("the field could not be given the focus"), waits for the live
     slot to show the predicate separates delivered from undelivered pastes.
  5. **The deadline binds both waits.** Each wait takes the lesser of its 2 s and the request's remaining deadline, and a
     deadline that ends it first answers that stage's error (`clipboardReadTimedOut` or `pasteUnconfirmed`), recorded as
     "deadline"; the client's grace past the deadline lets that answer arrive. The paste lock's wait was already bound.
     The effect poll's own reads are bound too (the review of 2026-11-01, P2): each of `effectReading`'s three AX
     requests takes the wait's time left as its messaging timeout (at most 1 s, at least 50 ms, the observation's
     `deadlineBounded` rule, now `boundedMessagingTimeout`), none is sent once the time is spent, and a changed reading
     that came back after the time ran out is not accepted, so a slow target's late reply answers the stage's error
     rather than confirming a line and moving multi-line typing on.
  Restore and concealed safety are unchanged: the restore runs on every path, guarded by the change count, and a
  concealed request never pastes. Proven offline on `runPaste`'s seams (the fake clock, the test's own pasteboard, the
  main-thread probe, the key-focus read), the trust rule and `enablementHolds` on the enablement fakes, and the engine's
  trust decision on a stub Chromium bundle; nothing here posted, activated or touched the general pasteboard. Not in
  this change: making background Chrome take ⌘V (3b, an attended A/B under these diagnostics) and the late ⌘V after a
  restore pasting the person's clipboard, which no engine step can stop once ⌘V is posted (the vendor shares it).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on #187.
  *Amended 2026-11-01 (3b, `fix/187-3b-chrome-focus`, the design session's 3b ruling): 3b is the attended slot's B arm,
  behind an engine.json switch, and ships only on the slot's evidence.* **The mechanism the design session gave:** route
  3's AppKit-defined subtype 1 only sets the app's `isActive` and arms `_mouseActivationInProgress`; the window becomes
  key at the next mouse down (AppKit's key-only branch, no ordering). Route 3's own click supplies that mouse down; a
  paste posts none, so background Chrome, whose `AXActivationPoint` lies outside its frame (U13) and so takes no
  activation click under the inert-point rule (the focus hold's review, 2026-10-22, fix 3), is left active with no key
  window, and ⌘V goes nowhere. The vendor supplies it: `enforceActiveState(app.primaryWindow)` (`FUN_1001b2ed8`, before
  the pasteboard is written; F §7.1, §5.5) posts subtype 1 plus a left click at the window's `AXActivationPoint`
  wherever the point is (`FUN_10071f65c`, which skips the click only without a window number or a frame). **The arm:**
  `pasteFocusEnforcement: true` in engine.json (absent or false: arm A, today's behaviour unchanged) lifts the
  inert-point rule's outside-frame skip for the paste route's activation only (paste and type_text's paste route): an
  activation point outside a known frame, with a known window number, takes the activation click as every route-3
  activation click is made (the same pair, order and recipe: the window number in the `NSEvent`, fields 91 and 92 set to
  it, the window location translated by the frame's origin, no flags, subtype 3, which is also the vendor's recipe in
  `FUN_10071f65c`), on the reasoning that a point outside the frame keeps the app's views from taking it as a click. The
  inside-frame rule (a control or an unread hit takes no click) and every other step stand: the activation's guard
  polls after the click and repairs a raise it causes; the paste route runs no delivery guard or after-action watch
  (those are the pointer actions', #200), so a raise later than the guard's end is the slot's order watchers' to see.
  The engine's `status` reports the switch (`pasteFocusEnforcement`, read by `cua engine status --json` as
  `.status.pasteFocusEnforcement`), since an unknown key is ignored and arm B could otherwise run silently as A; every
  paste's `meta.paste` carries `focusEnforcement` {`arm` "A"/"B", `activated` (this paste ran the activation pair),
  `point` (the activation point inside or outside the frame, or the frame unknown, or none), `liftedClick`, and, once
  ⌘V is posted, `activationToCommandVMs` and `clickToCommandVMs`}, value-free. **Not a lasting setting:** the slot's
  rule (B ships only if it delivers with zero page clicks, no menu opened, and window order and front app unchanged)
  decides, and the PR that follows either makes the lift the paste's behaviour, retiring the switch, or removes it.
  Tests: `PasteActivationTests` (switch off: the outside point takes no click; switch on, paste and type_text: the pair
  at the outside point with its window fields, the guard repairing its raise, the record; only the outside-frame skip
  lifted; the status reports the switch), `PasteOperationTests` (the record's times to each line's ⌘V),
  `ActivationClickTests` (a non-paste route's activation unchanged), `ConfigTests` (the key's strict parse).
  *Superseded 2026-11-01 by the inert-click entry (#187, `fix/187-3b-slot`, below): the switch, the arm and the
  paste-only lift are retired; a hold never ends with its arm set.*
- Decision (#188, 2026-11-01, `fix/188-electron-field-focus`; amends the input results' meta in Interfaces, "Observation
  (skyshot)"'s focus read and the method matrix's prepares): **an element click records how it was carried, every
  observation's meta names the focus a selection cannot mask, and the matrix's prepares stop inheriting an earlier
  row's selection.** "The field could not be given the focus" was never the engine's: it is the matrix prepare's check
  after its `__app.click(index)`, which read the focus off the trailer's "The focused UI element is N" line, and the
  renderer prints that line only when nothing is selected (render.mjs trailer). A focused field still holding an earlier
  row's selection (drag.select's, selectText's "matrix") read as unfocused, which made four cua rows invalid in every run
  since hold 2a: electron background typeText.replace, pressKey.tab and paste.replace@finder, and
  electron/frontmost/paste.replace. The vendor passes them because its click leaves no selection; cua's click on the
  Electron field is an AXPress (the field advertises it), which keeps the selection.
  1. **The click's record** (`meta.elementClick`, carried as `cua/elementClick` and kept in the matrix's engine meta):
