     `plan` ("action" with its `action`, or "synthesized"), the ladder's `reason` ("advertised", "descendant": the gate,
     "noAction", "notPlainLeft", "forced": the spike's), and when a hit-test was taken (the gate's, or a synthesized
     click's reach check, recorded on a refusal too) its `hit` against the element ("element", "descendant", "ancestor",
     "other", "noAnswer") and `hitTest: "application"`, since cua hit-tests the owner's application element where the
     vendor hit-tests under the element's window (F-helper-logic.md:79). This turns hold 3's inferred AXPress into a
     recording. Response meta is the cell's last call's, so a prepare's click is overwritten by its later calls: the
     record is read where the click is its cell's last call (a click row's act, a cell of its own).
  2. **The focus in the observation's meta.** `cua/observation` now rides on every observation: `focusedIndex`, the
     index the observation gave the app's AXFocusedUIElement (the read the trailer prints; null when it named none), and
     `focusedRole`, both added by the service from the Skyshot's `focused` token and its numbering; and the engine's
     `focusRead` (an AXError, "notAnElement" or "outsideTheWalk"), now on the whitelist, when the read named no element.
     The app's focused element, not the per-node AXFocused flag, because it is what keys reach and what the trailer
     shows: hold 10's forced arms had AXFocused true while the application's read failed. Value-free: indexes and roles,
     never text, and never the model's text.
  3. **The matrix's prepares stand alone.** A prepare that needs its field focused (focusOn in `plan.mjs`: selectedIn,
     the Tab cases) clicks when the trailer does not show the field focused; if a selection still masks the trailer, it
     empties the field as a stated step (`prepared: ["field emptied to clear a selection"]` on the row; writing the same
     value back does not collapse a Chromium selection), where it has a value to write back (TextEdit's selectedIn has
     none and does not clear). A trailer still masked after that is its own reason, "the focus could not be read: a
     selection masks the trailer", never "could not be given the focus". The row then judges the focus by cua's
     `focusedIndex` of the prepare's last observation, the state the act starts from, in the trailer's place whenever
     it is there (`focusReadBy: "observation"`, else "trailer"; the vendor reports no meta). `emptyField` (the probe
     cells and the Unicode matrix) names its emptying (`prepared: ["field emptied"]`) and the masked reason alike.
  4. **The product question is kept out of the matrix.** Whether cua's click on a text field should keep an earlier
     selection (an AXPress keeps it, a posted click collapses it to a caret, and an AXPress cannot place a caret at the
     click's point) is answered by its own recorded-only live cell in the #187 slot (frontmost Electron field, cua and
     one vendor cell), not by these rows, which clear the selection and say so.
  5. **Limit, deferred:** the application's focus read answers AXError -25212 once no activation is held (hold 10's
     forced arms): `focusedIndex` is then null and `focusRead` names the error, and a prepare judged by it reads the
     field unfocused. No row depends on it: the matrix's rows run inside the held activation.
  Proven offline: the record's reasons and wire in `ElementClickRecordTests`; the service's focus meta and click record
  in `services-engine-service.test.mjs`; the prepares, the stated clear, the masked reason and the verdict's preference
  in the native matrix's plan, run and probe-plan tests. Nothing live ran; the four rows are judged at the next matrix
  sitting.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on #188.
- Decision (#187, 2026-11-01, `fix/187-3b-slot`; amends #187's entry above, items 3–4, "Text"'s paste route and the
  input results' `meta.paste` in Interfaces): **a Return between two of type_text's pasted lines is settled before the
  next line's baseline, a Return that shows no effect stops the text unconfirmed at its own stage, and every line gets
  a value-free record.** The slot's live false success (B#3.5 and chrome-korean-B#0.14, `줄1⏎줄2` into Chrome): `entered`
  5 and no error, line 2 "confirmed" by `effectWait: reading` 1–2 ms after its ⌘V, while the page held "줄1\n" and line 2
  never landed. Line 2's baseline was taken right after the Return was posted, with no wait for the Return's own effect,
  so the Return's late newline (a moved caret or count) was the change that confirmed line 2; Electron and TextEdit show
  the same 1–5 ms confirmation of line 2 and did land it, so the check was structurally blind, not Chrome's.
  1. **The settle.** A Return followed by a later pasted line takes a baseline just before it is posted (on the record
     of the line it opens), then waits for its effect, bounded as a line's effect wait is (2 s or the request's deadline,
     the effect poll's 25 ms): with a baseline, the reading must move from it and then hold for one poll, since a
     reading's three requests are not atomic and a late half of the Return's effect must not land after the next
     baseline, and a reading that moved but had not held when the time ran out is the timeout of item 2, never success
     (the review of 0d888300..c32bec9c, finding 3: a range moved at 1975 ms and a count at 2025 ms settled at 2000 ms on a
     half-applied baseline, and the Return's late count then "confirmed" a dropped next line); with none, a trusted
     notification from the Return's post on, the gate armed just before it as it is for a ⌘V, so a field's only report
     arriving while the Return's delivery guard samples counts (finding 2: a text beginning with a newline lost it); with
     neither, nothing is waited for (`noChannel`: the next line, equally blind, is answered by its read after ⌘V, item
     3's rule). A reading that came back after the time ran out is not accepted, as in the effect wait. The next line's
     baseline is then read fresh, so it holds the Return's effect and only that line's own paste can confirm it. A
     Return after the last pasted line is not settled: no later baseline depends on it, and a search field's submitting
     Return may change nothing, which would make `query⏎` an error. The cost is about two polls per Return between lines (~50 ms).
  2. **No effect is unconfirmed, never success.** A Return whose effect never shows in the bound answers
     `pasteUnconfirmed` (the same name, so the wrapper's classification and type_text's count clause apply unchanged)
     with its own sentence: "The text could not be confirmed: a Return was sent for a newline between lines, but no
     change was seen in the field, so the next line was not pasted. The Return may have arrived: observe the field with
     get_app_state before any retry, and never retry blindly." The next line is not pasted (nothing after an unseen
     Return could be confirmed), the Return's units are not counted in `entered`, and `meta.paste` is the line it
     opened, `stage: "returnEffect"`, `confirmed: false`. A change count that moved during the settle does not turn it
     into `clipboardConflict`: no one's contents were pasted at that stage (the restore stays guarded as ever).
  3. **Per-line records** (`meta.paste.perLine`, oldest first, one per line reached: its paste began, or the settled
     Return opening it was posted; an empty line between two Returns has its Return's record only): `line`, `stage`,
     `confirmed`, `keyFocusAtPaste`, the read and effect waits with their times, `readAfterPostMs`,
     `firstReadAfterPostMs` and the new `effectAfterPostMs` (the effect wait's end from the line's own ⌘V, the
     confirmation's time when confirmed), `baseline` and `confirming` as numbers only ({`range` [location, length],
     `characters`}), and `openingReturn` {`settle`, `settleMs` from the Return's post, `toCommandVMs` from it to the line's
     ⌘V}. The top-level fields stay the last line's, for the kit and the matrix, which read them. The readings' numbers
     are new on the wire: item 4's "never … its reading" now means never the text, while counts and offsets are kept,
     since they are what separates a line's own change from a neighbour's.
  Not addressed: a trusted notification from the Return, or from the previous line, delivered after the next line's
  hand-over still counts for that line (the notification gate is "after this line's hand-over and ⌘V"), and a line
  confirmed by a notification leaves the next Return's baseline possibly ahead of the reading; neither was seen in the
  slot, whose confirmations were by reading. Why Chrome read line 2's data and inserted nothing stays open; with this
  change such a line answers `pasteUnconfirmed` after its 2 s, and the per-line records say what moved. Proven offline
  on `runPaste`'s fakes (`PasteOperationTests`: a Return landing 10 ms late no longer confirms an undelivered line 2,
  and failed before the change; a Return that never lands, by the bound and by the deadline; one half-landing across
  the bound, unconfirmed; a leading Return's notification during its delivery guard counted (both failing before the
  review's fixes); the moved count during a
  settle; the records' shape over an empty line; a trailing Return; a blind field; the settle on its own); the earlier
  multi-line tests' fake Returns now land, as a real field's do. Nothing live ran; the slot's typeText.lines rows judge
  it next.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on the slot's false confirmation.
- Decision (#187's inert click, 2026-11-01, `fix/187-3b-slot`; amends route 3 in "Input" and the input results' meta and
  `meta.paste.focusEnforcement` in Interfaces; replaces maws-3f's skip-only rule of 2026-10-22 and the 3b amendment's
  paste-only lift): **a hold never ends with its arm set.** The live evidence: cua's click-less subtype 1 to an
  inactive Chrome, whose point lies outside its frame, armed `_mouseActivationInProgress`; the later subtype 2 left the
  arm in place; at the person's later real activation Chrome was frontmost with no key window and ⌘V was never read.
  The AppKit reading (static, macOS 26.6.2, `~/.local/share/187/appkit-outside-click.md` and `appkit-active-subtype1.md`
  on the MacBook): a pid-posted mouse down is routed by its window number, never by its point; under the arm it takes
  #200's branch 1 (key and main, the arm cleared, no ordering request), and at a point outside the frame its hit view
  is nil, so neither it nor its mouse up reaches a view. Where the click at the window's `AXActivationPoint` is skipped
  (`outsideFrame`, `hitTestFailed`, `control`, `actionsUnknown`, `noPoint`), an inert click consumes the arm, at a
  moment that depends on the holder:
  1. **Early, for a holder that posts no left mouse down of its own** (paste and type_text's paste route, a ⌘ key
     equivalent's focus hold, the element action plan's AXPress and the element holds of set_value, select_text and
     the secondary action, the menu record, a non-left click): right after subtype 1, before the holder's input, since
     keys and ⌘V need the key window first. Such a holder joining a hold whose arm no mouse down has consumed posts the
     kept inert click itself, before its input (`HeldActivations.acquire`); once consumed, a joiner posts nothing.
  2. **None early, for a holder that posts its own left mouse down** (a left coordinate click, a synthesized element
     click and focusField's fallback click, a drag; `postsLeftMouseDown`): its events are exactly what they were before
     the inert click existed, and its own mouse down consumes the arm on branch 1 with no ordering, which #200's work
     keeps. A background coordinate click into Chrome is the most common agent action, and an early click there would
     turn its own click into an ordering click into a key window (#200's raise). The two are serialized on the arm's
     lock (the review of 0d888300..c32bec9c, finding 1): such a holder claims a set arm before its events go out
     (`FakeActivation.postAction`) and consumes it once they are out; a joiner of item 1 waits out the claim and then
     finds the arm consumed, and an inert click's own down and up go out under the lock, so no inert click lands
     between a drag's down and its up, nor ahead of the action's mouse down with branch 1's no-ordering lost. A holder
     stopped before its events never claims, leaving the arm to item 3. (Amended 2026-11-05, "#261's hold join" below:
     such a holder joining a hold of its own connection whose arm was spent early re-arms it.)
  3. **At release**, when the hold's last holder deactivates and no left mouse down stamped with a window number reached
     the process (an intervention or the activation's guard refusing the action, an error before its events): the kept
     inert click goes out before subtype 2 (`FakeActivation.deactivate`). AppKit's arm is the application's, so a mouse
     down to any window of the process consumes it.
  4. **The click.** A left down and up with the activation click's recipe, stamped with the window's number, at the
     `AXActivationPoint` itself when it lies at least 8 pt beyond the frame, else at (frame.minX − 24, frame.midY) in
     global coordinates (`inertClickActivationPointClearance`, `inertClickFrameOffset`, Routes.swift): a titled resizable
     window's resize band reaches 4 pt outside its frame, where the hit is the theme frame and resize tracking may
     start. A point off every screen is fine. A click at an inert activation point inside the frame is posted right
     after subtype 1 for every holder, as it always was.
  5. **The exceptions, click-less and recorded:** the activation point in a non-left click's target (`spared`), a window
     with no number (`noWindowNumber`: nothing to stamp, and AppKit routes by nothing else) and a window whose frame is
     unknown (`noFrame`, accepted by the design session as the third: no point is known to lie outside it, so a click
     there could land in the resize band or on a view). An inert point inside a sparing rect would be spared too (a rect
     inside the frame never holds it).
  6. **The record.** `activationClick` keeps the decision at the point (`posted` only there; the lift's rewrite of it
     to `posted` is gone). The new `activationClickKind` names the click that consumed the arm and when: {kind
     `activationPoint`, when `early`}, {kind `inertOutside`, `point` `activationPoint` | `offset`, when `early` |
     `release`}, {kind `action`, when `consumedByAction`}, or {kind `skipped`, `skippedReason`}; value-free. It rides in
     the input meta of the action that ran the activation or consumed its arm, and of a joiner that posted the kept click
     (passed on as `cua/activationClickKind`), and as `click` in `meta.paste.focusEnforcement`, which drops `arm` and
     `liftedClick`.
  7. **The switch is retired.** engine.json's `pasteFocusEnforcement` is no longer read: any value is ignored as every
     unknown key is, and startup is unaffected. `status` no longer carries it (dropped rather than reported as ignored:
     there is no arm left to confirm). The #187 kit (`scripts/cursor-sitting/item-187.mjs`) still reads
     `.status.pasteFocusEnforcement`, `focusEnforcement.arm` and `liftedClick`, so until it adapts it reads every row's
     arm unconfirmed. The shared-hold caveat is answered by item 1's join.
  8. **Unchanged:** the agent cursor never draws the click (it learns of an action's events only from the route's
     announce hook; the activation posts to the process alone); no subtype 2 goes to a process that was frontmost before
     the action (`activate` posts nothing to an app that believes it has focus, `deactivate` skips a frontmost one, and
     posts no release click then either).
  Rejected: a frontmost rule (click whenever the target has no key window), because subtype 1 does not arm an app that
  is already active, so that click would take branch 3 with an order-front request on the person's front app, and
  because it treats a symptom cua creates; keeping the lift on the paste route alone (every other no-click route-3
  activation of Chrome left the same arm); and the first build of this entry (5e982c02), which posted the inert click
  early on every route, so a pointer action's own click met an already-key window as an ordering click, a new raise on
  Chrome, the design session's ruling against it. Proven offline (`ActivationClickTests`: each skip reason's inert click
  at the right point with its window fields, the 8 pt boundary both ways, the in-frame click unchanged, the three
  exceptions, the meta; Chrome's left click and drag posting exactly their own events with `consumedByAction`; a refused
  pointer action, by an intervention and by its guard, getting the release click before subtype 2; a non-left click and
  a registry holder with no click getting it early, a joiner of an unconsumed hold posting it and a later one nothing;
  a joiner on another thread while a drag's events go out posting nothing between its down and up (failing before the
  claim);
  the cursor told once; no subtype 2 to a previously frontmost process; the pointer, refusal and join cases failing
  under the always-early build; `FocusHoldTests`: a focus hold on Chrome clicks before its input;
  `PasteActivationTests`: the paste's early click under its delivery guard, a paste joining an unconsumed hold clicking
  before its ⌘V; `PasteOperationTests`, `EngineSettingsTests`, the route tests' pre-existing sequences restored, and the
  service's pass-through). Nothing live ran.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for the design session's rulings on #187's arm (the hold rule amended the same day).
- Decision (#200 on the paste route, 2026-11-01, `fix/187-3b-slot`; amends #200's entry, "Paste" in the Design, route 3
  in "Input" and the input results' `delivery` and `afterActionRepairs` in Interfaces): **the paste route's events go out
  through route 3's delivery guard and after-action watch, as a pointer action's do.** #200 promised them for the paste
  route, which ran its own activation and ⌘V with neither, so the slot had to watch window order itself (its 5 ms order
  watcher saw no raise over the paste rows, item-187.md); since the inert-click entry above every paste activation posts
  a click and makes the window key before ⌘V. A safety net, then, not the fix of a seen raise.
  1. **The guard.** The paste's own activation leaves its hold open (`holdsThroughDelivery`: its guard settles on one
     sample), and the paste's first event, its first ⌘V or the Return opening a text that begins with a newline, goes out
     through `FakeActivation.deliver` inside that hold, checking the paste's window (the primary window's number; a
     shared activation takes a hold of its own, as a pointer action's does). One guard and one hold an action, so the
     later lines and Returns are the watch's. A stop before the first event releases the hold.
  2. **The watch covers the whole paste.** `RepairWindow` (Activation.swift) keeps it open while the paste runs, whatever
     its read, effect and Return-settle waits, and closes it 1 s after the paste's last ⌘V or Return; the person's input
     is still read against the first event, so their input at any point of the paste ends it; its other rules are
     #200's. It samples on its own thread: the paste's waits, its restore and its answer never wait for it, the restore
     still runs before the paste answers, and no repair touches the pasteboard. Its repairs are keyed by
     `delivery.postedAt` (the first event's) and their latency is from it.
  3. **The record**: `delivery` on paste's and type_text's paste-route results, #200's shape; `afterActionRepairs` as on
     any result. The service already passes both on.
  4. **The seam**: the paste's keys go to the keys' target through the activation system's post (`gatedPost` in the
     engine, as before), so a unit test runs a paste to its end on route 3's stubs.
  Costs: the guard's 60 ms minimum and three stable samples after the first event before the read wait begins (a read
  during the guard is seen at once; `readAfterPostMs` stays from ⌘V's post), about +60–70 ms per paste into an app that
  did not believe it had focus; and the display held from the activation to the guard's end, at most the 100 ms cap,
  where the activation's guard released it about 30 ms in before (the snapshot, the baseline read and the write now fall
  inside the hold). Rejected: one guard around the whole operation (nothing would sample while it ran, a raise at line 1
  showing until its end, and the guard would hold the answer after the restore); a guard per ⌘V and Return (a hold per
  event against #200's one an action, and about 60 ms a line); reading the person's input against the latest event (the
  person's own input between two lines would be undone). Proven offline (`PasteActivationTests`: the first ⌘V's raise
  undone inside the activation's one hold, paste and type_text; a raise between lines after the first ⌘V's second
  repaired, the paste answered restored with the watch still running, a repair inside the second after the last ⌘V and
  none past it, both failing before the change; a stop before ⌘V releasing the hold). Nothing live ran.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on the paste route's guard.
- Decision (#259's diagnostic, 2026-11-01, `fix/187-3b-slot`; amends the input results' meta in Interfaces): **a
  coordinate click records its placement, value-free, from the screenshot point to the posted events**
  (`meta.coordinateClick`, carried as `cua/coordinateClick`). #187's slot (cell 3 (ii)) saw cua's `click([120, 168])`
  into the frontmost Electron fixture land one text line (~16 px) low where the vendor's, given the same point, landed on
  target; the offset's source (a title-bar or content inset, the screenshot's origin, the scale, rounding, or the window
  location the events carry) cannot be read from the results as they stand. The record names each step: the screenshot
  point as given, the window-local point the conversion computed (`ObservedScreenshot.windowPoint`), the global
  location the events are posted at, the window location they are stamped with (through the one helper `stampWindow`
  stamps by, `stampedWindowLocation`; null with no window number), the AX frame the conversion used, and the frame read
  again in the click's `beforePosting`, after its activation and just before its events (one AX frame read per
  coordinate click; null when the click stopped before). Numbers only. The conversion is unchanged: the record is #259's
  evidence for the live cell, which decides the fix. Proven offline (`CoordinateClickRecordTests`: the wire for #259's
  cell, the posted fields against the click events' own, the meta; the service's pass-through and reset). Nothing live
  ran.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, cursor235's implementer for #259's diagnostic.
- Decision (#261's hold join, 2026-11-05, `fix/187-3b-slot`; amends #187's inert-click entry above, item 2, and route 3
  in "Input"): **a holder that posts its own left mouse down, joining a hold of its own connection whose arm was spent
  early, re-arms it.**
  1. **What changed.** In `HeldActivations.acquire`, a joiner with `postsLeftMouseDown` (a left coordinate click, a
     synthesized element click, focusField's fallback click, a drag) that finds a current activation whose arm was
     consumed early, by the inert click outside the frame or an earlier holder's own mouse down
     (`FakeActivation.armSpentOutsideActivationPoint`), ends it (its deactivation, subtype 2; the release click is a
     no-op, the arm being consumed) and runs its own in its place (subtype 1, the inert click kept for the release, its
     guard), keeping every holder's share. A lingering focus hold carries over under the new activation, and its next ⌘
     key or element action finds the window key. The joiner's own mouse down consumes the new arm through `postAction`
     (`activationClickKind` {kind `action`, when `consumedByAction`}), and the new activation's `activationGuardMs`,
     `activationClick` and kind are recorded as a run's are.
  2. **Why.** Item 1 of the entry above made every no-click holder spend the arm early. A ⌘ key's focus hold or an
     element action's lingers up to 120 s, and a coordinate click into the same app within it then posted its own mouse
     down into an already-key window of an app believing itself active: an ordering click, #200's raise, which the
     delivery guard repairs but the person may see. Before the entry above, that hold left Chrome's arm set, and the
     click consumed it with no ordering. ⌘L or an AXPress into background Chrome followed by a click into it is a common
     sequence, so the inert click would have introduced the raise there.
  3. **Its own connection.** The re-arming happens only when every current holder belongs to the joiner's connection.
     Each caller passes an `ActivationHolder` (its `ConnectionState.id`): the focus hold, the menu record, the pointer
     routes, the forced-key route and paste. A connection runs one request at a time, so a holder on the joiner's own
     connection is a lingering hold, never one mid-action. The registry now tracks holders by share (each acquire's own,
     which its release removes) rather than by a count.
  4. **Not under a recorded menu.** When a recorded menu's span holds the activation (`ActivationHolder.menuSpan`), the
     join is today's: withdrawing the activation the menu's items were computed under could close or disable the menu the
     model is about to click into (the menu's held activation, 2026-10-21).
  5. **Not when the activation point's click consumed it** (the implementer's narrowing of the ruling, reported to the
     design session). Where the click at an inert activation point inside the frame spent the arm (TextEdit, Finder,
     Electron), a new activation would post that click again, and the joiner's mouse down would still meet a key window.
     Re-arming would only add a subtype 2/1 pair and a guard, so these keep today's join and the raise the guard repairs,
     which predates the inert click.
  6. **A failed guard.** The joiner is refused as `activationFailed`, as a run's failure is, with the new activation
     undone (its kept inert click, then subtype 2). The old activation stays held, marked withdrawn, with the remaining
     holders' shares kept and the joiner's not added. It is treated as a stale one: the hold's next acquire or `refresh`
     (its next ⌘ key or element action) runs a new activation with its guard, and its last release posts no second
     subtype 2. A deactivation that cannot post keeps today's join. Rejected: dropping the entry, because the lingering
     holders' releases would then have nothing to release; and restoring the old activation, which would mean another
     subtype 1 and guard inside a refusal.
  7. **No hold is left a set arm** (the review of 4ea25f9f..4d68fee4, one P2). A joiner that re-armed and then stopped
     before its events (an intervention, events not made) used to leave the new arm set under the hold that remained, and
     the hold's next key, which only refreshes an already-held activation, met no key window. Now two guards prevent
     this. At release, a pointer share that leaves only holders with no mouse down of their own posts the kept inert
     click if the arm is still set; it is recorded in that action's meta as {`inertOutside`, when `release`}. And the
     already-held path (`refresh`, the focus hold's next key or element action) consumes a set arm before its input, as
     a no-click joiner of `acquire` does, recorded in its meta as `early`. So no-click input never meets a set arm,
     whatever left it set. As in `acquire`, the in-flight intervention check runs first, under the registry's lock (the
     re-review of 4d68fee4..23d68dae), so a user event stops the request with nothing posted.
  8. **The window number of the final subtype 2** (the reviewer's reading, settled statically). After a swap, the
     hold's last subtype 2 carries the click's window number, which may be a dialog's. On 25G83, subtype 2's dispatch
     (`-[NSApplication sendEvent:]`'s fast path, then `_handleAppKitDefinedEvent:`'s table, then
     `_handleDeactivateEvent:`) does not need that number to resolve to a live window: the handler's window lookup at
     +496 reads an application ivar, a failed lookup continues, and +1096 clears `isActive`. So a closed dialog does not
     keep the app active, and the swap keeps the click's window (`~/.local/share/187/appkit/d-act.txt`, `d-act2.txt`,
     `d-app-sendEvent.txt` on the MacBook). Application overrides and live behaviour are unverified; the dialog variant
     of the keys-then-click row is queued for the next slot.
  9. **Unchanged.** Another connection's holder present (it may be mid-action under the activation, a paste's ⌘V or a
     drag's events): today's join, so the joiner's ordering mouse down is left to the delivery guard to repair (ticket
     pending). A pointer joining a hold whose arm is still set (a skipped click): its own mouse down consumes the arm. A
     no-click joiner: item 1. The replacement of a stale activation.
  Proven offline only (`FocusHoldTests`: a ⌘ hold on Chrome and then a click on the same connection posting subtype
  2, subtype 1 and the click alone, recorded `consumedByAction` with its guard, the hold's share kept, the next ⌘ key
  inside the new activation and the hold's end withdrawing it once, failing before the change; a re-arming whose guard
  fails, refused, leaving the hold to activate again at its next key and to withdraw once, failing before the change; a
  no-click joiner consuming early and never re-arming; a pointer on a still-set arm, another connection's hold (alone
  and beside the clicker's own) and a recorded menu's span, each sharing as today; the existing click-inside-a-hold
  tests, at an inert activation point, unchanged; after the review, a re-arming click stopped before its events
  leaving the hold's next key no set arm, failing with both guards off, and each guard's own test, failing with that
  guard off). A keys-then-click row with a raise watcher is queued for the next
  attended slot.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on #261's hold join.
- Decision (#187's isolating slot, 2026-11-05, `fix/187-isolate`; the design session's rulings on the next slot's kit,
  item 1): **a control build without the inert click, cut at compile time, and the hold join named in the meta.**
  1. **The cut.** Under `CUA_CONTROL_NO_INERT_CLICK`, `activationClickPlan`'s one return of `.inertOutside` returns
     `.skipped(.controlBuild)` instead, so the arm is left set as before #187's inert click. That one return removes
     both the early and the release inert click, the release click being the plan's kept point. Everything else of
     #261 stays (the hold join, the multi-line paste, the paste route's guard and watch, stage meta); with no arm spent
     early, the hold join's re-arming never fires, the pre-#261 behaviour for that path. The `controlBuild` skip
     reason (on the wire as {kind `skipped`, skippedReason `controlBuild`}) and the ping's `variant:
     "control-no-inert-click"` (`engineVariant`, Version.swift) exist only under the condition, so the running binary
     says what it is, and a sitting attests each half from the engine's ping, not from the bundle's metadata.
  2. **Why at compile time.** The slot compares the merged engine with the same engine minus one behaviour. A runtime
     switch (an engine.json key, a request flag) would ship the regression's path in every release and let any
     configuration turn it on; a separate source branch would let the two halves drift. One `#if` around one return
     keeps the halves identical elsewhere, and a release cannot reach it.
  3. **How it stays out of a release.** The shipping Package.swift never defines the condition, and the shipping
     builder (`buildEngine`, src/runtime/engine.mjs) has no option for it. The kit copies native/engine/ whole into a
     scratch directory, adds the define to the copy's Package.swift and builds the copy with the unchanged builder;
     the build hash covers Package.swift, so the control has its own build id. ci-kit's `engine-swift` gate compiles
     the branch (`swift build -Xswiftc -DCUA_CONTROL_NO_INERT_CLICK`, its own scratch), so it cannot rot unnoticed;
     its tests are not run there, the shipping suite asserting the inert click.
  4. **`activationJoin` in the input meta.** Each `HeldActivations.acquire` and `refresh` that reaches the registry
     records how it came by the process's activation: `fresh` (no holder: a new one ran), `shared` (a current one
     shared, none run: a joiner, a no-click joiner consuming a set arm, or a holder's refresh of the one it holds),
     `rearmed` (a pointer joining its own connection's spent-arm hold: that one ended and a new one ran) and
     `replacedSpent` (a stale or withdrawn one replaced, by acquire or refresh). It is written before any activation
     runs, so a failed guard's refusal names the path it failed on; a request stopped before the registry (an
     intervention under its lock) or that acquires nothing leaves it absent. The last acquire or refresh of a request
     wins, so a click's meta names its own join. Before, the re-arming wrote the new activation's guard and click into
     meta, but nothing said the join happened; the keys-then-click row's premise reads it.
  5. **Carried to results.** The service passes `activationJoin` through as `cua/activationJoin` (reset per call, as
     every optional input key), and the client keeps a ping's `variant` in the engine's identity, so
     `cua/nativeEngine` names it ({engine, version, build, variant}); a release's identity is unchanged. The client
     still accepts an engine on its version and build alone: the variant attests, it does not gate.
  Proven offline (the shipping suite, 731 tests: each value asserted on the path that produces it in `FocusHoldTests`
  and `ActivationClickTests`, a click replacing a stale hold's activation added; the shipping ping naming no variant;
  `swift build` with the define compiling, and under it the inert-click and ping tests failing as they should; the
  client's and service's tests for the variant and the join's pass-through). Nothing live ran.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235's implementer for the design session's rulings on #187's isolating slot.
- Decision (#282, 2026-11-05, `fix/282-chrome-first-mouse`; the design session's ruling, 1ea561bc 33e95829, in the
  issue's body; amends route 3 in "Input", #187's inert-click entry and "#261's hold join" above): **the device mode
  reaches the macOS engine; in shared mode a background pointer into a Chromium web area is element-routed or refused
  with nothing posted, in dedicated mode the inert click goes first and nothing is put back, and everywhere a pointer
  that may only have activated its window answers `clickUnconfirmed`.**
  1. **The cause** (#187's isolating slot; the issue's file:line reading). Where route 3's activation clicks no
     AXActivationPoint (Chrome's lies outside its frame), a left pointer holder (`defersArmClick`) posts no early inert
     click, and its own down is the first mouse event after subtype 1 (`activationClickKind {action, consumedByAction}`).
     AppKit takes it as the activating click and asks `acceptsFirstMouse:`; Chromium's `RenderWidgetHostViewCocoa`
     answers NO below the popup levels, so the page gets neither the down nor the up, and the engine answered success.
     Since 5941e988 (#176).
  2. **The mode.** `desktopModeFor` (src/runtime/launch.mjs: `CUA_DESKTOP_MODE`, else engine.json's `desktop`, else
     `shared`) now resolves for a macOS session on cua's engine too; the launch puts it in the worker's environment, and
     the service (`desktopMode`) names it in every connection's `ping` as `desktop`, so the engine keeps it on that
     connection (`ConnectionState.desktop`), never engine-wide: one engine serves sessions of either mode. A ping naming
     none leaves `shared`; another value is invalid params. Doctor's macOS row reports it as the Linux row does (an
     invalid one fails the row).
  3. **The web-area test** (Routes.swift `pointerView`). The process is Chromium's by its principal class
     (`chromiumFamily`: `BrowserCrApplication`, `AtomApplication`; Electron's web contents are the same view), and the
     AX hit at the pointer's point (a coordinate click's and a drag's start: the window's hit-test; an element click's:
     its reach check's against the owner's application) is an AXWebArea or lies inside one, its parents walked up to
     300 levels and no further than its window. Chosen over `webShaped`, which counts any app with an `http` URL type
     (Safari, Firefox), whose views are not Chromium's: the known-accept rule below is Chromium's alone, and the
     honest result covers the rest.
  4. **The known-accept rule.** A Chromium process's native views (its toolbar: `BridgedContentView` accepts first
     mouse) count as known to accept, but only once an observation has seen a web area in the process (`webAreaSeen`):
     before its page tree is exposed, a page point may answer outside any web area and would be misread as native. And
     only when the walk established it (`webAreaWalk` `outside`: it reached the hit's window, sheet or application with
     every role and parent read; the review of 739346e5..8e859095, one P2): a failed parent read, an unreadable role or
     the 300-level bound running out is `incomplete`, since an AXWebArea may lie beyond. That is the one rule. A Chromium
     hit not so established (no hit, an incomplete walk, a page tree not yet seen) is a `possibleWebArea`, which shared
     mode treats as a web area (the element route, else the refusal): the owner's rule refuses input the app may ignore,
     and a plain click with an action still goes. Outside Chromium everything is `unknown`.
  5. **What is affected: the activation the pointer meets clicked no activation point** (the implementer's reading,
     wider than the ruling's "the pointer's down would consume the arm"). A new activation's click plan is read before
     subtype 1 (the activation point and the hit-test there are window geometry, which subtype 1 does not move), so
     the refusal posts nothing and holds no screen updates; a held one counts when it ran and its click was not the
     activation point's (`activatedWithoutActivationPointClick`), checked under the registry's lock before any join,
     withdrawal or run. This takes in a pointer joining a spent hold (⌘L then a click, focusField's fallback inside
     set_value's held activation, another connection's hold, a recorded menu's), because with the re-arming narrowed
     (item 8) its down would meet a key window and order it front, #200's raise, which the owner's ruling keeps off a
     shared desktop; the ruling names focusField's fallback among the refusals, which only this reading reaches. Where
     the activation point's click made the window key (Electron, TextEdit) nothing changes: #188's Electron row
     delivers there.
  6. **Shared mode** (the default). A left click, double click or drag so affected into a Chromium web area is never
     posted (`PointerHold.refusedWithoutActivationPointClick`, thrown as the internal `PointerRefused` before anything is
     posted, so no activation or inert click is left behind and no hold ends with its arm set). A plain left click whose
     hit is an allowlisted control advertising AXPick or AXPress (`webAreaElementRoute`, item 13) is carried by that
     action exactly as an element click's (`elementActionClick`: the focus hold first when the app does not believe it
     has focus, then the action), its meta's `elementClick` reason `webArea`. Anything else (no action, a double
     click, a drag, focusField's fallback, which is refused and never pressed, since an action is no focusing) is
     `backgroundClickRefused` (-10005): `<App>'s web page does not accept a background click; bring <App> forward or use
     a dedicated desktop`, the Linux engine's form. Right and middle clicks are unaffected.
  7. **Dedicated mode.** Left pointers take the inert click right after subtype 1 (`defersArmClick` false), so their
     own down meets a key window and is delivered, and the action's events go out with no delivery guard and no
     after-action watch (`delivery` absent): the window may come forward and stays so, the Linux foreground route's
     choice. Scoped to the action: its activation's own guard is unchanged (three stable samples, its hold released by
     it), and a later shared-mode connection's action guards as ever. No re-arming.
  8. **`rearmed`** stays only for a shared-mode pointer into a view known to accept first mouse
     (`PointerHold.rearmsSpentArm`). Into any other view the new arm's consuming down may be lost; the spent arm's key
     window delivers it, the raise left to the delivery guard, as before #261.
  9. **`clickUnconfirmed`** (-10005), everywhere: when an action's own left mouse down claimed the activation's arm
     (`FakeActivation.postAction`) and its view is not known to accept first mouse, the action answers, after its
     events and with its full meta (the delivery guard's included), `The click could not be confirmed: it was sent to
     <App> in the background, but it may only have activated the window without reaching what is under the pointer.
     Observe the app with get_app_state before any retry, and never retry blindly.` (`drag` for a drag). An error,
     not a success with a note as the paste route's no-channel case: the loss has a known mechanism here, and the model
     must look before it goes on. The route's guard counts as passed for `routeAdvice`. In practice: non-Chromium apps
     whose activation point was not clicked, a Chromium process whose page tree was not yet seen, and a dedicated
     action whose inert click could not be placed.
  10. **Model-facing text.** `docs/cua-api/agent-cursor.md`, cua's own macOS-only first-call paragraph, gains a second
     paragraph with the refusal's sentence and `clickUnconfirmed` (the surface inventory's "Table 3 addition
     (2026-11-05, #282)"); `core.md`'s port is untouched. The two names join the set a concealed set_value's
     diagnostic repeats (src/services/sky.mjs), since focusField's fallback can stop under them.
  11. **Not done.** Route 2′ on a Chrome carrying Chromium 212c026a (the ruling's D) and WebKit's first-mouse rules
     (Safari's page refuses first mouse unless a selection may be dragged; it gets `clickUnconfirmed`, not a refusal).
  12. **The coordinate hit-test asks the application** (found live on the hosted runner, probe/282-hosted, runs
     42123497534 and 42124119631; the design session's conditions, 1ea561bc bbaa0eba). The coordinate click, the drag
     and the coordinate scroll asked AXUIElementCopyElementAtPosition of the window element, which answers
     kAXErrorIllegalArgument (-25201): their hit was always nil, so on 8e859095 shared mode posted a background Chrome
     click and answered `clickUnconfirmed`, neither refused nor element-routed. `hitTest(window:pid:at:)` now asks the
     window's process's application element and keeps the hit only when its first AXWindow ancestor is the target window
     (CFEqual); a hit in another window of the app, one whose ancestry cannot be read to a window, or no answer stays
     nil, a Chromium process's possible web area. What the real hit changes: those three requests' owner is the hit's
     process (checked against the forbidden list, its events counted as the user's on the app), a right or middle
     coordinate click's sparing area is the hit's frame rather than the one point, and the web-area walk sees the page
     (the element route now reachable). A hit that is itself the window, sheet or application is a coarse answer, an
     incomplete walk, never a view known to accept first mouse. The element click and focusField's fallback always hit-
     tested the owner's application (`ElementPointing`) and are unchanged.
  13. **The element route is an allowlist** (the design session's ruling, 1ea561bc fe37aee9, on the hosted A/B, run
     42130746324, two identical repetitions: AXPress on the page's AXTextArea answered success and did nothing, no focus
     and no events, #282's false success again). Shared mode presses only `webAreaPressableRoles`: AXButton, AXCheckBox,
     AXRadioButton, AXLink, AXPopUpButton, AXMenuItem, AXMenuButton, AXDisclosureTriangle and AXTab. No AXToggle:
     Chromium's role mapping (ui/accessibility/platform/ax_platform_node_cocoa.mm `nativeRoleFromAXRole`) exposes a
     toggle button and a switch as AXCheckBox (subroles AXToggleButton and AXSwitch), a tab as AXRadioButton (subrole
     AXTabButton) and a select as AXPopUpButton, so no web element answers AXToggle or AXTab, and the list covers them as
     AXCheckBox and AXRadioButton. Every other role, text entry included, and any hit with an `AXEditableAncestor`
     (text and editing are #283's) is `backgroundClickRefused`. A checkbox's or radio button's AXValue (so a toggle's, a
     switch's, a tab's) is read before the press and polled every 20 ms for up to 500 ms after it
     (`pressConfirmingValue`): unchanged, or unreadable after a readable one, or with no readable baseline at all (the
     press still goes, the model's intended action; the re-review of c768d2bb..1c8f2229), the click answers `clickUnconfirmed`
     (`pressUnconfirmed`'s sentence), never success. The criterion is that the control changes exactly once: Chrome's
     own AXPress on a checkbox synthesizes trusted pointer events in the page, which are Chrome's, not cua's posting,
     so no claim of zero pointer events is made for the element route.
  14. **The control, and its late action** (the hosted probe, run 42131623237; the design session's rulings, 1ea561bc
     7a6a3638). A hit at a control's point may be its child (the label's AXStaticText), so the hit resolves to the
     nearest allowlisted ancestor (`webAreaControl`), passing only through plain, non-interactive children
     (`webAreaPassableRoles`: AXStaticText, AXImage, AXGroup, the last Chromium's generic container), and that one is
     pressed. The walk refuses, as `backgroundClickRefused`, when it meets an editable element (a text-entry role or an
     `AXEditableAncestor`), any other role (another interactive one included), or the AXWebArea before a control, and
     when it cannot read the ancestry (a role or parent unread, or 300 levels): it never presses outside the web area.
     The meta's `elementClick` names the control's role (`pressedRole`) and whether it was the hit or an ancestor
     (`pressed`) and the walk itself, roles only from the hit up to where it stopped (`walk`), with why it stopped on a
     refusal (`walkStopped`, also recorded then); its reason stays `webArea`, since `descendant` names the gate's choice of
     synthesis. The probe showed
     the missing AXPress was Chromium's accessibility enablement coming up, not activation: with no activation at all a
     page checkbox began advertising AXPress 1.6 to 1.7 s after the engine's first observation (its enablementPrior
     false). So where the process's accessibility is fresh (under 10 s since its first observation by any connection or
     the engine's last enablement write attempt, failed or not, `WebAccessibility.freshSince`; or no web area seen in it
     yet: the final hosted A/B, run 42134748488, found the write failing on Chrome for Testing, so a freshness keyed on a
     successful write never opened, while Chrome reached its complete mode anyway 1.1 to 1.6 s after the observation; the
     observation now reports the attempt as `enablementWrite` "success" | "failed" and the AXError as
     `enablementWriteError`, value-free), a resolved control with no action is re-read every
     100 ms for up to 2 s, posting nothing, before the refusal (`webAreaActionRereading`); a role the walk refuses waits
     not at all. After the read or the re-read, and before any activation or press, the chosen control is requalified as
     it is then (`webAreaControlRequalified`, the re-review of c768d2bb..1c8f2229: a node may change during the wait): an
     allowlisted role, not editable, its parents read up to the AXWebArea; otherwise it is refused, nothing posted, and
     its action and value check follow its current role. The refusal of a control still without an action asks for an observation first
     (`backgroundClickRefusedObserveFirst`). **The cost:** up to about 3 s of latency on the first click into a fresh Chrome
     whose control has not yet exposed its action, and the whole wait when it never does (3 s since #292's re-read
     deadline).
  Proven offline (`BackgroundPointerTests`, 26, failing before the change where they bite: shared mode's refusal of
  a click, a double click and a drag into a web area with nothing posted, no hold taken and nothing left held; a plain
  click carried by the element route with nothing posted by cua; a web-area pointer joining its own or another connection's
  spent hold refused with the hold untouched; an Electron-shaped activation still posting; dedicated mode's inert click
  before the down, no restore, no watch, no re-arming; `clickUnconfirmed` for an unknown view's consuming click and
  drag and for a dedicated click whose inert click could not be placed, success for a known-accept view; an unknown
  view sharing a spent hold rather than re-arming; the web-area walk, its stop at the window, and after the review an incomplete walk (a failed parent read, an unreadable role, the bound run out with the web area beyond) never known to accept, a possible web area refused or element-routed in shared mode and unconfirmed in dedicated; the
  element route's allowlist (the textarea and an editable-ancestor hit refused, a button passing) and its value check (an unchanged checkbox answering clickUnconfirmed); the walk (a label child pressing its button, an editable element or another interactive role in between refusing) and the re-read (a late action found at 1.6 s and pressed, a timeout refused, no wait where not fresh); the ping's per-connection mode. The service's ping and pass-through, the launch's mode,
  doctor's row and the client's ping in their JS suites). Nothing live ran: the hosted Chrome for Testing A/B of the
  ruling's acceptance is the next step.
  Corrected by #292's entry below: the write "failing" on Chrome for Testing is Chrome's -25208 answer to the
  AXEnhancedUserInterface write, which takes effect.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235's implementer for the design session's ruling on #282.
- Decision (#283, 2026-11-05, `fix/283-web-text-focus`; the design session's rulings, 1ea561bc a9607cd9, on the
  proposal b93055dd:293; amends #282's entry above): **in shared mode a plain left click into a background Chromium
  web area's text field is carried by AXFocused, accepted only once it reads back true, with the caret placed at the
  point where the field's own character boxes locate it; the result's note says the field was focused, not clicked.**
  1. **The measurement** (#283's hosted probe, `probe/283-hosted`, runs 42146016617, 42147887133 and 42148369195;
     Chrome for Testing 156 on macOS 26.6.2 and 26.7.2, every rep alike). AXFocused on the field focuses an input, a
     textarea and a contenteditable region before Chromium's complete accessibility mode as after it, with no pointer
     event, TextEdit frontmost and Chrome's window order unchanged (102 cells); AX reads it true 65 to 142 ms after the
     write. A contenteditable region's hit is its AXStaticText, whose AXFocused is not settable and whose write answers
     success while doing nothing. AXPress does nothing before complete mode and, after it, makes Chrome play trusted
     pointer events into an input and a region: not this route. Focus alone puts the caret at the field's existing
     selection, else at its start. AXRangeForPosition answers {0,0} for every point and AXTextMarkerForPosition
     answers no value; AXSelectedTextRange {k, 0} after focus puts the caret at k on all three kinds, and type_text
     then lands there by route 2′. Round 2 (run 42150236590,
     https://github.com/SSFSKIM/cua/actions/runs/42150236590): before complete mode every character box is empty;
     AXVisibleCharacterRange answers the whole text even when the field is scrolled; a linear box scan of 3,074
     characters takes 165 to 185 ms. The region `alpha bravo <b>charlie delta</b><br>echo foxtrot <span>golf
     hotel</span> india` exposes five AXStaticText children of its AXTextArea and no element for the <br>, whose
     AXValue `alpha bravo charlie delta\necho foxtrot golf hotel india` counts the <br> as one character; a static
     text's AXNumberOfCharacters reads 0, its AXValue is its text. Its texts found in that value in order start at
     12 and 26 (a sum of lengths is one short after the <br>), and AXSelectedTextRange {start + local, 0} on the
     region placed the caret at both measured points, typing landing there. The live A/B at 2c016f7b (run 42152333871,
     https://github.com/SSFSKIM/cua/actions/runs/42152333871) found two faults: a static text reads
     AXVisibleCharacterRange {0, 0}, so a scan honoring it covered nothing and the region's caret was never placed
     (12 of 12 cells, `noCharacterBoxes` after the full 2 s); and a fresh textarea's boxes arrive line by line, so one
     click of 10 settled on `pointOutsideText` with the clicked line's boxes still empty.
  2. **The target** (`webAreaFocusTarget`). #282's walk stops at an editable element (`walkStopped editable`); the
     route then walks again from the hit to the nearest text-entry role (`webAreaEditableRoles`, which now holds
     AXSecureTextField) whose AXFocused is settable, through editable elements and #282's passable roles only, its own
     ancestry read up to the AXWebArea (`webAreaAncestry`, shared with the control's requalification). A text-entry
     role whose AXFocused is not settable refuses (`notFocusable`), as do another role, the web area and an unread
     ancestry; each refusal records the walk, as #282's does.
  3. **The focus** (`focusPlacingCaret`). Route 1 (`inputRoute action`), no activation: the in-flight check, the
     cursor at the point, AXFocused written and polled every 20 ms for up to 300 ms. A field that never reads focused
     answers `clickUnconfirmed`; the write's own answer never counts. #282's re-read gate does not apply.
  4. **The caret** (`caretOffset`, `fieldCaretOffset`, `regionTextStart`). The field's characters' boxes
     (AXBoundsForRange {i, 1}), scanned linearly over the whole value up to `caretScanLimit` characters
     (AXVisibleCharacterRange is not read: the whole text on a scrolled text field, {0, 0} on a static text): in the box that holds
     the point the caret goes before the character on its left half and after it on its right; to the right of a line's
     last box, the end of that line, before its break, taken only where that end is established (a line break's box,
     or the next offset being the text's end, a line break, or a character whose non-empty box starts on a lower line, read even
     outside the scanned range), since the rightmost box read need not be the line's last; an empty field is 0. A
     contenteditable region's AXStaticText hit is scanned the same way over its own AXValue, and its local offset
     mapped into the region's: the region's descendants walked depth-first in children order (at most
     `regionWalkLimit`, 2,000, nodes), each non-empty static text's value found in the region's AXValue onward from
     where the previous one ended, the hit's found location its start. Then AXSelectedTextRange {offset, 0} is
     written on the focused element and polled back there. Not placed (`elementClick.caret`): `secure` (a password
     field is focused only, its value and ranges never read; a field whose AXSubrole read fails, as against having none,
     counts as one), `unreadable` (AXValue did not read as a string: never taken for an empty field),
     `noCharacterBoxes` (the scan read no usable box at all, as before complete mode), `pointOutsideText` (boxes were
     read, but none holds the point and it is past no established line end; never a guessed offset; a miss while a
     character that should have a box had none is `boxesIncomplete` inside the scan, never recorded: item 6),
     `selectionMismatch`, `unmapped` (a region's hit that is not an AXStaticText, or whose start is not found: a value
     or a node's role or children list unreadable, a static text not found, the hit not reached, the walk's bound run out).
  5. **The answer.** `elementClick {plan action, action AXFocused, reason focusField | focusedNotPlaced, pressedRole,
     pressed hit | ancestor, walk, caret}`, and the result's `note`, which the service now passes for `click` as for
     paste and type_text: placed, "focused through accessibility, with the caret at the point; no mouse event was
     sent, so the page saw no click"; not placed, typing goes to the field's existing selection or its start, use
     select_text or set_value first. A double click, a drag and focusField's fallback stay refused (#289).
  6. **Boxes not there yet** (`caretAwaitingBoxes`; the design session's ruling 37d4c12c, widened by 9ba80c48 after
     run 42152333871). Focus is written and confirmed at once; then, where the caret answers `noCharacterBoxes` or
     `boxesIncomplete` (a miss while some character that should have a box had none: line breaks, whitespace, controls
     and format characters such as U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+FEFF and U+00AD, combining marks and a surrogate
     pair's trailing half may legitimately have none and never count (a pair is one character, its box on its leading
     half), so a multi-line field's newlines never make a click wait) and the
     process is fresh by #282's rule (`webAreaAccessibilityFresh`), the offset is computed again every 100 ms for up
     to 2 s (`webAreaActionRereadSampleMs`, `webAreaActionRereadMs`), posting nothing, until it answers anything else;
     a complete-miss `pointOutsideText` or `unmapped` found then, or at once, is the answer. An incomplete miss not
     waited for (not fresh) or still incomplete at 2 s is `pointOutsideText`. Before each re-read the hit must still resolve through
     `webAreaFocusTarget` to an element CFEqual to the one focused and not secure, or the wait stops with nothing more
     read; an offset found by a re-read is written only if the field also still reads AXFocused true, is still that
     element, and its AXValue's length is the one read when the wait began. Otherwise, still empty at 2 s, or not
     fresh (no wait at all), `noCharacterBoxes`, with no range written. The secure field never
     gets here. Limits: the first click into a fresh Chrome's text field can take up to about 3 s (#292's re-read deadline), as #288's does;
     with AXVisibleCharacterRange dropped, the scan is bounded only by the 5,000-character cap on the value's length
     (`caretScanLimit`; a longer value is `pointOutsideText`, #290).
  Proven offline (`WebAreaFocusTests`, 39: the field hit, the region's text hit focusing its region, each refusal, the
  secure field by role, by subrole and by a failed subrole read, focused with nothing read, a write that never reads
  back true, a caret read back elsewhere, a point in no box against no usable box at all, an unreadable value, the
  line-end rule only where the end is established (a failed box read, a cut scan, a soft wrap, an empty next box at
  negative screen y, the text's end) and the empty-line rule, the region mapping (round 2's five-node page at both
  points, a nested group, a repeated word, each unmapped case, an unread role among them), the wait for boxes
  (appearing at 1.6 s, never by 2 s, a process not fresh, each failed re-validation, a field turned secure mid-wait
  read no more, another answer on a re-read; boxes arriving partly then wholly placed, an incomplete miss at 2 s and
  when not fresh, a complete miss at once with a multi-line value's newlines, a trailing space and U+200B boxless,
  the characters that may lack a box, a surrogate pair as one character, an emoji line still arriving against a
  whole one), a static text scanned over its whole value, the in-flight stop before any
  write, the record and the note; the service's note test for click).
  Proven live (`docs/evidence/2026-11-05-web-text-focus/`; hosted runs 42155227806 and its repeat 42155231373 at af4bd331,
  macOS 26.6.2 and 26.7.2 with Chrome for Testing 156; the earlier run 42152333871 found the visible-range and
  half-arrived-boxes bugs fixed in af4bd331). Main refuses every text-field click. On the branch, the input, the textarea,
  a one-node and a multi-node contenteditable (in its 2nd and 3rd text node, after a `<br>`), a prior selection and a point
  past a line's end are focused, with the caret at the expected offset and type_text landing there: in 55 to 199 ms 3 s
  after the observation, and in 1.5 to 1.9 s 0.1 s after it, by the re-read (32 of 32 fresh clicks in the repeat run).
  The password field is focused only, and a padding click is focused but not placed. Every cell had zero pointer events,
  TextEdit stayed frontmost, and Chrome was not raised.
  Corrected by #292's entry below: the enablement write these runs report as failed (-25208) is Chrome's answer to the
  AXEnhancedUserInterface write, which takes effect.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235 for the design session's rulings on #283.
- Decision (#292, 2026-11-05, `fix/288-fresh-chrome`; the design session's rulings, 1ea561bc ecc2143d, 5c2f27a3 and
  199d194c, and the owner's: on a shared desktop AXEnhancedUserInterface is acceptable while cua drives that Chrome,
  provided cua releases it when done; corrects #282's and #283's entries above): **the engine owns and releases the EUI
  write Chrome answers with -25208, at end_task, at the engine's stop, or 30 s after cua's last observation of or input
  to that Chrome; an input after a release enables it again; the re-reads wait for Chrome's debounce.**
  1. **The correction** (#288's hosted spike, `probe/288-hosted`, runs 42161269408 and 42162367095; Chrome for Testing
     156). Chrome lists AXEnhancedUserInterface and not AXManualAccessibility (Electron's; Chromium's source has none),
     so the engine writes EUI to Chrome. Chrome answers that write with -25208 (kAXErrorNotImplemented) and acts on it:
     complete mode arrives about 2.2 s later (chrome_browser_application_mac.mm's 2 s debounce) and the attribute then
     reads true. #282's and #283's texts, and the evidence READMEs of 2026-11-05, call that answer a failed
     AXManualAccessibility write: it is the EUI write, and it takes effect. Since the engine took it for a failure it
     never owned the attribute, and Chrome kept complete mode, the screen-reader flag and animated window moves (3 to 5
     frames, 80 to 156 ms, against 2 frames and 10 to 90 ms) past the connection's close, the engine's SIGTERM and after.
  2. **Ownership** (`enableReporting`). A write answering -25208 counts as set when the attribute reads true right after
     it, and is owned by the A3 rule as any set: only when it read false before the write, or the engine wrote it back
     since. Still false after it is not set and not owned. `enablementWrite` reports "success" for such a set, with
     `enablementWriteError` -25208 beside it. The write back (`releaseIfUnobserved`) counts -25208 as done, unread (the
     attribute may read true after a write-back: AppKit's readback), and is not retried; Chrome drops complete mode in 4
     to 150 ms.
  3. **The release, whichever comes first.** (a) end_task: the service's end_task hook sends `endTask` before it closes
     the connection (client.mjs `release`), and the engine releases at once every process whose only remaining observer
     or last actor was that connection (an older engine answers methodNotFound, unread). (b) The engine's stop: the
     shutdown request, as before, and now a termination signal (`Lifecycle.beforeSignalExit`); SIGKILL leaves it on.
     (c) `enablementReleaseGraceMs`, 30 s, after the process's last activity, whether or not a connection is still open
     (a model that never calls end_task keeps its connection until the 15-minute idle stop, and EUI must not stay on all
     that time). Activity is an observation (its enablement step), an input to the process (every input method of a
     resolved app), and the end of either request, so the grace runs from the request's end; any connection's counts,
     and an input's connection joins the process's observers. Each activity arms a timer on the global queue with a new
     per-process token, so only the newest releases. A close without end_task releases nothing by itself, except a
     process with no timer armed (a release that failed: retried then). The grace's release takes the entry's lock as
     every release does: an observation in flight took the token under the class lock before its read and write, so
     either it came first (the timer is stale) or it waits on the entry's lock and reads after the reset, setting the
     attribute again, owned, since the write-back is remembered (`writtenBack`). A process released with a connection
     still observing it is kept, not retired. A grace of 0 is the previous immediate release at the close.
  4. **An input after a release** (the ruling 5c2f27a3). The model may act on a stale element 40 s later with no
     observation. In a Chromium-family process every input first takes the observation's enablement step
     (`inputEnablement`: the role read, then `enableReporting`), so the attribute is set again, owned, and the write
     moves `freshSince`: #282's press re-read and #283's box re-read apply, and neither answers noAction or
     noCharacterBoxes because of cua's own release. Elsewhere an input is activity only.
  5. **The re-read deadline** (the ruling 199d194c; `webAreaRereadDeadline`). Chrome's complete mode lands about 2.2 s
     after the EUI write, past a 2 s re-read that starts with the write, as an input's own enablement makes it start. So
     both re-reads run until the later of 2 s from their start and `freshSince` + 2.6 s (the 2.2 s and a margin),
     capped at 3 s from their start; with no write since, 2 s as before.
  6. **Limits.** EUI stays on while cua drives that Chrome: until release (end_task, engine stop, or 30 s after cua's
     last action on that Chrome); SIGKILL leaves it on. Each task after an end_task, and the first action after a grace
     release, pays Chrome's 2.2 s debounce again (#288's latency); the first click into a fresh Chrome can take up to
     about 3 s. No early write at first resolution is made (the design session dropped it). A request running longer
     than the grace (a 30 s settle) could be released under it; its end re-arms the grace.
  7. **The review of d2568c76..96c6f093** (four findings, all fixed). (i) The relay closes fully
     (src/repl-host/native-pipe.mjs: end, then destroy), and `Server.serve` stopped reading once an earlier answer's
     write failed, so an `endTask` buffered behind the hide could be lost: the client now unpublishes the connection,
     fails its waiting requests, sends the hide unawaited and `endTask`, and closes only on endTask's answer, an error
     or 1 s (`endTaskWaitMs`; end_task's hook is bounded at 2 s); and the server, after a failed write, still reads
     the frames already buffered and handles an `endTask` among them (`endTasksAfterFailedWrite`, each read bounded
     at 1 s), so a client that gave up waiting still ends its task. (ii) The stop raced live connections: `releaseAll`
     now enters a stopping state under the class lock before it takes the processes; from then on `enableReporting`
     writes nothing (checked again under the entry's lock, so an observation registered before the stop and waiting
     on that lock does not re-enable after the reset) and `noteActivity` records nothing, and every owned entry is
     reset whatever its observers; the shutdown request and a termination signal share it. (iii) A grace timer
     checked its token before the entry's lock: activity landing between that check and the reset found the attribute
     still owned and returned, then was reset underneath; the token is now checked again under the entry's lock, and a
     stale one resets nothing. (iv) #288's container rule failed open on unreadable children (item 2 of #288's entry).
  Proven offline (`EnablementOwnershipAndGraceTests`, 20: -25208 with a true readback owned and released, with a false
  one not owned, a value read true before never written, the write back's -25208 done and not retried; the release 30
  s after the last observation, not after the close; an open but idle connection released at 30 s and its next
  observation re-enabling, owned; a re-observation at 20 s cancelling the 30 s release; an input restarting the grace
  from itself; a click after a grace release re-enabling owned and fresh; end_task at once for the last actor only; the
  stop at once during a grace; a failed grace release retried at the close; 0 as the immediate release; an observation
  during the grace's reset re-enabling; the `endTask` request; and for the review, activity between the grace's first
  check and its reset making it stale, an observation during the stop's reset writing nothing, one registered before
  the stop not re-enabling after it, an endTask buffered behind a failed write still handled. `RereadDeadlineTests`, 3: the rule, the action re-read
  and the box re-read finding complete mode at 2.3 and 2.4 s after a write. The existing release tests at grace 0;
  the client's and the service's endTask order, and over a fully closing relay the close after endTask's answer and
  at its bound, in their JS suites.) Nothing live ran: the hosted check (EUI reads
  false after end_task and after 30 s idle, a checkbox loses AXPress, the window moves without animation) is the next
  step.
  Proven live (`docs/evidence/2026-11-06-eui-release/`; hosted run 42168334138 at 63926945 and 42167787338 at
  96c6f093, Chrome for Testing 156, 2 reps each, all alike). At end_task, `endTask` answered `{released: 1}` in 2 ms and EUI
  read false 12–15 ms later, with the checkbox's AXPress and the text boxes gone; a window move then settled in 2 frames
  (8–55 ms) where main's stayed animated (4–6 frames, 94–159 ms). SIGTERM released in 27 ms. An idle connection was
  released 31.7 s after its last observation, and a re-observation at 20 s moved the release to 32 s after it. The grace
  fires 1.5–2.1 s past the nominal 30 s, every rep: the utility-QoS timer's coalescing, likely. A click 1.6 s after a
  release, with no re-observation, re-enabled EUI and was element-routed (AXPress, the checkbox changed once) in
  2.4 s, inside the 3 s cap. Main never released.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235's implementer for the design session's rulings on #292.
- Decision (#288, 2026-11-05, `fix/288-fresh-chrome`; the design session's rulings; amends #282's items 3 and 4):
  **a left click by route 3 into a Chromium process where no web area has been seen establishes the page tree before
  its walk decides, and an element with an AXWebArea child is never outside.**
  1. **The measurement** (#288's hosted spike, `probe/288-hosted`, runs 42161269408 and 42162367095; Chrome for Testing
     156). Before any role read a page point hit-tests to a coarse AXScrollArea that walks to the AXWindow, which
     #282's walk took for outside (then, with no web area seen, a possible web area). After the application's role read
     (Basic mode) a search of the window finds the AXWebArea in 11 to 29 ms, but for 40 to 350 ms more the page point
     can answer Chrome's web-contents AXScrollArea, whose child is the AXWebArea, before it resolves inside the page.
     The toolbar's Reload resolves to an AXButton whose walk reaches the AXWindow with no web area.
  2. **The container rule** (`pointerView`, every pointer, seen or not): a hit, or any element on its walk below the
     stop, with an AXWebArea among its children is `aboveWebArea`, a possible web area, never outside. Children, or a
     child's role, that cannot be read are unknown, which the walk takes for incomplete, a possible web area too (the
     review of d2568c76..96c6f093: a failed read had counted as no web-area child, so with a web area seen a page point
     whose web-contents scroll area could not be read was taken to accept first mouse); the establishment keeps
     re-hitting an unknown hit as it does a container.
  3. **The establishment** (`establishedPointerView`, the coordinate click and the element click's synthesized path,
     route 3, left button, a Chromium process with `hasSeenWebArea` false): the application's AXRole read, the target
     window searched for an AXWebArea (as `containsWebArea`, bounded by 1,000 nodes; `noteWebArea` when found), and
     the point hit-tested again by the click's own hit-test; a hit that is such a container is hit-tested again every
     25 ms for up to 500 ms until it is something else, the waits ending by 600 ms after the step began. The walk then
     decides on the last hit, with the search's answer as `webAreaSeen`; the element route walks that hit. Nothing is
     activated or posted during it, and the intervention check before the activation is unchanged. Recorded as
     `pointerEstablish {ms, webAreaFound, rehits}` in the input meta (`cua/pointerEstablish`). A drag keeps the walk
     without the establishment.
  Proven offline (`PointerEstablishTests`, 8: the container rule, unreadable children as a possible web area, the toolbar button resolved outside, a container
  re-hit until the page control, the bound with a fast and a slow search, no web area found, no establishment where
  seen or outside Chromium, the record's wire; the service's pass-through). Nothing live ran: the first-click latency
  and the pre-observation toolbar limits stand until the hosted kit measures them.
  Proven live (the same evidence). A first contact with no observation cannot reach this step: a coordinate click
  needs the connection's screenshot (`noScreenshotForCoordinates`), and element tokens come only from get_app_state. So
  the state with no web area seen arises after a grace release, which clears it. There the step ran in 10–24 ms
  (`pointerEstablish {webAreaFound true, rehits 1}`): Chrome's Reload walked outside every web area and was posted by
  route 3, and page text was refused with nothing posted. The posted Reload did not reload the page, on main as on
  the branch: the click went to the observation's point, which Chrome for Testing's toolbar re-layout (about 18 s
  after launch) had moved Reload off (#294's entry below), not this step. The first quick
  click's latency, re-stated: 1.7–2.0 s for a checkbox press and 1.7–1.9 s for a textarea focus, 0.1 s after the first
  observation, on both engines.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, cursor235's implementer for the design session's rulings on #288.

- Decision (#294, 2026-11-06, `fix/294-toolbar-idle`; the design session's ruling, 1ea561bc ee29283e): **#294 is not
  lost input, and nothing in the engine changes for it.** #294 was registered from #288's live check: a route-3 click
  on Chrome's Reload answered success and did not reload once the window had sat 20 s or more. #294's hosted spike
  (`docs/evidence/2026-11-06-toolbar-relayout/`; runs 42173188779, 42175037968 and 42176012181; Chrome for Testing 156,
  two images) found the cause. About 18–20 s after launch, Chrome for Testing re-lays out its toolbar in the kit's
  620-pt window: the Sign In pill collapses and the disabled Forward button returns, so Reload moves from x 442 to
  478. A click at the first observation's Reload point then lands on Forward.
  - **The clicks were delivered, and the clock was Chrome's launch, not idle.** Delivered and missed clicks were
    identical in the engine's meta (fresh activation, `consumedByAction`), in the events a pid tap saw, and in Chrome's
    AppKit state. The boundary fell between 18.1 s and 18.7 s after launch in both reps.
  - **The isolating cells.** Route 3 at Reload's current centre at 25 s reloaded, and so did the element click, which
    re-reads the frame. A real HID click at the old point missed with no engine running. An observation made after
    the re-layout gave a click that reloaded.
  - **What is excluded:** a held activation (synthesized clicks take a fresh one), AppKit's first click into an
    inactive window (Chromium's `acceptsFirstMouse:` answers YES), and Chromium throttling (the page stays visible
    and unthrottled).
  - **The ruling.** Close #294 as not-a-bug; correct #288's evidence and this log; have the #288 kit's `noobs` cell
    locate Reload at click time. The general hazard, a coordinate click into a screenshot the app has since
    re-laid out, is #296: a stale-point guard that refuses in both modes, measured for false positives before it is
    built.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-06, cursor235 for the design session's ruling on #294.

- Amended by the #305 entry (2026-11-06, below): an interactive hit, or an element route's target, that is itself a
  recorded node the screenshot showed at the point, still there, is kept as `recordedHit` (items 5 and 2b).
  Decision (#296, 2026-11-06, `fix/296-stale-point`; the design session's rulings, 1ea561bc ee29283e and 0c0b70f0, on
  #294's spike and #296's false-positive table): **a coordinate click or drag is refused, with nothing posted, in both
  device modes, when what the screenshot its coordinates came from showed at the point is gone, has moved off it, or
  has given way to another interactive element.** A declared divergence: the vendor posts at the point blindly.
  1. **The record** (`ObservedScreenshot.nodes`; StalePoint.swift `screenshotNodes`). Every observation that returns a
     screenshot keeps, with it, each node of the window's walk: its handle, role, title, description and AX frame (global
     points, as clicks are posted), the part of that frame its clipping ancestors leave showing (`visibleFrame`'s rule
     over the recorded tree), and whether the walk recorded all its children (a frameless descendant's unrecorded children
     count against its nearest framed ancestor, since a frameless node is never chosen at a point), with the window's
     origin at the capture. Titles and descriptions are kept as the walk keeps them, cut at 10,000 UTF-16 units. An
     observation without a screenshot leaves the record, as it leaves the screenshot. The menu bar is not recorded: it is
     not in the window's image.
  2. **Where it applies.** Every coordinate pointer that presses at a screenshot point: `click {x, y}` with every button
     and count, on every route (3, 4, and the spike's 2 and 2′), and a drag's start point. Not a drag's end, where it
